\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}
is continuous in
\left(\gamma,\tau,\theta\right)
.

The proof of Proposition
4.2
relies on the following:

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
\varepsilon_{5}
be as in Proposition
4.1
. For every
\delta\in\left(0,1\right)
, there exist
\varepsilon_{5}^{\prime}\left(\delta\right)\in\left(0,\varepsilon_{5}\left(\delta\right)\right)
and
C_{5}^{\prime}=C_{5}^{\prime}\left(\delta\right)>0
such that for every
\varepsilon\in\left(0,\varepsilon_{5}^{\prime}\left(\delta\right)\right)
and
\left(\gamma,\tau,\theta\right)\in P^{k}_{\varepsilon}\left(\delta\right)
, the operator
L_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}:V_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}^{\perp}\to V_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}^{\perp}
defined by

L_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\left(\Phi\right)=\Pi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}^{\perp}\left(\Phi-\Delta^{-1}\left[\lambda_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon}f^{\prime}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right)\Phi\right]\right)\quad\forall\Phi\in V_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}^{\perp}

(4.17)

satisfies

\left\|\Phi\right\|_{H^{1}_{0}}\leq C^{\prime}_{5}\left\|L_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\left(\Phi\right)\right\|_{H^{1}_{0}}.

(4.18)

In particular,
L_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}
is an isomorphism.

Proposition
4.2
and Lemma
4.3
(together with Proposition
4.4
and Lemma
4.5
), are the heart of the Lyapunov–Schmidt procedure. We prove them by using a similar approach as in the case of higher dimensions (see for instance Deng–Musso–Wei
[
DengMusWei
]
and Robert–Vétois
[
RobVet1
,
RobVet2
]
). Aside from the usual differences in the computations due to the exponential term, the main difference here lies in the use of the Poincaré–Sobolev inequalities (
7.2
) and (
7.7
), which take advantage of the additional dimensions of the kernel
V_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}
given by the directions of the bubbles.

Proof of Lemma
4.3
.

We proceed by contradiction. We assume that there exist sequences
\left(\varepsilon_{n},\gamma_{n},\tau_{n},\theta_{n},\Phi_{n}\right)_{n\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}}
such that
\varepsilon_{n}\to 0
,
\left(\gamma_{n},\tau_{n},\theta_{n}\right)\in P^{k}_{\varepsilon}\left(\delta\right)
and

\Phi_{n}\in V_{\varepsilon_{n},\gamma_{n},\tau_{n}}^{\perp},\quad\left\|\Phi_{n}\right\|_{H^{1}_{0}}=1\quad\text{and}\quad\left\|L_{\varepsilon_{n},\gamma_{n},\tau_{n},\theta_{n}}\left(\Phi_{n}\right)\right\|_{H^{1}_{0}}=\smallo\left(1\right)

(4.19)

as
n\to\infty
. For simplicity of notations, we denote
\overline{\gamma}_{n}:=\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon_{n}}
,
r_{n}:=r_{\varepsilon_{n}}
,
d_{n}:=d_{\varepsilon_{n}}
,
\lambda_{n}:=\lambda_{\varepsilon_{n}}
,
h_{n}:=h_{\varepsilon_{n}}
,
w_{n}:=w_{\varepsilon_{n}}
,
\Psi_{n}:=\Psi_{\varepsilon_{n},\gamma_{n},\tau_{n}}
,
U_{n}:=U_{\varepsilon_{n},\gamma_{n},\tau_{n},\theta_{n}}
,
\overline{B}_{i,n}:=\overline{B}_{\varepsilon_{n},\gamma_{i,n},\overline{\tau_{i,n}}}
,
B_{i,n}:=B_{\varepsilon_{n},\gamma_{i,n},\tau_{i,n}}
,
L_{n}:=L_{\varepsilon_{n},\gamma_{n},\tau_{n},\theta_{n}}
,
V_{n}^{\perp}:=V_{\varepsilon_{n},\gamma_{n},\tau_{n}}^{\perp}
and
Z_{j,i,n}:=Z_{j,i,\varepsilon_{n},\gamma_{n},\tau_{n}}
for all
i\in\left\{1,\dotsc,k\right\}
and
j\in\left\{0,1\right\}
, where
\gamma_{n}:=\left(\gamma_{1,n},\dotsc,\gamma_{k,n}\right)
,
\tau_{n}:=\left(\tau_{1,n},\dotsc,\tau_{k,n}\right)
,
\overline{\tau_{i,n}}:=\left(\tau_{i,n},0\right)
and
\theta_{n}:=\left(\theta_{1,n},\dotsc,\theta_{k,n}\right)
. It follows from (
4.19
) that

\lambda_{n}\int_{\Omega}h_{n}f^{\prime}\left(U_{n}\right)\Phi_{n}^{2}dx=\left\|\Phi_{n}\right\|_{H^{1}_{0}}^{2}-\left<\Phi_{n},L_{n}\left(\Phi_{n}\right)\right>_{H^{1}_{0}}=1+\smallo\left(1\right)

(4.20)

as
n\to\infty
. On the other hand, since
f^{\prime}>0
,
\lambda_{n}\to\lambda_{0}
and
h_{n}\to h_{0}
in
C^{0}\left(\overline{\Omega}\right)
, we obtain

\lambda_{n}\int_{\Omega}h_{n}f^{\prime}\left(U_{n}\right)\Phi_{n}^{2}dx=\bigO\left(I_{n}\right),\quad\text{where }I_{n}:=\int_{\Omega}f^{\prime}\left(U_{n}\right)\Phi_{n}^{2}dx.

(4.21)

In what follows, we will prove that
I_{n}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
, thus contradicting (
4.20
) and (
4.21
).

Estimation of
I_{n}
in the balls
B\left(\overline{\tau_{i,n}},r_{n}\right)
.
For
i\in\left\{1,\dotsc,k\right\}
, by rescaling and using (
4.19
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i,n}},r_{n}\right)}f^{\prime}\left(U_{n}\right)\Phi_{n}^{2}dx=\mu_{i,n}^{2}\int_{B\left(0,r_{n}/\mu_{i,n}\right)}f^{\prime}\big(\gamma_{i,n}^{-1}\widehat{U}_{n}+\gamma_{i,n}\big)\widehat{\Phi}_{n}^{2}dx,

(4.22)

\displaystyle\int_{(\Omega-\overline{\tau_{i,n}})/\mu_{i,n}}\big<\nabla\widehat{\Phi}_{n},\nabla\psi\big>dx-\lambda_{n}\mu_{i,n}^{2}\int_{(\Omega-\overline{\tau_{i,n}})/\mu_{i,n}}\hat{h}_{n}f^{\prime}\big(\gamma_{i,n}^{-1}\widehat{U}_{n}+\gamma_{i,n}\big)\widehat{\Phi}_{n}\psi dx

\displaystyle\hskip 214.0pt=\smallo\left(\left\|\nabla\psi\right\|_{L^{2}}\right)\qquad\forall\psi\in C^{\infty}_{c}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}\right)

(4.23)

as
n\to\infty
, where
\mu_{i,n}
,
\hat{h}_{n}
,
\widehat{\Phi}_{n}
and
\widehat{U}_{n}
are defined by

\displaystyle\mu_{i,n}^{2}

\displaystyle:=4\gamma_{i,n}^{-2}\exp\left(-\gamma_{i,n}^{2}\right),\quad\hat{h}_{n}\left(x\right):=h_{n}\left(\overline{\tau_{i,n}}+\mu_{i,n}x\right),

\displaystyle\widehat{\Phi}_{n}\left(x\right)

\displaystyle:=\Phi_{n}\left(\overline{\tau_{i,n}}+\mu_{i,n}x\right)\quad\text{and}\quad\widehat{U}_{n}\left(x\right):=\gamma_{i,n}\left(U_{n}\left(\overline{\tau_{i,n}}+\mu_{i,n}x\right)-\gamma_{i,n}\right)

for all
x\in(\Omega-\overline{\tau_{i,n}})/n
. By using (
3.10
), (
3.34
) and (
6.2
), we obtain

\widehat{U}_{n}\left(x\right)\sim\gamma_{i,n}\left(\overline{B}_{i,n}\left(\overline{\tau_{i,n}}+\mu_{i,n}x\right)-\gamma_{i,n}\right)\sim\ln\frac{1}{1+\lambda_{n}h_{n}\left(\overline{\tau_{i,n}}\right)\left|x\right|^{2}}

(4.24)

as
n\to\infty
, uniformly in
x\in B\left(0,r_{n}/\mu_{i,n}\right)
. By using (
4.24
) together with the definition of
\mu_{i,n}
, we obtain

\mu_{i,n}^{2}f^{\prime}\big(\gamma_{i,n}^{-1}\widehat{U}_{n}+\gamma_{i,n}\big)\sim\frac{8}{\big(1+\lambda_{n}h_{n}\left(\overline{\tau_{i,n}}\right)\left|x\right|^{2}\big)^{2}}

(4.25)

as
n\to\infty
, uniformly in
x\in B\left(0,r_{n}/\mu_{i,n}\right)
. By remarking that

\big\|\nabla\widehat{\Phi}_{n}\big\|_{L^{2}}=\left\|\nabla\Phi_{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}=1

and using (
4.23
) and (
4.25
), we obtain that
\big(\widehat{\Phi}_{n}\big)_{n}
converges, up to a subsequence, weakly in
D^{1,2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}\right)
, strongly in
L^{p}_{\loc}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}\right)
for all
p\geq 1
and pointwise almost everywhere in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
to a solution
\widehat{\Phi}_{0}
of the equation

\Delta\widehat{\Phi}_{0}=\frac{8\lambda_{0}h_{0}\left(0\right)\widehat{\Phi}_{0}}{\big(1+\lambda_{0}h_{0}\left(0\right)\left|x\right|^{2}\big)^{2}}\qquad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2}.

(4.26)

Furthermore, since
\Phi_{n}\in\mathcal{H}
, we obtain that
\widehat{\Phi}_{0}
is even in
x_{2}
. By using a result of Baraket–Pacard
[
BarPac
]
, it follows that
\widehat{\Phi}_{0}\in\text{span}\left\{Z_{0},Z_{1}\right\}
,
where

Z_{0}\left(x\right):=\frac{1-\lambda_{0}h_{0}\left(0\right)\left|x\right|^{2}}{1+\lambda_{0}h_{0}\left(0\right)\left|x\right|^{2}}\quad\text{and}\quad Z_{1}\left(x\right):=\frac{2\lambda_{0}h_{0}\left(0\right)x_{1}}{1+\lambda_{0}h_{0}\left(0\right)\left|x\right|^{2}}\quad\forall x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\,.

In particular, note that the Poincaré–Sobolev inequality (
7.2
) applies to
\widehat{\Phi}_{0}
. On the other hand, for every
i\in\left\{1,\dotsc,k\right\}
, since
\Phi_{n}\in E_{\varepsilon_{n},\gamma_{n},\tau_{n}}^{\perp}
, we get
\left<B_{i,n},\Phi_{n}\right>_{H^{1}_{0}}=\left<Z_{0,i,n},\Phi_{n}\right>_{H^{1}_{0}}=\left<Z_{1,i,n},\Phi_{n}\right>_{H^{1}_{0}}=0
, which, by integrating by parts and using the equations satisfied by
B_{i,n}
,
Z_{0,i,n}
and
Z_{1,i,n}
, gives

\displaystyle\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i,n}},r_{n}\right)}f\left(\overline{B}_{i,n}\right)\Phi_{n}dx=0,

(4.27)

\displaystyle\lambda_{n}h_{n}\left(\overline{\tau_{i,n}}\right)

\displaystyle\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i,n}},r_{n}\right)}\hskip-2.0ptf^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{i,n}\right)\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\left[\overline{B}_{\varepsilon_{n},\gamma,\overline{\tau_{i,n}}}\right]_{\gamma=\gamma_{i,n}}\Phi_{n}dx+\left<\Phi_{n},\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\left[\Psi_{\varepsilon_{n},\gamma,\tau_{n},0}\right]_{\gamma=\gamma_{n}}\right>_{H^{1}_{0}}=0

(4.28)

together with an analogous estimate for the derivative in
\tau_{i}
. It follows from (
4.19
), (
4.27
) and the Poincaré–Sobolev inequality (
7.7
) that

\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i,n}},r_{n}\right)}f^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{i,n}\right)\Phi_{n}^{2}dx=\bigO\Big(\left\|\nabla\Phi_{n}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\Big)=\bigO\left(1\right).

(4.29)

On the other hand, by using Cauchy–Schwartz’ inequality together with (
3.14
), (
3.15
) and (
4.19
), we obtain

\left<\Phi_{n},\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\left[\Psi_{\varepsilon_{n},\gamma,\tau_{n},0}\right]_{\gamma=\gamma_{n}}\right>_{H^{1}_{0}}=\smallo\left(1\right)\text{ and }\left<\Phi_{n},\partial_{\tau_{i}}\left[\Psi_{\varepsilon_{n},\gamma_{n},\tau,0}\right]_{\tau=\tau_{n}}\right>_{H^{1}_{0}}=\smallo\left(1\right)

(4.30)

as
n\to\infty
. By rescaling, it follows from (
4.28
) and (
4.29
) that

\displaystyle\mu_{i,n}^{2}\int_{B\left(0,r_{n}/\mu_{i,n}\right)}f^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{i,n}\left(\overline{\tau_{i,n}}+\mu_{i,n}x\right)\right)\widehat{\Phi}_{n}\left(x\right)^{2}dx=\bigO\left(1\right),

(4.31)

\displaystyle\left<\Phi_{n},\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\left[\Psi_{\varepsilon_{n},\gamma,\tau_{n},0}\right]_{\gamma=\gamma_{n}}\right>_{H^{1}_{0}}=-\lambda_{n}h_{n}\left(\overline{\tau_{i,n}}\right)\mu_{i,n}^{2}\int_{B(0,r_{n}/\mu_{i,n})}f^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{i,n}\left(\overline{\tau_{i,n}}+\mu_{i,n}x\right)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 162.0pt\times\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\left[\overline{B}_{\varepsilon_{n},\gamma,\overline{\tau_{i,n}}}\left(\overline{\tau_{i,n}}+\mu_{i,n}x\right)\right]_{\gamma=\gamma_{n}}\widehat{\Phi}_{n}\left(x\right)dx.

(4.32)

Here again, we obtain an analogous estimate to (
4.32
) for the derivative in
\tau_{i}
.
By using (
6.2
) and (
6.3
) together with the definition of
\mu_{i,n}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\left[\overline{B}_{\varepsilon_{n},\gamma,\tau_{n}}\left(\overline{\tau_{i,n}}+\mu_{i,n}x\right)\right]_{\gamma=\gamma_{n}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow Z_{0}\left(x\right)\text{ for a.e. }x\in\mathbb{R}^{2},

(4.33)

\displaystyle\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\left[\overline{B}_{\varepsilon_{n},\gamma,\tau_{n}}\left(\overline{\tau_{i,n}}+\mu_{i,n}x\right)\right]_{\gamma=\gamma_{n}}

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(1\right),

(4.34)

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau_{i}}\left[\overline{B}_{\varepsilon_{n},\gamma_{i,n},\left(\tau,0\right)}\left(\overline{\tau_{i,n}}+\mu_{i,n}x\right)\right]_{\tau=\tau_{i,n}}

\displaystyle=\frac{Z_{1}\left(x\right)}{\mu_{i,n}\gamma_{i,n}}+\smallo\left(\frac{1}{\mu_{i,n}\gamma_{i,n}}\right)

(4.35)

as
n\to\infty
, uniformly in
x\in B\left(0,r_{n}/\mu_{i,n}\right)
. For every
R>0
, since
\big(\widehat{\Phi}_{n}\big)_{n}
converges strongly to
\widehat{\Phi}_{0}
in
L^{1}_{\loc}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}\right)
, it follows from (
4.24
), (
4.25
) and (
4.33
) that

\mu_{i,n}^{2}\int_{B\left(0,R\right)}f^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{i,n}\left(\overline{\tau_{i,n}}+\mu_{i,n}x\right)\right)\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\left[\overline{B}_{\varepsilon_{n},\gamma,\overline{\tau_{i,n}}}\left(\overline{\tau_{i,n}}+\mu_{i,n}x\right)\right]_{\gamma=\gamma_{n}}\widehat{\Phi}_{n}\left(x\right)dx\\
\longrightarrow 8\int_{B\left(0,R\right)}\frac{8Z_{0}\left(x\right)\widehat{\Phi}_{0}\left(x\right)dx}{\big(1+\lambda_{0}h_{0}\left(0\right)\left|x\right|^{2}\big)^{2}}

(4.36)

as
n\to\infty
. On the other hand, by using Hölder’s inequality together with (
4.24
), (
4.25
), (
4.31
), (
4.34
) and (
7.2
), we obtain

\displaystyle\Bigg|\mu_{i,n}^{2}\int_{A\left(0,R,r_{n}/\mu_{i,n}\right)}f^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{i,n}\left(\overline{\tau_{i,n}}+\mu_{i,n}x\right)\right)\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\left[\overline{B}_{\varepsilon_{n},\gamma,\overline{\tau_{i,n}}}\left(\overline{\tau_{i,n}}+\mu_{i,n}x\right)\right]_{\gamma=\gamma_{n}}\widehat{\Phi}_{n}\left(x\right)dx\Bigg|

\displaystyle\qquad=\bigO\left(\left(\mu_{i,n}^{2}\int_{A\left(0,R,r_{n}/\mu_{i,n}\right)}f^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{i,n}\left(\overline{\tau_{i,n}}+\mu_{i,n}x\right)\right)dx\right)^{1/2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=\bigO\left(\left(\int_{B\left(0,R\right)^{c}}\frac{dx}{\big(1+\lambda_{n}h_{n}\left(\overline{\tau_{i,n}}\right)\left|x\right|^{2}\big)^{2}}\right)^{1/2}\right)=\smallo_{R}\left(1\right),

(4.37)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{B\left(0,R\right)^{c}}\hskip-3.0pt\frac{Z_{0}\left(x\right)\widehat{\Phi}_{0}\left(x\right)dx}{\big(1+\lambda_{0}h_{0}\left(0\right)\left|x\right|^{2}\big)^{2}}\right|=\bigO\left(\left(\int_{B\left(0,R\right)^{c}}\hskip-3.0pt\frac{dx}{\big(1+\lambda_{0}h_{0}\left(0\right)\left|x\right|^{2}\big)^{2}}\right)^{1/2}\right)=\smallo_{R}\left(1\right),

(4.38)

where
\smallo_{R}\left(1\right)\to 0
as
R\to\infty
, uniformly in
n\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}
, where
A\left(0,R,r_{n}/\mu_{i,n}\right)
is as in (
3.18
). It follows from (
4.30
), (
4.32
) and (
4.36
)–(
4.38
) that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{Z_{0}\left(x\right)\widehat{\Phi}_{0}\left(x\right)dx}{\big(1+\lambda_{0}h_{0}\left(0\right)\left|x\right|^{2}\big)^{2}}=0.

(4.39)

By proceeding in the same way but using (
4.35
) instead of (
4.33
)–(
4.34
), we obtain

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{Z_{1}\left(x\right)\widehat{\Phi}_{0}\left(x\right)dx}{\big(1+\lambda_{0}h_{0}\left(0\right)\left|x\right|^{2}\big)^{2}}=0.

(4.40)

Since
\widehat{\Phi}_{0}\in\text{span}\left\{Z_{0},Z_{1}\right\}
, it follows from (
4.26
), (
4.39
) and (
4.40
) that
\widehat{\Phi}_{0}\equiv 0
. For every
R>0
, by using (
4.25
) and since
\big(\widehat{\Phi}_{n}\big)_{n}
converges strongly to
\widehat{\Phi}_{0}
in
L^{2}_{\loc}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}\right)
, we then obtain

\mu_{i,n}^{2}\int_{B\left(0,R\right)}f^{\prime}\big(\gamma_{i,n}^{-1}\widehat{U}_{n}+\gamma_{i,n}\big)\Phi_{n}^{2}dx=\smallo\left(1\right)

(4.41)

as
n\to\infty
. On the other hand, by proceeding as in (
4.37
), we obtain

\mu_{i,n}^{2}\int_{A\left(0,R,r_{n}/\mu_{i,n}\right)}f^{\prime}\big(\gamma_{i,n}^{-1}\widehat{U}_{n}+\gamma_{i,n}\big)\Phi_{n}^{2}dx=\smallo_{R}\left(1\right),

(4.42)

where
\smallo_{R}\left(1\right)\to 0
as
R\to\infty
, uniformly in
n\in\mathbb{N}^{\ast}
. It follows from (
4.22
), (
4.41
) and (
4.42
) that

\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i,n}},r_{n}\right)}f^{\prime}\left(U_{n}\right)\Phi_{n}^{2}dx=\smallo\left(1\right)

(4.43)

as
n\to\infty
.

Estimation of
I_{n}
in the annuli
A\left(\overline{\tau_{i,n}},r_{n},R_{n}\right)
, where
R_{n}:=\exp\left(-\overline{\gamma}_{n}\right)
.
For every
i\in\left\{1,\dotsc,k\right\}
, for small
p>1
, by using Hölder’s inequality, (
3.16
) and (
4.19
) together with the continuity of the embedding
H^{1}_{0}\left(\Omega\right)\hookrightarrow L^{2p^{\prime}}\left(\Omega\right)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{A\left(\overline{\tau_{i,n}},r_{n},R_{n}\right)}f^{\prime}\left(U_{n}\right)\Phi_{n}^{2}dx

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\left\|f^{\prime}\left(U_{n}\right)\mathbf{1}_{A\left(\overline{\tau_{i,n}},r_{n},R_{n}\right)}\right\|_{L^{p}}\left\|\Phi_{n}\right\|^{2}_{H^{1}_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\left\|f^{\prime}\left(U_{n}\right)\mathbf{1}_{A\left(\overline{\tau_{i,n}},r_{n},R_{n}\right)}\right\|_{L^{p}}\right)=\smallo\left(1\right)

(4.44)

as
n\to\infty
.

Estimation of
I_{n}
in
\Omega_{R_{n},\tau_{n}}
.
By using (
4.19
), we obtain that
\left(\Phi_{n}\right)_{n}
converges, up to a subsequence, weakly in
H^{1}_{0}\left(\Omega\right)
and pointwise almost everywhere in
\Omega
to a function
\Phi_{0}
. Furthermore, (
4.19
) gives that

\int_{\Omega}\left<\nabla\Phi_{n},\nabla\psi\right>dx-\lambda_{n}\int_{\Omega}h_{n}f^{\prime}\left(U_{n}\right)\Phi_{n}\psi dx=\smallo\left(1\right)

(4.45)

as
n\to\infty
, for all
\psi\in C^{\infty}_{c}\left(\Omega\right)
. By rescaling as in (
4.22
) and using (
4.25
) together with the fact that
\widehat{\Phi}_{n}\rightharpoonup 0
in
D^{1,2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}\right)
, we obtain that

\sum_{i=1}^{k}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i,n}},r_{n}\right)}h_{n}f^{\prime}\left(U_{n}\right)\Phi_{n}\psi dx=\smallo\left(1\right)

(4.46)

as
n\to\infty
. By using similar estimates as in (
4
), we obtain

\sum_{i=1}^{k}\int_{A\left(\overline{\tau_{i,n}},r_{n},R_{n}\right)}h_{n}f^{\prime}\left(U_{n}\right)\Phi_{n}\psi dx=\smallo\left(1\right)

(4.47)

as
n\to\infty
. By using (
3.10
), (
3.14
) and since
w_{n}\to w_{0}
in
C^{0}\left(\overline{\Omega}\right)
, we obtain that
U_{n}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{R_{n},\tau_{n}}}
is uniformly bounded and converges pointwise to
u_{0}
in
\Omega
. Since moreover
\Phi_{n}\rightharpoonup\Phi_{0}
in
H^{1}_{0}\left(\Omega\right)
,
\lambda_{n}\to\lambda_{0}
and
h_{n}\to h_{0}
in
C^{0}\left(\overline{\Omega}\right)
, it follows from (
4.45
)–(
4.47
) that
\Phi_{0}
is a solution of the equation

\Delta\Phi_{0}=\lambda_{0}h_{0}f^{\prime}\left(w_{0}\right)\Phi_{0}\qquad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{n}.

Since
w_{0}
is non-degenerate, we then obtain that
\Phi_{0}\equiv 0
. It then follows from standard integration theory that

\int_{\Omega_{R_{n},\tau_{n}}}f^{\prime}\left(U_{n}\right)\Phi_{n}^{2}dx=\smallo\left(1\right)

(4.48)

as
n\to\infty
.

Finally, by combining (
4.43
), (
4
) and (
4.48
), we obtain a contradiction with (
4.20
) and (
4.21
). This ends the proof of Lemma
4.3
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
4.2
.

We let
N_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}:V_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}^{\perp}\to V_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}^{\perp}
and
T_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}:V_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}^{\perp}\to V_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}^{\perp}
be the operators defined as

\displaystyle N_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\left(\Phi\right)

\displaystyle:=\Pi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}^{\perp}\big(\Delta^{-1}[\lambda_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon}(f\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}+\Phi\right)-f\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right)-f^{\prime}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right)\Phi)]\big),

\displaystyle T_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\left(\Phi\right)

\displaystyle:=L^{-1}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\left(N_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\left(\Phi\right)-\Pi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}^{\perp}\left(R_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right)\right)

for all
\Phi\in V_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}^{\perp}
, where
R_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}
and
L_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}
are as in (
4.1
) and (
4.17
). Remark that the equation (
4.15
) can be rewritten as the fixed point equation
T_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\left(\Phi\right)=\Phi
. For every
C>0
,
\varepsilon\in\left(0,\varepsilon_{5}^{\prime}\right)
and
\left(\gamma,\tau,\theta\right)\in P^{k}_{\varepsilon}\left(\delta\right)
, we define

V_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\left(C\right):=\left\{\Phi\in V_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}^{\perp}:\,\left\|\Phi\right\|_{H^{1}_{0}}\leq C\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right\}.

We will prove that if
C
is chosen large enough, then
T_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}
has a fixed point in
V_{\gamma,\tau,\theta}\left(C\right)
. By using (
4.18
), we obtain

\left\|T_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\left(\Phi\right)\right\|_{H^{1}_{0}}\leq C_{5}^{\prime}\big(\left\|N_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\left(\Phi\right)\right\|_{H^{1}_{0}}+\left\|R_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right\|_{H^{1}_{0}}\big).

(4.49)

For every
\Phi_{1},\Phi_{2}\in V_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\left(C\right)
and
\psi\in V_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}^{\perp}
, by integrating by parts and applying the mean value theorem, we obtain

\displaystyle\left<N_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\left(\Phi_{1}\right)-N_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\left(\Phi_{2}\right),\psi\right>_{H^{1}_{0}}

\displaystyle\qquad=\lambda_{\varepsilon}\int_{\Omega}h_{\varepsilon}\left(f^{\prime}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}+t\Phi_{1}+\left(1-t\right)\Phi_{2}\right)-f^{\prime}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right)\right)\left(\Phi_{1}-\Phi_{2}\right)\psi dx

\displaystyle\qquad=\lambda_{\varepsilon}\int_{\Omega}h_{\varepsilon}f^{\prime\prime}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}+st\Phi_{1}+s\left(1-t\right)\Phi_{2}\right)\left(t\Phi_{1}+\left(1-t\right)\Phi_{2}\right)\left(\Phi_{1}-\Phi_{2}\right)\psi dx

(4.50)

for some functions
s,t:\Omega\to\left[0,1\right]
. Since
\lambda_{\varepsilon}\to\lambda_{0}
,
h_{\varepsilon}\to h_{0}
in
C^{0}\left(\overline{\Omega}\right)
and
f^{\prime\prime}
is increasing, it follows from (
4
) that

\left<N_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\left(\Phi_{1}\right)-N_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\left(\Phi_{2}\right),\psi\right>_{H^{1}_{0}}\\
=\bigO\bigg(\int_{\Omega}f^{\prime\prime}\left(\left|U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right|+\left|\Phi_{1}\right|+\left|\Phi_{2}\right|\right)\left(\left|\Phi_{1}\right|+\left|\Phi_{2}\right|\right)\left|\Phi_{1}-\Phi_{2}\right|\left|\psi\right|dx\bigg).

(4.51)

For every
p>1
, by using Hölder’s inequality together with the continuity of the embedding
H^{1}_{0}\left(\Omega\right)\hookrightarrow L^{3p^{\prime}}\left(\Omega\right)
, we obtain

\int_{\Omega}f^{\prime\prime}\left(\left|U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right|+\left|\Phi_{1}\right|+\left|\Phi_{2}\right|\right)\left(\left|\Phi_{1}\right|+\left|\Phi_{2}\right|\right)\left|\Phi_{1}-\Phi_{2}\right|\left|\psi\right|dx\\
=\bigO\Big(\left\|f^{\prime\prime}\left(\left|U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right|+\left|\Phi_{1}\right|+\left|\Phi_{2}\right|\right)\right\|_{L^{p}}\left\|\left|\Phi_{1}\right|+\left|\Phi_{2}\right|\right\|_{H^{1}_{0}}\left\|\Phi_{1}-\Phi_{2}\right\|_{H^{1}_{0}}\left\|\psi\right\|_{H^{1}_{0}}\Big).

(4.52)

Since
f^{\prime\prime}
is increasing, we obtain

f^{\prime\prime}\left(\left|U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right|+\left|\Phi_{1}\right|+\left|\Phi_{2}\right|\right)\leq f^{\prime\prime}\big(\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\big)+f^{\prime\prime}\big(\widetilde{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}\big),

(4.53)

where

\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}:=\left(1+\delta_{\varepsilon}\right)\left|U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right|\quad\text{and}\quad\widetilde{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}:=\left(1+\delta_{\varepsilon}^{-1}\right)\left(\left|\Phi_{1}\right|+\left|\Phi_{2}\right|\right).

Remark that
\widetilde{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}\to 0
in
H^{1}_{0}\left(\Omega\right)
as
\varepsilon\to 0
since
\Phi_{1},\Phi_{2}\in V_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\left(C\right)
. By using Hölder’s inequality together with the Moser–Trudinger’s inequality and the continuity of the embedding
H^{1}_{0}\left(\Omega\right)\hookrightarrow L^{6p}\left(\Omega\right)
, we then obtain

\displaystyle\big\|f^{\prime\prime}\big(\widetilde{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}\big)\big\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle=2\big\|\widetilde{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}\big(3+2\widetilde{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}^{2}\big)\exp\big(\widetilde{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}^{2}\big)\big\|_{L^{2p}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\big\|\widetilde{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}\big\|_{H^{1}_{0}}\big(3+2\big\|\widetilde{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}\big\|_{H^{1}_{0}}^{2}\big)\big\|\exp\big(\widetilde{\Phi}_{\varepsilon}^{2}\big)\big\|_{L^{2p}}=\smallo\left(1\right)

(4.54)

as
\varepsilon\to 0
. For every
i\in\left\{1,\dotsc,k\right\}
, by remarking that
f^{\prime\prime}\left(s\right)\leq 6sf^{\prime}\left(s\right)
for all
s\geq 0
and using similar estimates as in (
4.24
) and (
4.25
), we obtain

f^{\prime\prime}\big(\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\left(x\right)\big)=\bigO\left(\frac{\overline{\gamma}_{i}\left(\tau\right)\overline{\mu}_{i}\left(\tau\right)^{2}}{\left(\overline{\mu}_{i}\left(\tau\right)^{2}+\left|x-\overline{\tau_{i}}\right|^{2}\right)^{2}}\right)

(4.55)

uniformly in
x\in B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)
, where
\overline{\mu}_{i}\left(\tau\right)
is defined by

\overline{\mu}_{i}\left(\tau\right)^{2}:=4\overline{\gamma}_{i}\left(\tau\right)^{-2}\exp\big(-\overline{\gamma}_{i}\left(\tau\right)^{2}\big).

It follows from (
4.55
) that

\big\|f^{\prime\prime}\big(\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\big)\mathbf{1}_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\big\|_{L^{p}}=\bigO\left(\overline{\gamma}_{i}\left(\tau\right)\overline{\mu}_{i}\left(\tau\right)^{-2/p^{\prime}}\right),

(4.56)

where
p^{\prime}
is the conjugate exponent of
p
. By using (
3.16
), we obtain

\big\|f^{\prime\prime}\big(\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\big)\mathbf{1}_{A\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon},R_{\varepsilon}\right)}\big\|_{L^{p}}=\smallo\left(1\right)

(4.57)

as
\varepsilon\to 0
, where
R_{\varepsilon}:=\exp\left(-\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\right)
, provided we choose
p
such that
p<1/\delta_{0}
. Furthermore, since
U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}
is uniformly bounded in
\Omega_{R_{\varepsilon},\tau}
, we obtain

\big\|f^{\prime\prime}\big(\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\big)\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{R_{\varepsilon},\tau}}\big\|_{L^{p}}=\bigO\left(1\right).

(4.58)

By putting together (
4.51
)–(
4.53
), (
4
) and (
4.24
) and (
4.58
), we obtain

\left\|N_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\left(\Phi_{1}\right)-N_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\left(\Phi_{2}\right)\right\|_{H^{1}_{0}}=\bigO\left(\overline{\gamma}_{i}\left(\tau\right)\overline{\mu}_{i}\left(\tau\right)^{-2/p^{\prime}}\left\|\left|\Phi_{1}\right|+\left|\Phi_{2}\right|\right\|_{H^{1}_{0}}\left\|\Phi_{1}-\Phi_{2}\right\|_{H^{1}_{0}}\right).

(4.59)

Remark that since
\Phi_{1},\Phi_{2}\in V_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\left(C\right)
, we obtain

\overline{\gamma}_{i}\left(\tau\right)\overline{\mu}_{i}\left(\tau\right)^{-2/p^{\prime}}\left\|\left|\Phi_{1}\right|+\left|\Phi_{2}\right|\right\|_{H^{1}_{0}}=\smallo\left(1\right)

(4.60)

as
\varepsilon\to 0
, provided we choose
p
such that
2/p^{\prime}<\delta_{1}+1/2
, i.e.
p<4/\left(3-2\delta_{1}\right)
. It follows from (
4.59
) and (
4.60
) that

\left\|N_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\left(\Phi_{1}\right)-N_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\left(\Phi_{2}\right)\right\|_{H^{1}_{0}}=\smallo\big(\left\|\Phi_{1}-\Phi_{2}\right\|_{H^{1}_{0}}\big)

(4.61)

as
\varepsilon\to 0
. By using (
4.3
), (
4.49
), (
4.61
) and since
N_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\left(0\right)=0
, we obtain that there exist
\varepsilon_{6}\left(\delta\right)\in\left(0,\varepsilon_{5}\left(\delta\right)\right)
and
C_{6}=C_{6}\left(\delta\right)>0
such that for every
\varepsilon\in\left(0,\varepsilon_{6}\left(\delta\right)\right)
and
\left(\gamma,\tau,\theta\right)\in P^{k}_{\varepsilon}\left(\delta\right)
,
T_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}
is a contraction mapping on
V_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\left(C_{6}\right)
. We can then apply the fixed point theorem, which gives that there exists a unique solution
\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\in V_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\left(C_{6}\right)
to the equation (
4.15
). The continuity of
\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}
in
\left(\gamma,\tau,\theta\right)
follows from the continuity of
U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}
,
Z_{0,i,\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}
and
Z_{1,i,\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}
in
\left(\gamma,\tau,\theta\right)
. This ends the proof of Proposition
4.2
.
∎

As a last step, we prove the following:

Proposition 4.4
.

Let
\varepsilon_{6}
and
\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}
be as in Proposition
4.2
. Then there exists
\delta_{7}\in\left(0,1\right)
such that for every
\delta\in\left(0,\delta_{7}\right)
, there exists
\varepsilon_{7}\left(\delta\right)\in\left(0,\varepsilon_{7}\left(\delta\right)\right)
such that for every
\varepsilon\in\left(0,\varepsilon_{7}\left(\delta\right)\right)
, there exists
(\gamma_{\varepsilon},\tau_{\varepsilon},\theta_{\varepsilon})\in P^{k}_{\varepsilon}\left(\delta\right)
such that

U_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{\varepsilon},\tau_{\varepsilon},\theta_{\varepsilon}}+\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{\varepsilon},\tau_{\varepsilon},\theta_{\varepsilon}}=\Delta^{-1}\left[\lambda_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon}f\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{\varepsilon},\tau_{\varepsilon},\theta_{\varepsilon}}+\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{\varepsilon},\tau_{\varepsilon},\theta_{\varepsilon}}\right)\right].

(4.62)

The proof of Proposition
4.4
relies on the following:

Lemma 4.5
.

Set

\widetilde{R}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}:=U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}+\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}-\Delta^{-1}\left[\lambda_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon}f\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}+\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right)\right].

Then for every
i\in\left\{1,\dotsc,k\right\}
and
\delta\in\left(0,1\right)
, we have

\displaystyle\left<\widetilde{R}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta},Z_{0,i,\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right>_{H^{1}_{0}}

\displaystyle=-8\pi\sum_{j=1}^{k}\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\big[E^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big]\left(E^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}+\theta_{j}\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\right)+\frac{4\pi}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}E^{\left(i\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}+\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{5}}\right),

(4.63)

\displaystyle\left<\widetilde{R}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta},B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}\right>_{H^{1}_{0}}

\displaystyle=-8\pi\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\left(E^{\left(i\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}+\theta_{i}\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\right)+\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right),

(4.64)

\displaystyle\left<\widetilde{R}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta},Z_{1,i,\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right>_{H^{1}_{0}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{4\pi}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}\Bigg(a_{0}l\tau_{i}^{l-1}-\frac{2}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{1}{\tau_{i}-\tau_{j}}\Bigg)+\smallo\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}d_{\varepsilon}}\right)

(4.65)

as
\varepsilon\to 0
, uniformly in
\left(\gamma,\tau,\theta\right)\in P^{k}_{\varepsilon}\left(\delta\right)
.

Remark 4.6
.

As an evidence of the
strong
interaction generated by the Moser-Trudinger critical nonlinearity, we stress that the variables
\theta
and
\gamma
are intricately coupled in the expansions (
4.63
)–(
4.65
) used to determine
(\gamma_{\varepsilon},\theta_{\varepsilon},\tau_{\varepsilon})
. This is not the case for 2-dimensional Liouville-type equations (see for instance
[
ChenLin-Liouville
]
), for which it is possible to construct blowing-up solutions without introducing neither the parameter
\theta
nor the bubbles
B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}
in
V_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}
(see for instance
[
EGP
]
working also in the
H^{1}_{0}(\Omega)
-framework). Finally, even not facing a situation with clustering or nonzero weak limit like ours, it is delicate to get a clean energy expansion in the Moser-Trudinger critical case (see
[
delPinoMusRuf2
]
). In particular, this expansion has to eventually fit with the cancellation pointed out by
[
ManMar
]
for the blow-up solutions.

Proof of (
4.63
).

We start with computations that will be used also in the proofs of (
4.64
)-(
4.64
). Given
Z\in H^{1}_{0}(\Omega)
, integration by parts yields

\left<\widetilde{R}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta},Z\right>_{H^{1}_{0}}=\int_{\Omega}[\Delta(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}+\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta})-f_{\varepsilon}(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}+\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta})]Zdx,

where we use the notation
f_{\varepsilon}=\lambda_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon}f
.
1
1

1

We shall always write
f_{\varepsilon}(U)
instead of
f_{\varepsilon}(x,U)
, ignoring the dependence on
x
.

We now expand for real numbers
U
and
R
,

\exp[(U+R)^{2}]=\exp(U^{2})\exp(2UR+R^{2})=\exp(U^{2})[1+2UR+\bigO(U^{2}R^{2})]

(4.66)

uniformly for
\left|UR\right|\leq 1
and
\left|R\right|\leq 1\leq\left|U\right|
, so that, recalling that
f^{\prime}\left(t\right)=\left(1+2t^{2}\right)\exp\left(t^{2}\right)
,

f\left(U+R\right)=f\left(U\right)+f^{\prime}\left(U\right)R+\bigO\left(U^{3}R^{2}\exp\left(U^{2}\right)\right),

(4.67)

and similarly for
f_{\varepsilon}
since
\lambda_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon}=\bigO\left(1\right)
. We apply this to

U=U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}=u_{\varepsilon}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}+\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau},\quad R=\widetilde{\Phi}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}:=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\theta_{i}B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}+\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}

(4.68)

to obtain

f_{\varepsilon}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}+\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right)=f_{\varepsilon}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right)+f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right)\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}\theta_{i}B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}+\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right)\\
+\bigO\left(\exp\left(U^{2}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right)U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}^{3}\widetilde{\Phi}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}^{2}\right).

Recalling Proposition
3.2
, and in particular that
U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}
is an exact solution outside the balls
B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}+r_{\varepsilon}^{2}\right)
, we get

\displaystyle\left<\widetilde{R}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta},Z\right>_{H^{1}_{0}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{k}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\left[\Delta U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}-f_{\varepsilon}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right)\right]Zdx

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j=1}^{k}\int_{\Omega^{j}_{\varepsilon}}\left[\Delta U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}-f_{\varepsilon}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right)\right]Zdx

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j=1}^{k}\theta_{j}\int_{\Omega}\left[\Delta B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\tau_{j}}-f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right)B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\tau_{j}}\right]Zdx

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\Omega}\left[\Delta\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}-f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right)\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right]Zdx

\displaystyle\quad+\bigO\left(\int_{\Omega}\left|U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right|^{3}\exp\big(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}^{2}\big)\widetilde{\Phi}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}^{2}\left|Z\right|dx\right)

\displaystyle=:\sum_{j=1}^{k}\left[(A)_{j}+(A^{\prime})_{j}+(B)_{j}\right]+(C)+(D),

(4.69)

where
\Omega^{j}_{\varepsilon}:=B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}+r_{\varepsilon}^{2}\right)\backslash B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)
. We now set
Z=Z_{0,i,\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}
in (
4
) and estimate the various terms.

In order to evaluate
(A):=\sum_{j=1}^{k}(A)_{j}
, expand as in (
3.22
)

U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}=\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}+E^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}+F^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau},\quad\text{in }B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right).

(4.70)

Using Proposition
3.5
and omitting some indices, we get

R_{j}\left(x\right):=E^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}+F^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\left(x\right)=E^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}+\bigO\left(\frac{\left|x-\overline{\tau_{j}}\right|}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}d_{\varepsilon}}\right)=:R_{j}^{s}\left(x\right)+R_{j}^{r},

(4.71)

for all
x\in B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)
, where the letters
s
and
r
stand for “symmetric” and “remainder”, respectively. Using (
4.70
) and (
3.33
), we get

\displaystyle Z_{0,i}:=Z_{0,i,\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}

\displaystyle=\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\big[\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}+R_{j}\big]

\displaystyle=\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\big[\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}\left(x\right)+E^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big]+\bigO\left(\left|x-\overline{\tau_{j}}\right|\right),\quad\text{in }B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right),

(4.72)

where we also replaced
\bigO\left(\left|x-\overline{\tau_{j}}\right|/\left(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}d_{\varepsilon}\right)\right)
by
\bigO\left(\left|x-\overline{\tau_{j}}\right|\right)
for simplicity.
Using Proposition
3.2
and (
3.20
), i.e.
\Delta{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\tau_{j}}=\Delta\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}
, in
B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)
,
we can write

(A)_{j}=\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\left[\Delta\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}-f_{\varepsilon}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right)\right]Z_{0,i}dx.

We now Taylor expand as in (
4.67
) with

U=\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}},\quad R=R_{j}=E^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}+F^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau},

and since
\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}
is an exact solution in
B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)
, we estimate

(A)_{j}=\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\underbrace{\left[\Delta\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}-\lambda_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{\tau_{j}}\right)f\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}\right)\right]}_{=0}Z_{0,i}dx\\
-\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\lambda_{\varepsilon}\left(h_{\varepsilon}-h_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{\tau_{j}}\right)\right)f\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}\right)Z_{0,i}dx-\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\lambda_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon}f^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}\right)R_{j}Z_{0,i}dx\\
+\bigO\left(\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{3}\exp\big(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}^{2}\big)R_{j}^{2}\left|Z_{0,i}\right|dx\right).

(4.73)

Observing that
h_{\varepsilon}-h_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{\tau_{j}}\right)=\bigO\left(\left|x-\overline{\tau_{j}}\right|\right)
, using (
4.71
) to bound
F^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}
, writing

f^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}\right)=\bigO\left(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}f\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}\right)\right)=\bigO\big(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}\exp\big(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}^{2}\big)\big),\quad\text{in }B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right),

and using
\left|Z_{0,i}\right|=\bigO\left(1\right)
, we simplify to

\displaystyle(A)_{j}

\displaystyle=-\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\lambda_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{\tau_{j}}\right)f^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}\right)E^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\big[\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}+E^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big]dx

\displaystyle\quad+\bigO\left(\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{3}\exp\big(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}^{2}\big)\left(|E^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}|^{2}+\left|x-\overline{\tau_{j}}\right|\right)dx\right).

(4.74)

Now write

\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\big[\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}+E^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big]=\delta_{ij}\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\left[\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}\right]+\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\big[E^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big],\quad\text{on }B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right),\\

where
\delta_{ij}
is the Kronecker symbol. Observing that

\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}\left(x\right)=\overline{B}_{\gamma_{j}}\big(\sqrt{\lambda_{\varepsilon,j}}\left(x-\overline{\tau_{j}}\right)\big),\quad\text{where }\lambda_{\varepsilon,j}:=\lambda_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{\tau_{j}}\right),

(4.75)

with the change of variables
\sqrt{\lambda_{\varepsilon,j}}\left(x-\overline{\tau_{j}}\right)=y
and Proposition
6.3
, we get

\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\lambda_{\varepsilon,j}f^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}\right)dx=\int_{B(0,\sqrt{\lambda_{\varepsilon,j}}r_{\varepsilon})}f^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{\gamma_{j}}\right)dy=8\pi+\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right),

(4.76)

where
\overline{B}_{\gamma_{j}}
is as in Proposition
6.1
. With the same change of variables and Proposition
6.3
, we also get

\displaystyle\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\lambda_{\varepsilon,i}f^{\prime}\big(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}\big)\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\left[\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}\right]dx

\displaystyle=\int_{B(0,\sqrt{\lambda_{\varepsilon,i}}r_{\varepsilon})}f^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{\gamma_{i}}\right)Z_{0,\gamma_{i}}dy=-\frac{4\pi+\smallo\left(1\right)}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}},

where
Z_{0,\gamma_{i}}
is as in Proposition
6.2
. Now, using Proposition
3.4
, the dominant term in
(A)_{j}
becomes

\displaystyle-E^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\lambda_{\varepsilon,j}f^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}\right)\left(\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\big[E^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big]+\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\left[\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}\right]\right)dy

\displaystyle\qquad=-E^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\left(\left(8\pi+\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right)\right)\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\big[E^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big]-\delta_{ij}\frac{4\pi+\smallo\left(1\right)}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=-E^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\left(8\pi\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\big[E^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big]-\delta_{ij}\frac{4\pi}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right)+\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{5}}\right)

Concerning the remainder term in (
4
), again using Proposition
6.3
, we have

\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\exp\big(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}^{2}\big)\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{3}\delta_{\varepsilon}^{2}dx=\bigO\left(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\delta_{\varepsilon}^{2}\right)=\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{5}}\right),

and, with the usual change of variables and Proposition
6.4
, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\exp\big(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}^{2}\big)\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{3}\left|x-\overline{\tau_{j}}\right|dx=\bigO\left(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{3}\mu_{\gamma_{j}}^{3\delta_{0}-2\delta_{0}^{2}+\smallo\left(1\right)}\right)

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\mu_{\varepsilon}^{3\delta_{0}-2\delta_{0}^{2}+\smallo\left(1\right)}\right)

\displaystyle=\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{5}}\right),

where in the last identity, we used that
\delta_{\varepsilon}=\overline{\mu}_{\varepsilon}^{\delta_{1}+1/2}
and
3\delta_{0}-2\delta_{0}^{2}>\delta_{1}+\frac{1}{2}
thanks to (
4.2
), so that

\mu_{\varepsilon}^{3\delta_{0}-2\delta_{0}^{2}+\smallo\left(1\right)}=\bigO\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{a}}\right),\quad\text{for any }a\in\mathbb{R}.

We therefore get

(A)_{j}=-E^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\left(8\pi\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\big[E^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big]-\delta_{ij}\frac{4\pi}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right)+\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{5}}\right)

Summing over
j
, we then obtain

(A)=\sum_{j=1}^{k}(A)_{j}=-8\pi\sum_{j=1}^{k}E^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\big[E^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big]+\frac{4\pi}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}E^{\left(i\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}+\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{5}}\right).

(4.77)

As for the error term in the annuli, we have from Proposition
3.2
,

(A^{\prime})_{j}=\int_{\Omega^{j}_{\varepsilon}}\left(\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}-1\right)f_{\varepsilon}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right)Z_{0,i,\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}dx,

hence, from (
3.21
),

(A^{\prime})=\sum_{j=1}^{k}\bigO\left(|\Omega^{j}_{\varepsilon}|\overline{\mu}_{\varepsilon}^{-2\delta_{0}^{2}+\smallo\left(1\right)}\right)=\bigO\left(\overline{\mu}_{\varepsilon}^{3\delta_{0}-2\delta_{0}^{2}+\smallo\left(1\right)}\right)=\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{5}}\right),

(4.78)

where in the last line, we used that
\delta_{\varepsilon}=\overline{\mu}_{\varepsilon}^{\delta_{1}+1/2}
and
3\delta_{0}-2\delta_{0}^{2}>\delta_{1}+1/2
.

We now move on to the estimate of
(B)
. Integration by parts and using that
U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}
is an exact solution outside the balls
B\left(\overline{\tau_{m}},r_{\varepsilon}+r_{\varepsilon}^{2}\right)
give

\displaystyle(B)_{j}

\displaystyle=\theta_{j}\int_{\Omega}\left[\Delta Z_{0,i}-f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\theta}\right)Z_{0,i}\right]B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\tau_{j}}dx

\displaystyle=\theta_{j}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\left[\Delta U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}-f_{\varepsilon}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\theta}\right)\right]B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\tau_{j}}dx

\displaystyle=\theta_{j}\sum_{m=1}^{k}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{m}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\left[\Delta U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}-f_{\varepsilon}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\theta}\right)\right]B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\tau_{j}}dx

\displaystyle\quad+\theta_{j}\sum_{m=1}^{k}\int_{\Omega^{m}_{\varepsilon}}\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\left[\Delta U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}-f_{\varepsilon}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\theta}\right)\right]B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\tau_{j}}dx

\displaystyle=:\sum_{m=1}^{k}\left[(B)_{jm}+(B^{\prime})_{jm}\right].

(4.79)

Using the same notations as in (
4.70
)–(
4.71
) and using (
4.66
), which gives

f^{\prime}\left(B+R\right)=f^{\prime}\left(B\right)+\bigO\left(B^{3}\left|R\right|\exp\left(B^{2}\right)\right)

(4.80)

with

B=\overline{B}_{m}=\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{m},\tau_{m}},\quad R=R_{m}=E^{\left(m\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}+F^{\left(m\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau},

on
B\left(\overline{\tau_{m}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)
, we can now write

\displaystyle\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\left[\Delta U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}-f_{\varepsilon}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right)\right]=\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\left[\Delta\overline{B}_{m}\right]-f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right)\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\left[U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad=\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\left[\Delta\overline{B}_{m}\right]-\big[f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{m}\right)+\bigO\big(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{3}\exp\big(\overline{B}_{m}^{2}\big)\left|R_{m}\right|\big)\big]\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\left[\overline{B}_{m}+R_{m}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad=\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\underbrace{\left[\Delta\overline{B}_{m}-\lambda_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{\tau_{m}}\right)f\left(\overline{B}_{m}\right)\right]}_{=0}+\bigO\big(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{3}\exp\big(\overline{B}_{m}^{2}\big)\left(\left|x-\overline{\tau_{m}}\right|+\left|R_{m}\right|\right)\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\left[B_{m}\right]\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 157.0pt-\big[f^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{B}_{m}\right)+\bigO\big(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{3}\exp\big(\overline{B}_{m}^{2}\big)\left|R_{m}\right|\big)\big]\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\left[R_{m}\right],

(4.81)

where we have also bound
h_{\varepsilon}-h_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{\tau_{m}}\right)=\bigO\left(\left|x-\overline{\tau_{m}}\right|\right)
.
Expanding
\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\left[R_{m}\right]
as in (
4
), we then get

(B)_{jm}=-\theta_{j}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{m}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}f^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{m},\overline{\tau_{m}}}\right)B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\tau_{j}}\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\left[R_{m}\right]dx\\
+\bigO\left(\left|\theta_{j}\right|\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{4}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{m}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\exp\big(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{m},\overline{\tau_{m}}}^{2}\big)\left(\delta_{\varepsilon}+\left|x-\overline{\tau_{m}}\right|\right)dx\right),

hence

(B)_{jm}=-\theta_{j}\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\big[E^{\left(m\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big]\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{m}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}f^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{m},\overline{\tau_{m}}}\right)B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\tau_{j}}dx+\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{5}}\right).

Together with (
6.11
), for
j=m
, we obtain

\displaystyle(B)_{jj}

\displaystyle=-8\pi\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\theta_{j}\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\big[E^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big]+\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{5}}\right),

while, observing that
B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\tau_{j}}=\bigO\left(1\right)
on
B\left(\overline{\tau_{m}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)
if
j\neq m
, we get

(B)_{jm}=\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{5}}\right),\quad\text{for }j\neq m.

As for
(B^{\prime})_{jm}
, similarly as in (
4.78
), we can bound with (
3.21
) and Proposition
6.4

(B^{\prime})_{jm}=\int_{\Omega^{m}_{\varepsilon}}\left(\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}-1\right)\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\left[f_{\varepsilon}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right)\right]B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\tau_{j}}dx\\
=\bigO\left(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\int_{\Omega^{m}_{\varepsilon}}\left|f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right)\right|\left|Z_{0,j,\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right|dx\right)=\bigO\left(\overline{\mu}_{\varepsilon}^{3\delta_{0}-2\delta_{0}^{2}+\smallo\left(1\right)}\right)=\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{5}}\right).

(4.82)

Hence, finally, summing over
m
and
j
, we obtain

(B)=\sum_{j=1}^{k}(B)_{j}=-8\pi\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\theta_{j}\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\big[E^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big]+\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{5}}\right).

(4.83)

We now estimate the term
(C)
. Similar to (
4
), integration by parts and Proposition
3.2
give

\displaystyle(C)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\left[\Delta Z_{0,i}-f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\theta}\right)Z_{0,i}\right]\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}dx

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{k}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\left[\Delta U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}-f_{\varepsilon}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right)\right]\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}dx

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j=1}^{k}\int_{\Omega^{j}_{\varepsilon}}\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\left[\Delta U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}-f_{\varepsilon}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right)\right]\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}dx=:\sum_{j=1}^{k}\left[(C)_{j}+(C^{\prime})_{j}\right].

We can now use (
4
), and with the same notations, we write

(C)_{j}=-\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}\right)\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\big[R_{j}\big]\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}dx+\bigO\Bigg(\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{3}\exp\big(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}^{2}\big)\\
\times\left(\left(\left|x-\overline{\tau_{j}}\right|+\left|R_{j}\right|\right)\left|\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\left[\overline{B}_{j}\right]\right|+\left|R_{j}\right|\left|\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\left[R_{j}\right]\right|\right)\left|\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right|dx\Bigg).

The main term in
(C)_{j}
will be

\displaystyle(C_{1})_{j}

\displaystyle=-\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\big[E^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big]\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}\right)\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}dx

\displaystyle=-\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\big[E^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big]\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}2\gamma_{j}f_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}\right)\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}dx

\displaystyle\quad+\bigO\left(\frac{\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}|2\gamma_{j}f_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}\right)-f^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}\right)|\left|\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right|dx\right)

\displaystyle=-2\gamma_{j}\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\big[E^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big]\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\Delta B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\tau_{j}}\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}dx

\displaystyle\quad+\bigO\left(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\left|x-\overline{\tau}_{j}\right|f\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}\right)\left|\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right|dx\right)

\displaystyle+\bigO\left(\frac{\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\exp\big(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}^{2}\big)|2\gamma_{j}\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}-2\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}^{2}-1|\left|\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right|dx\right),

where we used that

\lambda_{\varepsilon,j}f\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}\right)=\Delta\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}=\Delta B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\tau_{j}},\quad\text{in }B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right).

Since
\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\perp B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\tau_{j}}
in
H^{1}_{0}\left(\Omega\right)
and
\Delta B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\tau_{j}}=0
in
\Omega\setminus B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)
, we have

\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\Delta B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\tau_{j}}\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}dx=\int_{\Omega}\left<\nabla B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\tau_{j}},\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right>dx=0,

(4.84)

and by Proposition
6.1
,

\gamma_{j}\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}-\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}^{2}=\bigO\left(1+t_{\gamma_{j}}\left(\cdot-\overline{\tau_{j}}\right)\right),\quad\text{in }B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right).

so that with a change of variables and Propositions
4.2
and
7.2
, we get

\displaystyle(C_{1})_{j}

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}r_{\varepsilon}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\exp\big(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}^{2}\big)\left|\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right|dx\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\bigO\left(\frac{\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\exp\big(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}^{2}\big)\left(1+t_{\gamma_{j}}\left(x-\overline{\tau_{j}}\right)\right)\left|\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right|dx\right)

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(r_{\varepsilon}\left\|\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right\|_{L^{2}}\right)+\bigO\left(\frac{\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{4}}\|\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau\theta}\|_{L^{2}}\right)=\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{5}}\right).

Note that we crucially used the orthogonality condition (
4.84
) to gain a factor
\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{-2}
. Again, with a change of variables and Proposition
7.2
, we bound

\displaystyle(C_{2})_{j}

\displaystyle=-\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}\right)\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\big[F^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big]\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}dx

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}\right)\left|x-\overline{\tau_{j}}\right|\left|\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right|dx\right)

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(r_{\varepsilon}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}\right)\left|\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right|dx\right)=\bigO\left(r_{\varepsilon}\|\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\|_{L^{2}}\right)=\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{5}}\right).

Similarly, for some exponent
a>0
(which plays no role),

\displaystyle(C_{3})_{j}

\displaystyle=\bigO\Bigg(\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{3}\exp\big(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}^{2}\big)\left(\left(\left|x-\overline{\tau_{j}}\right|+\left|R_{j}\right|\right)\left|\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\left[\overline{B}_{j}\right]\right|+\left|R_{j}\right|\left|\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\left[R_{j}\right]\right|\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\left|\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right|dx\Bigg)=\bigO\left(\left(\delta_{\varepsilon}+r_{\varepsilon}\right)\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{a}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\exp\big(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}^{2}\big)\left|\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right|dx\right)

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\left(\delta_{\varepsilon}+r_{\varepsilon}\right)\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{a-2}\|\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}|\|_{L^{2}}\right)=\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{5}}\right).

(4.85)

As for
(C^{\prime})_{j}
, in analogy with (
4.82
) (with
\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}
instead of
B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\tau_{j}}
), using (
3.21
) together with the Hölder and Poincaré inequalities, we bound

\displaystyle(C^{\prime})_{j}

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\int_{\Omega^{j}_{\varepsilon}}f^{\prime}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right)\left|\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right|dx\right)=\bigO\left(\|f^{\prime}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega^{j}_{\varepsilon})}\|\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\right)

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\overline{\mu}_{\varepsilon}^{\frac{1}{2}(3\delta_{0}-2\delta_{0}^{2})+\smallo(1)}\|\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\right)=\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{5}}\right).

Summing over
j
, we arrive at

(C)=\sum_{j=1}^{k}\left[(C_{1})_{j}+(C_{2})_{j}+(C_{3})_{j}+(C^{\prime})_{j}\right]=\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{5}}\right).

(4.86)

As for
(D)
, recalling that
\left|Z_{0,i}\right|=\bigO\left(1\right)
, we bound

\displaystyle(D)

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\int_{\Omega}\left|U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right|^{3}\exp\big(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}^{2}\big)\left(\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\theta_{i}^{2}\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}\right)dx\right)

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\int_{\Omega}\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{3}\exp\big(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}^{2}\big)\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}^{2}dx\right)+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\bigO\left(\theta_{i}^{2}\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}\int_{\Omega}\left|U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right|^{3}\exp\big(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}^{2}\big)dx\right)

\displaystyle=:(D_{1})+(D_{2}).

We first claim that

\int_{\Omega}\exp\big(U^{2}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big)dx=\bigO\left(1\right).

(4.87)

Indeed, with the usual decomposition given by (
4.70
), we get

\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\exp\big(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}^{2}\big)dx=\bigO\left(\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\exp\big(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}^{2}\big)dx\right)=\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right),

thanks to the usual change of variables and Proposition
6.3
. Then, summing over
j=1,\dots,k
and also using (
3.16
), we obtain (
4.87
). Then we immediately estimate

(D_{2})=\bigO\left(\theta_{i}^{2}\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{5}\right)=\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{5}}\right).

As for
(D_{1})
, from Hölder’s inequality and (
3.16
), we have

\displaystyle(D_{1}^{\prime}):=\int_{\Omega_{r_{\varepsilon},\tau}}\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{3}\exp\big(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}^{2}\big)\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}^{2}dx

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{3}\big\|\exp\big(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}^{2}\big)\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{r_{\varepsilon},\tau}}\big\|_{L^{p}}\left\|\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right\|_{L^{2p^{\prime}}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{3}\left\|\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)=\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{5}}\right),

where
p
is sufficiently small and
p^{\prime}
is the conjugate exponent of
p
. Moreover, with Proposition
7.2
, and the same change of variables used to estimate
(C_{1})_{j}
, we obtain

\displaystyle(D_{1})_{j}:=\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{3}\exp\big(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}^{2}\big)\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}^{2}dx

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{3}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\exp\big(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}^{2}\big)\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}^{2}dx\right)

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\left\|\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)=\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{5}}\right).

(4.88)

Summing up, we conclude

(D)=\sum_{j=1}^{k}(D_{1})_{j}+(D_{1}^{\prime})+(D_{2})=\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{5}}\right).

(4.89)

Now, putting together (
4.77
), (
4.78
), (
4.83
), (
4.86
) and (
4.89
), we conclude.
∎

Proof of (
4.64
).

We consider now (
4
) with
Z=B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}
and estimate the terms from
(A)
to
(D)
. Using (
4.67
), we get

\displaystyle(A)_{j}

\displaystyle=\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\underbrace{\left[\Delta\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}-\lambda_{\varepsilon,j}f\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}\right)\right]}_{=0}B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}dx

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\lambda_{\varepsilon}\left(h_{\varepsilon}-h_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{\tau_{j}}\right)\right)f\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}\right)B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}dx

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\lambda_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon}f^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}\right)R_{j}B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}dx+\bigO\left(\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{4}\exp\big(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}^{2}\big)R_{j}^{2}\right),

where
R_{j}
is as in (
4.71
). Similarly as in (
4
), we reduce to

\displaystyle(A)_{j}

\displaystyle=-\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\lambda_{\varepsilon,j}f^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}\right)E^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}dx

\displaystyle\quad+\bigO\left(\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{4}\exp\big(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}^{2}\big)\left(\left|x-\overline{\tau_{j}}\right|+\delta_{\varepsilon}^{2}\right)dx\right).

In the case
j=i
we use that

B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}=\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}\left(1+\bigO\left(\frac{\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right)\right),\quad\text{in }B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right),

(4.90)

(see Claim
3.1
) and with the usual change of variables, taking (
4.75
) and Propositions
6.3
and
6.4
into account, we obtain

\displaystyle(A)_{i}

\displaystyle=-\left(1+\bigO\left(\frac{\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right)\right)E^{\left(i\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\lambda_{\varepsilon,j}f^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}\right)\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}dx+\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=-\left(1+\bigO\left(\frac{\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right)\right)E^{\left(i\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\int_{B(0,\sqrt{\lambda_{\varepsilon,i}}r_{\varepsilon})}f^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{\gamma_{i}}\right)\overline{B}_{\gamma_{i}}dx+\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=-\left(8\pi+\bigO\left(\frac{\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right)\right)\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}E^{\left(i\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}+\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right)=-8\pi\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}E^{\left(i\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}+\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right).

For the case
j\neq i
, we use that
B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}=\bigO\left(\left(\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\right)/\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\right)
in
B(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon})
and with the same computations, we obtain

(A)_{j}=\bigO\left(\frac{\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right)|E^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}|\int_{B(0,\sqrt{\lambda_{\varepsilon,j}}r_{\varepsilon})}f^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{\gamma_{j}}\right)dx+\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right)=\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right),

so that summing up we conclude

(A)=\sum_{j=1}^{k}(A)_{j}=-8\pi\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}E^{\left(i\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}+\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right).

(4.91)

As for the annuli, similarly as in (
4.78
), we bound

(A^{\prime})=\int_{\cup_{j=1}^{k}\Omega^{j}_{\varepsilon}}\left(\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}-1\right)f_{\varepsilon}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right)B_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}dx=\bigO\left(\overline{\mu}_{\varepsilon}^{3\delta_{0}-2\delta_{0}^{2}+\smallo\left(1\right)}\right)=\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right).

(4.92)

We now turn to the estimate of
(B)
. Using a Taylor expansion, together with (
4.66
), (
4.70
) and (
4.71
), we write

\displaystyle(B)_{jm}

\displaystyle:=\theta_{j}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{m}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\left[\Delta B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\tau_{j}}-f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right)B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\tau_{j}}\right]B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}dx

\displaystyle=\theta_{j}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{m}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\left[\delta_{jm}\Delta\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}-f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{m},\overline{\tau_{m}}}\right)B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\tau_{j}}\right]B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}dx

\displaystyle\quad+\bigO\left(\left|\theta_{j}\right|\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{m}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{3}\exp\big(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{m},\overline{\tau_{m}}}^{2}\big)R_{m}B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}dx\right).

With Propositions
6.3
and
6.4
, we estimate the last term as
\smallo\left(\delta_{\varepsilon}/\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}\right)
.
For
j=m=i
, still with Proposition
6.3
, we compute, keeping (
4.90
) in mind

\theta_{i}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\left[\lambda_{\varepsilon,i}f\big(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}\big)-f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}\right)B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}\right]B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}dx\\
=-8\pi\theta_{i}\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}+\bigO\left(\left|\theta_{i}\right|\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\right)=-8\pi\theta_{i}\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}+\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right),

while for
j\neq m
, or
j\neq i
a similar computation based on Proposition
6.3
and (
4.90
) gives
(B)_{jm}=\smallo\left(\delta_{\varepsilon}/\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}\right).

Considering the integral in
\Omega_{r_{\varepsilon},\tau}
, where
\Delta B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\tau_{j}}=0
for every
j=1,\dots,k
, we estimate with the help of (
3.16
),

\displaystyle(B^{\prime})_{jm}

\displaystyle:=\theta_{j}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{m}},R_{\varepsilon}\right)\setminus B\left(\overline{\tau_{m}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right)B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\tau_{j}}B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}dx

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\left|\theta_{j}\right|\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{3}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{m}},R_{\varepsilon}\right)\setminus B\left(\overline{\tau_{m}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\exp\big(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}^{2}\big)dx\right)=\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right),

where
R_{\varepsilon}=\exp\left(-\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\right)
and, since
B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\tau_{j}}=\bigO\left(1\right)
in
\Omega\setminus B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)
, still with (
3.16
), we get

\displaystyle(B^{\prime\prime})_{jm}

\displaystyle:=\theta_{j}\int_{\Omega_{R_{\varepsilon},\tau}}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right)B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\tau_{j}}B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}dx=\bigO\left(\left|\theta_{j}\right|\right)=\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right),

In conclusion, we have proven that

(B)=\sum_{j=1}^{k}\left[(B)_{jm}+(B^{\prime})_{jm}+(B^{\prime\prime})_{jm}\right]=-8\pi\theta_{i}\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}+\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right).

(4.93)

To estimate
(C)
, we integrate by parts to obtain

\displaystyle(C)

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}\left[\Delta B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}-f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right)B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}\right]\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}dx=-\int_{\Omega}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right)B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}dx,

where we also used that
\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\perp B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}
in
H^{1}_{0}
. Using (
4.80
), we write

\displaystyle(C_{1})_{j}

\displaystyle:=-\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right)B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}dx

\displaystyle=-\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}\right)B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}dx

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\bigO\left(\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{3}\exp\big(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}^{2}\big)|R_{j}B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}|\right)}_{=\smallo\left(\delta_{\varepsilon}/\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}\right)\text{ as in \eqref{EqC}}}.

Then, recalling that
f^{\prime}\left(s\right)=\left(1+2s^{2}\right)\exp\left(s^{2}\right)=2sf\left(s\right)+\exp\left(s^{2}\right)
, which gives

\displaystyle(C_{1})_{j}

\displaystyle=-2\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}f_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}\right)B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}dx

\displaystyle\quad+\bigO\left(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\exp\big(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}^{2}\big)\left|\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right|dx\right)+\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right),

and using Proposition
7.2
, we obtain

\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\exp\big(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}^{2}\big)\left|\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right|dx=\bigO\left(\frac{\left\|\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right\|_{L^{2}}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}\right)=\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right).

Using Proposition
7.2
again, we simplify

\displaystyle(C_{1})_{i}=2\gamma_{i}^{2}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\lambda_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{\tau_{i}}\right)f\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}\right)\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}dx

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+\bigO\left(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\left|x-\overline{\tau_{i}}\right|f\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}\right)\left|\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right|dx\right)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+\bigO\left(\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\left(1+t_{\gamma_{i}}\left(x-\overline{\tau_{i}}\right)\right)f\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}\right)\left|\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right|dx\right)+\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=2\gamma_{i}^{2}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\Delta\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}dx+\bigO\left(\left(r_{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right)\left\|\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right\|_{L^{2}}\right)+\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right),

where the last integral vanishes thanks to
\Delta\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}=\Delta B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}
and to the condition
B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}\perp\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}
. A similar computation holds on
B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)
, where we can use that
B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}=\bigO\left(1\right)
if
j\neq i
. Hence

(C_{1})=\sum_{j=1}^{k}(C_{1})_{j}=\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right).

With (
3.16
) and the Hölder and Poincaré inequalities, we now bound

\displaystyle(C_{2})_{j}

\displaystyle:=-\sum_{j=1}^{k}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},R_{\varepsilon}\right)\setminus B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right)B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}dx

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{k}\bigO\left(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{3}\big\|\exp\big(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}^{2}\big)\mathbf{1}_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},R_{\varepsilon}\right)\setminus B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\big\|_{L^{p}}\left\|\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\frac{\left\|\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right\|_{L^{2}}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{a-3}}\right)=\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right),

upon choosing
a>3
. Again with (
3.16
) and the Hölder and Poincaré inequalities, and observing that
U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}=\bigO\left(1\right)
in
\Omega_{R_{\varepsilon},\tau}
,
we get

(C_{3}):=-\int_{\Omega_{R_{\varepsilon},\tau}}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right)B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}dx\\
=\bigO\left(\big\|\exp\big(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}^{2}\big)B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{R_{\varepsilon},\tau}}\big\|_{L^{p}}\left\|\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}}\right)=\bigO\left(\frac{\left\|\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right\|_{L^{2}}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}\right)=\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right),

where
p
is sufficiently small and
p^{\prime}
is the conjugate exponent of
p
. Adding up, we conclude

(C)=(C_{1})+(C_{2})+(C_{3})=\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right).

(4.94)

Finally the estimate

(D)=\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right)

(4.95)

follows exactly as the analog estimate in the proof of (
4.63
) (replacing
Z_{0,i,\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}
by
B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}})
, since all the terms contain
\theta_{i}^{2}
or
\left\|\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}
, which actually allows an estimate of the form
