(D)=\bigO\left(\delta_{\varepsilon}/\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{a}\right)
for every
a\geq 0
. Now, putting together (
4.91
), (
4.92
), (
4.93
), (
4.94
) and (
4.95
), we conclude.
∎

Proof of (
4.65
).

We now use (
4
) with
Z=Z_{1,i,\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}
, and again we need to estimate the terms from
(A)
to
(D)
.

We start with some estimates of
Z_{1,i}:=Z_{1,i,\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}
. From Claim
3.1
, we have

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau_{i}}\left[B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}\right]

\displaystyle=\partial_{\tau_{i}}\left[\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}\right]-\partial_{\tau_{i}}\left[C_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}\right]+\partial_{\tau_{i}}\left[A_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}H(\cdot,\overline{\tau_{i}})\right]

\displaystyle=\partial_{\tau_{i}}\left[\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}\right]+\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}\right),\quad\text{in }B\left(\overline{\tau}_{i},r_{\varepsilon}\right).

Now, recalling (
3.19
) and using (
3.15
), we write

Z_{1,i}=\partial_{\tau_{i}}\left[B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}\right]+\partial_{\tau_{i}}\left[\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right]=\partial_{\tau_{i}}\left[\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}\right]+\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}\right)=:Z_{1,i}^{a}+Z_{1,i}^{r},

(4.96)

in
B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)
, and with (
4.75
) and Proposition
6.1
, we estimate

Z^{a}_{1,i}=\frac{2}{\gamma_{i}}\frac{\lambda_{\varepsilon,i}\left(x_{1}-\tau_{i}\right)}{\mu_{i}^{2}+\lambda_{\varepsilon,i}\left|x-\overline{\tau_{i}}\right|^{2}}+\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{3}}\frac{1}{\mu_{i}+\left|x-\overline{\tau_{i}}\right|}\right),\quad\text{in }B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right),

(4.97)

where
\mu_{i}:=\mu_{\gamma_{i}}=\overline{\mu}_{\varepsilon}^{1+\smallo\left(1\right)}
is given by (
6.1
),
while directly from the definition of
B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}
, Claim
3.1
and (
3.15
), we also obtain

Z_{1,i}=\frac{(2+\smallo(1))\left(x_{1}-\tau_{i}\right)}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\left|x-\overline{\tau_{i}}\right|^{2}}+\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}\right),\quad\text{in }\Omega\setminus B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right),

(4.98)

which can be specialized to

Z_{1,i}=\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}d_{\varepsilon}}\right),\quad\text{in }B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right),\quad\text{for }j\neq i.

(4.99)

Let us also write from (
3.32
),

F^{\left(i\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\left(x\right)=\Lambda^{\left(i\right)}_{\varepsilon,\tau}\left(x_{1}-\tau_{i}\right)+\smallo\left(\frac{\left|x-\overline{\tau_{i}}\right|}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}d_{\varepsilon}}\right)\quad\text{in }B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right),

(4.100)

where

\Lambda^{\left(i\right)}_{\varepsilon,\tau}:=a_{0}l\tau_{i}^{l-1}-\frac{2}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{1}{\tau_{i}-\tau_{j}}=\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}d_{\varepsilon}}\right).

With the help of (
4.70
), as in (
4.73
), we can write

\displaystyle(A)_{j}=-\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\lambda_{\varepsilon}\left(h_{\varepsilon}-h_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{\tau_{j}}\right)\right)f\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}\right)Z_{1,i}dx

\displaystyle-\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\lambda_{\varepsilon,j}f^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}\right)R_{j}Z_{1,i}dx-\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\lambda_{\varepsilon}\left(h_{\varepsilon}-h_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{\tau_{j}}\right)\right)f^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}\right)R_{j}Z_{1,i}dx

\displaystyle+\bigO\left(\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{3}\exp\big(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}^{2}\big)R_{j}^{2}\left|Z_{1,i}\right|dx\right)=:(A_{1})_{j}+(A_{2})_{j}+(A_{3})_{j}+(A_{4}),

where
R_{j}
is as in (
4.71
).

We start with the main order term, which turns out to be the one involving
F^{\left(i\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}
and which we write, using (
4.97
) and (
4.100
), as

\displaystyle(A_{2}^{F})_{i}

\displaystyle:=-\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}f^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}\big(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}\big)F^{\left(i\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}Z_{1,i}dx

\displaystyle=-\frac{\Lambda_{\varepsilon,\tau}^{\left(i\right)}}{\gamma_{i}}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\lambda_{\varepsilon,i}f^{\prime}\big(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}\big)\bigg(\frac{2\lambda_{\varepsilon,i}\left(x_{1}-\tau_{i}\right)^{2}}{\mu_{i}^{2}+\lambda_{\varepsilon,i}\left|x-\overline{\tau_{i}}\right|^{2}}+\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}\right)\bigg)dx

\displaystyle\quad+\smallo\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}d_{\varepsilon}}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\lambda_{\varepsilon,i}f^{\prime}\big(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}\big)dx\right).

With the usual change of variables
\sqrt{\lambda_{\varepsilon,i}}\left(x-\overline{\tau_{i}}\right)=y
and using (
4.75
) and Proposition
6.3
together with
\gamma_{i}=\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\left(1+\smallo\left(1\right)\right)
and
\Lambda_{\varepsilon,\tau}^{\left(i\right)}=\bigO\left(1/\left(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}d_{\varepsilon}\right)\right)
, we get

\displaystyle(A_{2}^{F})_{i}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\Lambda_{\varepsilon,\tau}^{\left(i\right)}}{\gamma_{i}}\int_{B(0,\sqrt{\lambda_{\varepsilon,i}}r_{\varepsilon})}f^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{\gamma_{i}}\right)\bigg(\frac{2y_{1}^{2}}{\mu_{i}^{2}+\left|y\right|^{2}}+\smallo\left(1\right)\bigg)dy+\smallo\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}d_{\varepsilon}}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{(4\pi+\smallo(1))\Lambda_{\varepsilon,\tau}^{\left(i\right)}}{\gamma_{i}}+\smallo\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}d_{\varepsilon}}\right)=-\frac{4\pi\Lambda_{\varepsilon,\tau}^{\left(i\right)}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}+\smallo\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}d_{\varepsilon}}\right),

For
j\neq i
, using (
4.99
) and Proposition
6.4
, we get

\displaystyle(A_{2}^{F})_{j}

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}f^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}\right)\big|F^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big|\left|Z_{1,i}\right|dx\right)

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}f^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}\right)\frac{\left|x-\overline{\tau_{j}}\right|}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}d_{\varepsilon}}\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}d_{\varepsilon}}dx\right)

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}d_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\int_{B(0,\sqrt{\lambda_{\varepsilon,j}}r_{\varepsilon})}f^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{\gamma_{j}}\right)\left|y\right|dy\right)=\smallo\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}d_{\varepsilon}}\right).

Using (
4.96
), canceling the integral of the anti-symmetric term and using Proposition
6.3
, we get

\displaystyle(A_{2}^{E})_{i}

\displaystyle:=-\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}f^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}\big(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}\big)E^{\left(i\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}(Z_{1,i}^{a}+Z_{1,i}^{r})dx

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\frac{\big|E^{\left(i\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big|}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}f^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}\big(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}\big)dx\right)

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{4}}\int_{B(0,\sqrt{\lambda_{\varepsilon,i}}r_{\varepsilon})}f^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{B}_{\gamma_{i}}\right)dx\right)=\smallo\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}d_{\varepsilon}}\right).

When
j\neq i
, we have thanks to (
4.99
),

\displaystyle(A_{2}^{E})_{j}

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}f^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}\right)|E^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}|\left|Z_{1,i}\right|dx\right)

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{3}}\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}d_{\varepsilon}}\int_{B(0,\sqrt{\lambda_{\varepsilon,j}}r_{\varepsilon})}f^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{B}_{\gamma_{j}}\right)dx\right)=\smallo\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}d_{\varepsilon}}\right).

We now estimate
(A_{1})
. Using that
h_{\varepsilon}-h_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{\tau_{i}}\right)=\bigO\left(\left|x-\overline{\tau_{i}}\right|\right)
in
B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)
, by (
4.97
), we have

\left|(h_{\varepsilon}-h_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{\tau_{i}}\right))Z_{1,i}\right|=\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}\right),\quad\text{in }B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right),

(4.101)

and with Proposition
6.3
, we estimate

\displaystyle(A_{1})_{i}=\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}f\big(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}\big)dx\right)

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}\int_{B(0,\sqrt{\lambda_{\varepsilon,i}}r_{\varepsilon})}f\left(\overline{B}_{\gamma_{i}}\right)dy\right)

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right)=\smallo\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}d_{\varepsilon}}\right).

Observe that this says that thanks to
d_{\varepsilon}=\smallo\left(1\right)
, the term
\nabla h_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{\tau}_{i}\right)
does not play a role, contrary to what happens when the blow-up points are separated by a finite distance. For
j\neq i
, with (
4.99
), Proposition
6.4
and the usual change of variables we obtain

(A_{1})_{j}=\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}d_{\varepsilon}}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}f\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}\right)\left|x-\overline{\tau_{j}}\right|dx\right)=\smallo\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}d_{\varepsilon}}\right).

Similarly, one can bound with (
4.97
),

\displaystyle(A_{3})_{i}

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\left|x-\overline{\tau_{i}}\right|f^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}\right)\left|R_{i}\right|\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\left|x-\overline{\tau_{i}}\right|}dx\right)

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}f^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}\right)\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{3}}+\frac{\left|x-\overline{\tau_{i}}\right|}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}d_{\varepsilon}}\right)dx\right)=\smallo\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}d_{\varepsilon}}\right),

where we also used Propositions
6.3
and
6.4
. For
j\neq i
, an easier estimate holds, using (
4.99
) instead of (
4.97
), and
h_{\varepsilon}-h_{\varepsilon}\left(\tau_{i}\right)=\bigO\left(r_{\varepsilon}\right)
, so that

(A_{3})_{j}=\bigO\left(\frac{r_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}d_{\varepsilon}}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}f^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}\right)\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{3}}+\frac{\left|x-\overline{\tau_{j}}\right|}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}d_{\varepsilon}}\right)dx\right)=\smallo\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}d_{\varepsilon}}\right),

As for
(A_{4})_{i}
, using that

\big|E^{\left(i\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big|=\smallo\left(\delta_{\varepsilon}^{2}\left(\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\right)^{2}/\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{6}\right)=\bigO\big(\overline{\mu}_{\varepsilon}^{2\delta_{1}+1+\smallo\left(1\right)}\big),

and
\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{a}=\bigO\big(\overline{\mu}_{\varepsilon}^{\smallo\left(1\right)}\big)
for every
a\in\mathbb{R}
, we bound

\displaystyle(A_{4}^{E})_{i}

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\overline{\mu}_{\varepsilon}^{2\delta_{1}+1+\smallo\left(1\right)}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\exp\big(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}^{2}\big)\frac{1}{\mu_{i}+\left|x-\overline{\tau_{i}}\right|}dx\right)

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\overline{\mu}_{\varepsilon}^{2\delta_{1}+\smallo\left(1\right)}\int_{B(0,\sqrt{\lambda_{\varepsilon,i}}r_{\varepsilon})}\exp\big(\overline{B}_{\gamma_{i}}^{2}\big)dx\right)=\bigO\big(\overline{\mu}_{\varepsilon}^{2\delta_{1}+\smallo\left(1\right)}\big)=\smallo\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}d_{\varepsilon}}\right),

and, similarly, for
j\neq i
,

(A_{4}^{E})_{j}=\bigO\left(\overline{\mu}_{\varepsilon}^{2\delta_{1}+1+\smallo\left(1\right)}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\exp\big(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}^{2}\big)\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}d_{\varepsilon}}dx\right)=\smallo\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}d_{\varepsilon}}\right).

Using that
F^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}=\bigO\left(r_{\varepsilon}/\left(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}d_{\varepsilon}\right)\right)
, similarly as in the case of
(A_{4}^{E})_{j}
, we obtain

(A_{4}^{F})_{j}=\smallo\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}d_{\varepsilon}}\right),

including the case
j=i
. Summing over
j
, we obtain

(A)=\sum_{j=1}^{k}(A)_{j}=-\frac{4\pi\Lambda^{\left(i\right)}_{\varepsilon,\tau}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}+\smallo\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}d_{\varepsilon}}\right).

(4.102)

It remains to show that all the remaining terms are
\smallo\left(1/(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}d_{\varepsilon})\right)
.

Let us now estimate
(A^{\prime})
. By (
3.20
), (
3.21
), (
4.98
) and (
4.99
), we have

\displaystyle(A^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\sum_{j=1}^{k}\int_{\Omega_{\varepsilon}^{j}}\left|f_{\varepsilon}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right)\right|\left|Z_{1,i}\right|dx\right)=\bigO\left(\frac{\overline{\mu}_{\varepsilon}^{-2\delta_{0}^{2}+\smallo\left(1\right)}r_{\varepsilon}^{2}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}\right)

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\overline{\mu}_{\varepsilon}^{2\delta_{0}-2\delta_{0}^{2}+\smallo\left(1\right)}\right)=\smallo\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}d_{\varepsilon}}\right),

(4.103)

absorbing powers of
\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}
in the term
\overline{\mu}_{\varepsilon}^{\smallo\left(1\right)}
and using that
2\delta_{0}-2\delta_{0}^{2}>0
.

We now estimate
(B)
. Since
\Delta B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\tau_{j}}=\Delta\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}\textbf{1}_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}
, we have

(B^{\dagger})_{j}:=\theta_{j}\int_{\Omega}\Delta B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\tau_{j}}Z_{1,i}dx=\theta_{j}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\Delta\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}Z_{1,i}dx.

Then for
j\neq i
, together with (
4.99
) we obtain

\theta_{j}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\Delta\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}Z_{1,i}dx=\bigO\left(\frac{\left|\theta_{j}\right|}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}d_{\varepsilon}}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}f\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\overline{\tau_{j}}}\right)dx\right)=\smallo\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}d_{\varepsilon}}\right).

For
j=i
, we use the anti-symmetry to obtain

\theta_{i}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\Delta\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}Z_{1,i}dx=\theta_{i}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\lambda_{\varepsilon,i}f\big(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}\big)\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}\left(Z_{1,i}^{a}+Z_{1,i}^{r}\right)dx\\
=\theta_{i}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\lambda_{\varepsilon,i}f\big(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}\big)\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}D_{\tau_{i}}\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}dx=\smallo\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}d_{\varepsilon}}\right).

In order to estimate the second term in the integral in
(B)_{j}
, we start with the integral away from the blow-up points, and using (
3.17
), the definition of
B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\tau_{j}}
and (
3.10
), we get

(B^{\prime}_{j})=\theta_{j}\int_{\Omega_{r_{\varepsilon},\tau}}f^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right)B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\tau_{j}}Z_{1,i}dx=\bigO\big(\left|\theta_{j}\right|\big\|f^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right)Z_{1,i}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{r_{\varepsilon},\tau}}\big\|_{L^{p}}\\
\times\big\|B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\tau_{j}}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega\backslash B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\big\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}}\big)=\bigO\left(\left|\theta_{j}\right|\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\left\|\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}\ln\frac{C}{|\cdot-\overline{\tau_{j}}|}\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}}\right)=\smallo\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}d_{\varepsilon}}\right),

where
p
is sufficiently small and
p^{\prime}
is the conjugate exponent of
p
. It remains to estimate

(B)_{jm}:=\theta_{j}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{m}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\tau_{j}}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right)Z_{1,i}dx.

For
m\neq i
, it easily follows from (
4.99
) and Proposition
6.3
that

(B)_{jm}=\bigO\left(\frac{\left|\theta_{j}\right|}{d_{\varepsilon}}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{m}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right)dx\right)=\smallo\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}d_{\varepsilon}}\right).

The case
m=i
is more subtle.
Using (
4.80
) to split

(B)_{ji}=\theta_{j}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\tau_{j}}f_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}\big(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}\big)Z_{1,i}dx\\
+\bigO\left(\left|\theta_{j}\right|\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{3}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\exp\big(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}^{2}\big)\left|R_{i}\right|\left|Z_{1,i}\right|dx\right)=:(B_{1})_{ji}+(B_{2})_{ji}.

(4.104)

Now, writing

B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}=B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}^{s}+B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}^{r},

where

\displaystyle B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}^{s}

\displaystyle=\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}-C_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}+A_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}H\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},\overline{\tau_{i}}\right),

\displaystyle B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}^{r}

\displaystyle=A_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}\left(H\left(\cdot,\overline{\tau_{i}}\right)-H\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},\overline{\tau_{i}}\right)\right)=\bigO\left(\frac{|\cdot-\overline{\tau_{i}}|}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}\right),\quad\text{in }B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right),

and also using (
4.96
) and (
4.101
), we get

(B_{1})_{ii}=\theta_{i}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}^{s}\lambda_{\varepsilon,i}f^{\prime}\big(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}\big)Z_{1,i}^{a}dx\\
+\bigO\bigg(\left|\theta_{i}\right|\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}f^{\prime}\big(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}\big)\big(1+\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\underbrace{\left|x-\overline{\tau_{j}}\right|\left|Z_{1,i}\right|}_{=\bigO\left(1/\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\right)}\big)dx\bigg)=\smallo\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}d_{\varepsilon}}\right),

(4.105)

where we used skew-symmetry to cancel the first integral, and the usual change of variables to estimate the second one with Proposition
6.3
. When
j\neq i
, a similar approach gives analog results, with the splitting of
B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\tau_{j}}=B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\tau_{j}}^{s}+B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\tau_{j}}^{r}\left(x\right)
, where

\displaystyle B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\tau_{j}}^{s}

\displaystyle=A_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\tau_{j}}G\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},\overline{\tau_{i}}\right),

\displaystyle B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\tau_{j}}^{r}

\displaystyle=A_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\tau_{j}}\left(G\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},x\right)-G\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},\overline{\tau_{i}}\right)\right)=\bigO\left(\frac{\left|x-\overline{\tau_{i}}\right|}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}d_{\varepsilon}}\right),\quad\text{in }B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right),

which allows to cancel the symmetric term and obtain

(B_{1})_{ji}=\smallo\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}d_{\varepsilon}}\right)

As for
(B_{2})_{ji}
, the term involving
F^{\left(i\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}
can be estimated using a similar approach as in (
4.105
), since
F^{\left(i\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\left(x\right)=\bigO\left(\left|x-\overline{\tau_{i}}\right|/(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}d_{\varepsilon})\right)
in
B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)
. In the term involving
E^{\left(i\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}=\bigO\left(\delta_{\varepsilon}\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}/\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{3}\right)
, we use the estimate

\left|Z_{1,i}\right|=\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\left(\mu_{\gamma_{i}}+\left|x-\overline{\tau_{i}}\right|\right)}\right)=\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\mu}_{\varepsilon}^{1+\smallo\left(1\right)}}\right)

(4.106)

to finally obtain

(B_{2})_{ji}=\bigO\left(\frac{\left|\theta_{j}\right|\delta_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\mu}_{\varepsilon}^{1+\smallo\left(1\right)}}\right)=\smallo\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}d_{\varepsilon}}\right).

Summing up, we conclude

(B)=\sum_{j=1}^{k}\left[(B^{\dagger})_{j}+(B^{\prime})_{j}+\sum_{m=1}^{k}(B)_{jm}\right]=\smallo\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}d_{\varepsilon}}\right).

(4.107)

To bound the term
(C)
, let us start by observing that
\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\perp Z_{1,i}
implies

(C_{1}):=\int_{\Omega}\Delta\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}Z_{1,i}dx=0,

so that it remains to bound

(C_{2}):=-\int_{\Omega}f^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right)\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}Z_{1,i}dx.

Observe that a rough estimate on
B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)
using
\left|Z_{1,i}\right|=\bigO\left(1/\overline{\mu}_{\varepsilon}\right)
would lead to an exponentially large error term. Therefore we have to be more subtle and use again the Sobolev–Poincaré estimates which follow from
\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\perp B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}
.
We start by noticing that by (
3.17
) and the Sobolev embedding, we have

(C_{2}^{*}):=-\int_{\Omega_{r_{\varepsilon},\tau}}f^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right)\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}Z_{1,i}dx=\bigO\big(\big\|f^{\prime}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right)Z_{1,i}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{r_{\varepsilon},\tau}}\big\|_{L^{p}}\\
\times\left\|\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}}\big)=\bigO\left(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\left\|\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right\|_{L^{2}}\right)=\bigO\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}\right)=\smallo\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}d_{\varepsilon}}\right).

For
j\neq i
, we bound with (
4.99
) and Proposition
7.2
(which we can use thanks to (
4.84
)),

\displaystyle(C_{2}^{\dagger})_{j}

\displaystyle:=-\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}f^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right)Z_{1,i}\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}dx

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}d_{\varepsilon}}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}f^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right)\left|\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right|dx\right)

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\frac{\left\|\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right\|_{L^{2}}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}d_{\varepsilon}}\right)=\bigO\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{3}d_{\varepsilon}}\right)=\smallo\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}d_{\varepsilon}}\right).

(4.108)

We are left with
(C_{2}^{\dagger})_{i}
which we expand as in (
4.80
), giving

(C_{2}^{\dagger})_{i}=-\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}f^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}\big(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}\big)Z_{1,i}\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}dx\\
+\bigO\left(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{3}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\exp\big(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}^{2}\big)\big|\big(E^{(i)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}+F^{(i)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big)Z_{1,i}\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\big|dx\right)

(4.109)

The remainder term in (
4.109
) can be estimated as follows. By (
4.97
) and (
4.100
), we get
|F^{(i)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}Z_{1,i}|=\bigO\left(1/\left(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}d_{\varepsilon}\right)\right)
, and we use Proposition
7.2
to obtain an error term of order

\bigO\left(\frac{\|\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\|_{L^{2}}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}d_{\varepsilon}}\right)=\smallo\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}d_{\varepsilon}}\right).

As regards the term involving
E^{(i)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}
, using (
4.106
) and Proposition
7.2
, we obtain an error term of order

\bigO\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}\|\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\|_{L^{2}}}{\overline{\mu}_{\varepsilon}^{1+\smallo(1)}}\right)=\bigO\left(\overline{\mu}_{\varepsilon}^{2\delta_{1}+\smallo(1)}\right)=\smallo\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}d_{\varepsilon}}\right).

The first integral in (
4.109
) can be estimated by using (
4.96
) together with the estimate
\big(h_{\varepsilon}-h_{\varepsilon}\left(\tau_{i}\right)\big)\partial_{\tau_{i}}\left[\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}\right]=\bigO\left(1/\gamma_{\varepsilon}\right)
to obtain

\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}f^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}\big(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}\big)Z_{1,i}\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}dx=\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\lambda_{\varepsilon,i}f^{\prime}\big(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}\big)\partial_{\tau_{i}}\left[\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}\right]\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}dx\\
+\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}f^{\prime}\big(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}\big)\left|\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right|dx\right)=:-(C_{2}^{\ddagger})_{i}+(C_{2}^{r})_{i}.

The remainder term
(C_{2}^{r})_{i}
can be handled as in (
4
), giving

(C_{2}^{r})_{i}=\bigO\left(\frac{\left\|\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right\|_{L^{2}}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}\right)=\smallo\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}d_{\varepsilon}}\right).

Then we are left with the term
(C_{2}^{\ddagger})_{i}
, which is actually more subtle to bound. Let us first rewrite it as

(C_{2}^{\ddagger})_{i}=-\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\lambda_{\varepsilon,i}\Delta Z_{1,i}\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}dx,

using that

\Delta Z_{1,i}=\partial_{\tau_{i}}\left[\Delta U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right]=\partial_{\tau_{i}}\left[\Delta\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}\right]=\lambda_{\varepsilon,i}\partial_{\tau_{i}}\left[f\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\overline{\tau_{i}}}\right)\right],\quad\text{in }B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right).

In order to estimate
(C_{2}^{\ddagger})_{i}
we start by observing that the orthogonality condition
Z_{1,i}\perp\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}
and integration by parts imply

0=\int_{\Omega}\left<\nabla Z_{1,i},\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right>dx=\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\Delta Z_{1,i}\,\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}dx\\
+\int_{\Omega\backslash B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\Delta Z_{1,i}\,\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}dx+\int_{\partial B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\left(\partial_{\nu}Z_{1,i}^{int}-\partial_{\nu}Z_{1,i}^{ext}\right)\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}d\sigma.

(4.110)

Here
\nu
denotes the exterior normal to
\partial B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)
and

Z_{1,i}^{int}:=Z_{1,i}|_{\overline{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}},\quad Z_{1,i}^{ext}:=Z_{1,i}|_{\Omega\setminus B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}.

Note that the boundary integral in (
4.110
) is in general non-zero because
B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}
is
C^{1}
but not smooth across
\partial B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)
.
Now we reduced the estimate of
(C_{2}^{\ddagger})_{i}
to

(C_{2}^{\ddagger})_{i}=\int_{\Omega\backslash B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\Delta Z_{1,i}\,\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}dx+\int_{\partial B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\left(\partial_{\nu}Z_{1,i}^{int}-\partial_{\nu}Z_{1,i}^{ext}\right)\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}d\sigma.

Now using that

\displaystyle\Delta Z_{1,i}

\displaystyle=\partial_{\tau_{i}}\left[\Delta U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right]=\partial_{\tau_{i}}\left[\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}f_{\varepsilon}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\partial_{\tau_{i}}\left[\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}\right]f_{\varepsilon}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right)+\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}f^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right)\partial_{\tau_{i}}\left[U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right],\quad\text{in }B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right).

from (
3.17
), we obtain

\big\|\Delta Z_{1,i}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega\backslash B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\big\|_{L^{p}}=\bigO\left(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\right)

for some
p>1
, hence with the Hölder and Sobolev inequalities

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega\backslash B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\Delta Z_{1,i}\,\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}dx

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\big\|\Delta Z_{1,i}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega\backslash B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\big\|_{L^{p}}\left\|\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\left\|\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right\|_{L^{2}}\right)=\smallo\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}d_{\varepsilon}}\right).

Observe that
D_{\tau}\left[\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right]\in C^{1}\left(\overline{\Omega}\right)
, by elliptic estimates (the function
\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}
in Proposition
3.2
is smooth), hence we get

\partial_{\nu}Z_{1,i}^{int}-\partial_{\nu}Z_{1,i}^{ext}=\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\tau_{i}}\left[B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}^{int}\right]-\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\tau_{i}}\left[B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}^{ext}\right]

(4.111)

Using the definition of
B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}
and (
3.12
), we compute

\displaystyle\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\tau_{i}}\left[B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}^{int}\right]

\displaystyle=\partial_{\tau_{i}}\left[\partial_{\nu}B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}^{int}\right]=\partial_{\tau_{i}}\left[\partial_{\nu}\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}\right]+\partial_{\tau_{i}}\left[\partial_{\nu}(A_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}H\left(\cdot,\overline{\tau_{i}}\right))\right]

\displaystyle=\partial_{\tau_{i}}\left[\partial_{\nu}\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}\right]+\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}\right).

Similarly,

\displaystyle\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\tau_{i}}\left[B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}^{ext}\right]

\displaystyle=\partial_{\tau_{i}}\left[\frac{A_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}}{2\pi}\partial_{\nu}\left(\ln\frac{1}{\left|x-\overline{\tau_{i}}\right|}\right)\right]+\partial_{\tau_{i}}\left[\partial_{\nu}\left(A_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}H\left(\cdot,\overline{\tau_{i}}\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle=-\partial_{\tau_{i}}\left[\frac{A_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}}{2\pi\left|x-\overline{\tau_{i}}\right|}\right]+\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}\right).

Now in order to compute the difference of the two terms in (
4.111
), set

v_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}\left(x\right):=\frac{A_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}}{2\pi}\ln\frac{1}{\left|x-\overline{\tau_{i}}\right|}

and Note that
v_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}^{\prime}\left(r_{\varepsilon}\right)=\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}^{\prime}\left(r_{\varepsilon}\right)
by the definitions in Section
3.1
, where with a little abuse of notation, we use the prime to denote the radial derivative from
\overline{\tau_{i}}
.
Then, with a similar abuse of notation

\displaystyle-\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}^{\prime\prime}\left(r_{\varepsilon}\right)-\frac{\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}^{\prime}\left(r_{\varepsilon}\right)}{r_{\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle=\Delta\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}\left(r_{\varepsilon}\right)=\lambda_{\varepsilon,i}f\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}\left(r_{\varepsilon}\right)\right),

\displaystyle-v_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}^{\prime\prime}\left(r_{\varepsilon}\right)-\frac{v_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}^{\prime}\left(r_{\varepsilon}\right)}{r_{\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle=\Delta v_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}\left(r_{\varepsilon}\right)=0,

and subtracting we finally estimate

\displaystyle\left|\partial_{\nu}Z_{1,i}^{int}-\partial_{\nu}Z_{1,i}^{ext}\right|

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\left|\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}^{\prime\prime}\left(r_{\varepsilon}\right)-v_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}^{\prime\prime}\left(r_{\varepsilon}\right)\right|\right)+\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}\right)

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(f\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}\left(r_{\varepsilon}\right)\right)\right)+\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}\right)=\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\mu}_{\varepsilon}^{2\delta_{0}^{2}+\smallo\left(1\right)}}\right).

(4.112)

We now claim that

\left\|\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right\|_{L^{1}\left(\partial B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)\right)}=\bigO\left(r_{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\ln\frac{1}{r_{\varepsilon}}}+\left\|\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right\|_{L^{2}}\right)=\bigO\big(\overline{\mu}_{\varepsilon}^{\delta_{0}+\smallo\left(1\right)}\big).

(4.113)

This, together with (
4
) allows to bound

\displaystyle\int_{\partial B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\left(\partial_{\nu}Z_{1,i}^{int}-\partial_{\nu}Z_{1,i}^{ext}\right)\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}d\sigma

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\frac{\left\|\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right\|_{L^{1}\left(\partial B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)\right)}}{\overline{\mu}_{\varepsilon}^{2\delta_{0}^{2}+\smallo\left(1\right)}}\right)

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\overline{\mu}_{\varepsilon}^{\delta_{0}-2\delta_{0}^{2}+\smallo\left(1\right)}\right)=\smallo\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}d_{\varepsilon}}\right).

This completes the estimates of
(C_{2}^{\ddagger})_{i}
, hence

(C)=(C_{1})+(C_{2}^{\ast})+\sum_{j=1}^{k}\left[(C_{2}^{\dagger})_{j}+(C_{2}^{\ddagger})_{j}\right]=\smallo\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}d_{\varepsilon}}\right).

(4.114)

In order to prove (
4.113
), set
\widetilde{\Phi}\left(y\right):=\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\left(\overline{\tau_{i}}+r_{\varepsilon}y\right)
. We then have

\big\|\nabla\widetilde{\Phi}\big\|_{L^{2}\left(B\left(0,1\right)\right)}=\left\|\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right\|_{L^{2}\left(B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)\right)}.

By the trace inequality, we get

\frac{\left\|\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right\|_{L^{1}\left(B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)\right)}}{r_{\varepsilon}}=\big\|\widetilde{\Phi}\big\|_{L^{1}\left(B\left(0,1\right)\right)}=\bigO\left(\big\|\nabla\widetilde{\Phi}\big\|_{L^{2}\left(B\left(0,1\right)\right)}+\left|\frac{1}{\left|B\left(0,1\right)\right|}\int_{B\left(0,1\right)}\widetilde{\Phi}dy\right|\right),

(4.115)

since the right-hand side contains a norm equivalent to the
H^{1}
-norm. Now, by the Jensen and Moser–Trudinger inequalities, we have

\displaystyle\exp\left(\left(\frac{1}{|B\left(0,1\right)|}\int_{B\left(0,1\right)}\widetilde{\Phi}dy\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|B\left(0,1\right)|}\int_{B\left(0,1\right)}\exp\big(\widetilde{\Phi}^{2}\big)dy

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\pi r_{\varepsilon}}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\exp\big(\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}^{2}\big)dx\leq\frac{1}{\pi r_{\varepsilon}}

It follows that

\left|\frac{1}{|B\left(0,1\right)|}\int_{B\left(0,1\right)}\widetilde{\Phi}dy\right|\leq\sqrt{\ln\frac{1}{\pi r_{\varepsilon}}}

and (
4.113
) follows at once from (
4.115
). This completes the proof of (
4.114
).

We finally estimate

\displaystyle(D)

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\int_{\Omega}\left|U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right|^{3}\exp\big(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}^{2}\big)\left(\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}^{2}+\sum_{j=1}^{k}\theta_{j}^{2}B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\tau_{j}}^{2}\right)\left|Z_{1,i}\right|dx\right)

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\int_{\Omega}\left|U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right|^{3}\exp\big(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}^{2}\big)\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}^{2}\left|Z_{1,i}\right|dx\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j=1}^{k}\bigO\left(\theta_{j}^{2}\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}\int_{\Omega}\left|U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right|^{3}\exp\big(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}^{2}\big)\left|Z_{1,i}\right|dx\right)=:(D_{1})+\sum_{j=1}^{k}(D_{2})_{j}.

For every
j\in\left\{1,\dotsc,k\right\}
, with the rough estimate

Z_{1,i}=\bigO\left(\mu_{\gamma_{i}}^{-1}\right)=\bigO\big(\overline{\mu}_{\varepsilon}^{-1+\smallo\left(1\right)}\big),\quad\text{in }B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right),

we obtain as in (
4
)

\displaystyle(D_{1})_{j}

\displaystyle:=\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\left|U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right|^{3}\exp\big(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}^{2}\big)\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}^{2}\left|Z_{1,i}\right|dx

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\frac{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{3}}{\overline{\mu}_{\varepsilon}^{1+\smallo\left(1\right)}}\left(\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\exp\big(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\tau_{j}}^{2}\big)\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}^{2}dx\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\frac{\left\|\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}{\overline{\mu}_{\varepsilon}^{1+\smallo\left(1\right)}}\right)=\bigO\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}^{2}}{\overline{\mu}_{\varepsilon}^{1+\smallo\left(1\right)}}\right)=\bigO\big(\overline{\mu}_{\varepsilon}^{2\delta_{1}+\smallo\left(1\right)}\big)=\smallo\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}d_{\varepsilon}}\right).

Similarly, with (
3.17
),

(D_{1}^{\prime}):=\int_{\Omega_{r_{\varepsilon},\tau}}\left|U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right|^{3}\exp\big(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}^{2}\big)\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}^{2}\left|Z_{1,i}\right|dx=\bigO\Big(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\big\|f^{\prime}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right)Z_{1,i}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{r_{\varepsilon},\tau}}\big\|_{L^{p}}\\
\times\left\|\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2p^{\prime}}}\Big)=\bigO\left(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}\left\|\nabla\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)=\smallo\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}d_{\varepsilon}}\right).

As for the terms involving
\theta_{j}
, we can use the rough estimate
Z_{1,i}=\bigO\big(\overline{\mu}_{\varepsilon}^{-1+\smallo\left(1\right)}\big)
to get

\displaystyle(D_{2})_{j}

\displaystyle:=\theta_{j}^{2}\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{m}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\left|U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right|^{3}\exp\big(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}^{2}\big)\left|Z_{1,i}\right|dx

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\frac{\theta_{j}^{2}\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{5}}{\overline{\mu}_{\varepsilon}^{1+\smallo\left(1\right)}}\left(\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{m}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\exp\big(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{m},\tau_{m}}^{2}\big)dx\right)\right)=\bigO\big(\overline{\mu}_{\varepsilon}^{2\delta_{1}+\smallo\left(1\right)}\big)=\smallo\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}d_{\varepsilon}}\right),

while in
\Omega_{r_{\varepsilon},\tau}
, we can use (
3.17
) with
p=1
to obtain

(D_{2}^{\prime})_{j}:=\theta_{j}^{2}\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}\int_{\Omega_{r_{\varepsilon},\tau}}\left|U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right|^{3}\exp\big(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}^{2}\big)\left|Z_{1,i}\right|dx=\bigO(\theta_{j}^{2}\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{4})=\smallo\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}d_{\varepsilon}}\right).

Summing up, we obtain

(D)=\sum_{j=1}^{k}(D_{1})_{j}+(D_{1}^{\prime})+\sum_{j=1}^{k}\left[(D_{2})_{j}+(D_{2}^{\prime})_{j}\right]=\smallo\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}d_{\varepsilon}}\right).

(4.116)

Now, (
4.102
), (
4
), (
4.107
), (
4.114
) and (
4.116
) allow us to conclude.
∎

Proof of Proposition
4.4
.

We claim that for
\delta
and
\varepsilon
small enough, we can find
(\gamma_{\varepsilon},\theta_{\varepsilon},\tau_{\varepsilon})\in P^{k}_{\varepsilon}\left(\delta\right)
such that

\displaystyle\left<\widetilde{R}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{\varepsilon},\tau_{\varepsilon},\theta_{\varepsilon}},Z_{0,i,\varepsilon,\gamma_{\varepsilon},\tau_{\varepsilon}}\right>_{H^{1}_{0}}=\left<\widetilde{R}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{\varepsilon},\tau_{\varepsilon},\theta_{\varepsilon}},B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{\varepsilon},\tau_{\varepsilon}}\right>_{H^{1}_{0}}

\displaystyle=\left<\widetilde{R}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{\varepsilon},\tau_{\varepsilon},\theta_{\varepsilon}},Z_{1,i,\varepsilon,\gamma_{\varepsilon},\tau_{\varepsilon}}\right>_{H^{1}_{0}}=0,

(4.117)

for
i=1,\dots,k
, so that

\Pi_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{\varepsilon},\tau_{\varepsilon}}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{\varepsilon},\tau_{\varepsilon},\theta_{\varepsilon}}+\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{\varepsilon},\tau_{\varepsilon},\theta_{\varepsilon}}-\Delta^{-1}\left(\lambda_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon}f\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{\varepsilon},\tau_{\varepsilon},\theta_{\varepsilon}}+\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{\varepsilon},\tau_{\varepsilon},\theta_{\varepsilon}}\right)\right)\right)=0,

hence, together with Proposition
4.2
,
U_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{\varepsilon},\tau_{\varepsilon},\theta_{\varepsilon}}+\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{\varepsilon},\tau_{\varepsilon},\theta_{\varepsilon}}
is a solution to (
4.62
).
For every
\tau\in T^{k}_{\varepsilon}\left(\delta\right)
and
\gamma\in\widehat{\Gamma}^{k}_{\varepsilon}\left(\tau\right)
, let us set
\hat{\gamma}:=\gamma-\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\left(\tau\right)
and

\widehat{\Gamma}^{k}_{\varepsilon}:=\left\{\hat{\gamma}=\left(\hat{\gamma}_{1},\dotsc,\hat{\gamma}_{k}\right)\in(0,\infty)^{k}:\,\left|\hat{\gamma}_{i}\right|<\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}},\,\forall i\in\{i,\dots,k\}\right\},

and define
\left(L_{\varepsilon},M_{\varepsilon},N_{\varepsilon}\right):\widehat{P}^{k}_{\varepsilon}\left(\delta\right):=\widehat{\Gamma}^{k}_{\varepsilon}\times\Theta^{k}_{\varepsilon}\times T^{k}_{\varepsilon}\left(\delta\right)\to\mathbb{R}^{3k}
as

\displaystyle L_{\varepsilon}^{i}\left(\hat{\gamma},\tau,\theta\right)

\displaystyle:=-\frac{1}{8\pi}\left<\widetilde{R}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta},Z_{0,i,\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right>_{H^{1}_{0}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{k}\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\big[E^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big]\big(E^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}+\theta_{j}\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\big)-\frac{E^{\left(i\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}}{2\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}+\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{5}}\right),

\displaystyle M_{\varepsilon}^{i}\left(\hat{\gamma},\tau,\theta\right)

\displaystyle:=-\frac{1}{8\pi\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}\left<\widetilde{R}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta},B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}\right>_{H^{1}_{0}}=E^{\left(i\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}+\theta_{i}\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}+\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{3}}\right),

\displaystyle N_{\varepsilon}^{i}\left(\hat{\gamma},\tau,\theta\right)

\displaystyle:=-\frac{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}d_{\varepsilon}}{4\pi}\left<\widetilde{R}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau,\theta},Z_{1,i,\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right>_{H^{1}_{0}}=a_{0}l\left(\frac{\tau_{i}}{d_{\varepsilon}}\right)^{l-1}-\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{2d_{\varepsilon}}{\tau_{i}-\tau_{j}}+\smallo\left(1\right)

for
i=1,\dots k
, where the error terms in the right-hand sides are uniform for
\left(\hat{\gamma},\tau,\theta\right)\in\widehat{P}^{k}_{\varepsilon}\left(\delta\right)
. (Note that we wrote
\hat{\gamma}
in the left-hand side and
\gamma
instead of
\hat{\gamma}+\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\left(\tau\right)
in the right-hand side for simplicity, so that for instance the terms
E^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}
should be read as
E^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\hat{\gamma}+\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\left(\tau\right),\tau}
)

We claim that

\deg\big(\left(L_{\varepsilon},M_{\varepsilon},N_{\varepsilon}\right),\widehat{P}^{k}_{\varepsilon}\left(\delta\right),0\big)\neq 0

(4.118)

for
\delta
and
\varepsilon
small (to be fixed), where
\deg
denotes the Brouwer degree.
Let us consider the homotopy
\left(L_{\varepsilon}^{t},M_{\varepsilon}^{t},N_{\varepsilon}^{t}\right):\widehat{P}^{k}_{\varepsilon}\left(\delta\right)\to\mathbb{R}^{3k}
with
L_{\varepsilon}^{t}=\left(L_{\varepsilon}^{t,1},\dotsc,L_{\varepsilon}^{t,k}\right)
, etc. defined by

\displaystyle L_{\varepsilon}^{t,i}

\displaystyle=\left(1-t\right)\overline{L}_{\varepsilon}^{i}+tL_{\varepsilon}^{i},\quad\overline{L}_{\varepsilon}^{i}:=\sum_{j=1}^{k}\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\big[E^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big]\big(E^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}+\theta_{j}\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\big)-\frac{E^{\left(i\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}}{2\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}},

\displaystyle M_{\varepsilon}^{t,i}

\displaystyle=\left(1-t\right)\overline{M}_{\varepsilon}^{i}+tM_{\varepsilon}^{i},\quad\overline{M}_{\varepsilon}^{i}:=E^{\left(i\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}+\theta_{i}\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon},

\displaystyle N_{\varepsilon}^{t,i}

\displaystyle=\left(1-t\right)\overline{N}_{\varepsilon}^{i}+tN_{\varepsilon}^{i},\quad\overline{N}_{\varepsilon}^{i}:=a_{0}l\left(\frac{\tau_{i}}{d_{\varepsilon}}\right)^{l-1}-\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{2d_{\varepsilon}}{\tau_{i}-\tau_{j}}.

for
i=1,\dots k
and
t\in\left[0,1\right]
. We first show that
\left(L_{\varepsilon}^{t},M_{\varepsilon}^{t},N_{\varepsilon}^{t}\right)\neq 0
on
\partial\widehat{P}^{k}_{\varepsilon}\left(\delta\right)
for any
t\in[0,1]
if
\varepsilon>0
is sufficiently small. Otherwise there would be a sequence
\varepsilon_{n}\downarrow 0
(which we still denote by
\varepsilon
),
t_{\varepsilon}\in[0,1]
and

\left(\hat{\gamma}_{\varepsilon},\theta_{\varepsilon},\tau_{\varepsilon}\right)\in\partial\widehat{P}^{k}_{\varepsilon},\quad\text{i.e. }\hat{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\in\partial\widehat{\Gamma}^{k}_{\varepsilon},\text{ or }\theta_{\varepsilon}\in\partial\Theta^{k}_{\varepsilon},\text{ or }\tau_{\varepsilon}\in\partial T^{k}_{\varepsilon}\left(\delta\right),

(4.119)

such that

\left(L_{\varepsilon}^{t_{\varepsilon}}\left(\hat{\gamma}_{\varepsilon},\theta_{\varepsilon},\tau_{\varepsilon}\right),M_{\varepsilon}^{t_{\varepsilon}}\left(\hat{\gamma}_{\varepsilon},\theta_{\varepsilon},\tau_{\varepsilon}\right),N^{t_{\varepsilon}}\left(\hat{\gamma}_{\varepsilon},\theta_{\varepsilon},\tau_{\varepsilon}\right)\right)=0.

Then, multiplying
M^{t_{\varepsilon},j}_{\varepsilon}
by
\partial_{\gamma_{i}}E^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{\varepsilon},\tau_{\varepsilon}}
, subtracting it from
L^{t_{\varepsilon},i}_{\varepsilon}
for
j=1,\dots,k
and using Proposition
3.4
, we obtain (upon multiplication by
2\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}
)

E^{\left(i\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{\varepsilon},\tau_{\varepsilon}}=\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{3}}\right),\quad\text{for }i=1,\dots,k.

(4.120)

Plugging (
4.120
) into the equation for
M^{t_{\varepsilon},i}_{\varepsilon}
, we then obtain

\theta_{\varepsilon}=\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{4}}\right),

(4.121)

hence
\theta_{\varepsilon}\not\in\partial\Theta^{k}_{\varepsilon}
. Now (
4.121
), the equation for
L^{t_{\varepsilon},i}_{\varepsilon}
and Proposition
3.4
yield

\sum_{j=1}^{k}\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\big[E^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{\varepsilon},\tau_{\varepsilon}}\big]E^{\left(j\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{\varepsilon},\tau_{\varepsilon}}=\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}\ln^{2}\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{5}}\right),\quad\text{for }i=1,\dots,k.

(4.122)

We can rewrite (
4.122
) as

Q_{\varepsilon}E_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{\varepsilon},\tau_{\varepsilon}}=\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{3}}\right),

(4.123)

where, taking Proposition
3.4
into account,
Q_{\varepsilon}=\left(Q_{\varepsilon,ij}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq k}
is a
k\times k
matrix with
Q_{\varepsilon,ij}=\mathcal{Q}_{ij}+\smallo\left(1\right)
as
\varepsilon\to 0
and

\mathcal{Q}=\left(\mathcal{Q}_{ij}\right)_{1\leq i,j\leq k}:=\begin{pmatrix}1&1/l&\dots&1/l\\
1/l&1&\dots&1/l\\
\vdots&&\ddots&\vdots\\
1/l&1/l&\dots&1\end{pmatrix},\quad E_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{\varepsilon},\tau_{\varepsilon}}=\begin{pmatrix}E_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{\varepsilon},\tau_{\varepsilon}}^{\left(1\right)}\\
\vdots\\
E_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{\varepsilon},\tau_{\varepsilon}}^{(k)}\end{pmatrix}

(4.124)

Now, since

\det Q_{\varepsilon}=\det\mathcal{Q}+\smallo\left(1\right)=\left(1+\frac{k-1}{l}\right)\left(1-\frac{1}{l}\right)^{k-1}+\smallo\left(1\right)>0

for
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small, uniformly with respect to
(\gamma,\theta,\tau)\in P^{k}_{\varepsilon}\left(\delta\right)
, we can invert
Q_{\varepsilon}
in (
4.123
) and get

E_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{\varepsilon},\tau_{\varepsilon}}^{\left(i\right)}=\smallo\left(\frac{\delta_{\varepsilon}\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{3}}\right),\quad\text{for }i=1,\dots,k.

(4.125)

On the other hand, still by Proposition
3.4
, we have

E_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{\varepsilon},\tau_{\varepsilon}}=-2\frac{\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}Q_{\varepsilon}\hat{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}+\smallo\left(\left|\hat{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\right|\frac{\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right),

where we recall that
\hat{\gamma}=\gamma-\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\left(\tau\right)
.
Then, inverting
Q_{\varepsilon}
and using (
4.125
) we end up with

\hat{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}=\smallo\left(\delta_{\varepsilon}/\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\right),

which, for
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small implies that
\hat{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\not\in\partial\overline{\Gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{k}
.

Finally, writing
\hat{\tau}_{\varepsilon}:=\tau_{\varepsilon}/d_{\varepsilon}
, we have

N^{i}\left(\hat{\tau}_{\varepsilon}\right)=\smallo\left(1\right)
,
where
N=(N^{1},\dots,N^{k})
is as in (
4.130
). On the other hand,
\tau_{\varepsilon}\in\partial T^{k}_{\varepsilon}\left(\delta\right)
implies
\hat{\tau}_{\varepsilon}\in\partial\widehat{T}^{k}\left(\delta\right)
, where

\widehat{T}^{k}\left(\delta\right):=\Big\{y=\left(y_{1},\dotsc,y_{k}\right)\in\mathbb{R}^{k}:\,-\frac{k}{\delta}<y_{1}<y_{2}<\dots<y_{k}<\frac{k}{\delta}\\
\text{and }|y_{i}-y_{j}|>\delta,\,\forall i,j\in\left\{1,\dotsc,k\right\},\,i\neq j\Big\},

(4.126)

which is compact and this contradicts Lemma
4.7
for
\delta=\delta\left(a_{0},l,k\right)>0
sufficiently small such that
y^{*}\in\widehat{T}^{k}(\delta)
. Then we also have
\tau_{\varepsilon}\not\in\partial T^{k}_{\varepsilon}\left(\delta\right)
, which contradicts (
4.119
).

We have therefore proven that
(L^{t}_{\varepsilon},M^{t}_{\varepsilon},N^{t}_{\varepsilon})\neq 0
on
\partial\widehat{P}^{k}_{\varepsilon}\left(\delta\right)
, for
\varepsilon>0
sufficiently small, hence by homotopy invariance of the degree

\deg\big(\left(L_{\varepsilon},M_{\varepsilon},N_{\varepsilon}\right),\widehat{P}^{k}_{\varepsilon}\left(\delta\right),0\big)=\deg\big(\left(\overline{L}_{\varepsilon},\overline{M}_{\varepsilon},\overline{N}_{\varepsilon}\right),\widehat{P}^{k}_{\varepsilon}\left(\delta\right),0\big).

(4.127)

The degree of
\left(\overline{L}_{\varepsilon},\overline{M}_{\varepsilon},\overline{N}_{\varepsilon}\right)
does not change upon multiplication by an invertible matrix with determinant
1
, namely if we consider

\begin{pmatrix}\widetilde{L}_{\varepsilon}\\
\overline{M}_{\varepsilon}\\
\overline{N}_{\varepsilon}\\
\end{pmatrix}:=\begin{pmatrix}I_{k}&-D_{\gamma}\left[E_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right]&0\\
0&I_{k}&0\\
0&0&I_{k}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\overline{L}_{\varepsilon}\\
\overline{M}_{\varepsilon}\\
\overline{N}_{\varepsilon}\end{pmatrix},

where
D_{\gamma}\left[E_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right]=\big(\partial_{\gamma_{j}}\big[E^{\left(i\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big]\big)_{1\leq i,j\leq k}
,
I_{k}
is the
k\times k
identity matrix and
\widetilde{L}_{\varepsilon}:\widehat{P}^{k}_{\varepsilon}\left(\delta\right)\to\mathbb{R}^{k}
, is defined by

\widetilde{L}_{\varepsilon}^{i}=-E^{\left(i\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}
,
for
i=1,\dots k
, we get

\deg\big(\left(\overline{L}_{\varepsilon},\overline{M}_{\varepsilon},\overline{N}_{\varepsilon}\right),\widehat{P}^{k}_{\varepsilon}\left(\delta\right),0\big)=\deg\big(\big(\widetilde{L}_{\varepsilon},\overline{M}_{\varepsilon},\overline{N}_{\varepsilon}\big),\widehat{P}^{k}_{\varepsilon}\left(\delta\right),0\big).

Expanding
E^{\left(i\right)}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}
as in Proposition
3.4
, we do a final homotopy between
\big(\widetilde{L}_{\varepsilon},\overline{M}_{\varepsilon}\big)
and
\big(L_{\varepsilon}^{*},M_{\varepsilon}^{*}\big):\widehat{P}^{k}_{\varepsilon}\left(\delta\right)\to\mathbb{R}^{2k}
, where

L_{\varepsilon}^{*i}=-\frac{2\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\left(\hat{\gamma}_{i}+\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{\hat{\gamma}_{j}}{l}\right),\quad M_{\varepsilon}^{*i}=L_{\varepsilon}^{*i}+\theta_{i}\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}

for
i=1,\dots k
(with the same method as above to prevent zeroes on
\partial\widehat{P}^{k}_{\varepsilon}\left(\delta\right)
), so that

\deg\big(\big(\widetilde{L}_{\varepsilon},\overline{M}_{\varepsilon},\overline{N}_{\varepsilon}\big),\widehat{P}^{k}_{\varepsilon}\left(\delta\right),0\big)=\deg\big(\left(L^{*}_{\varepsilon},M^{*}_{\varepsilon},\overline{N}_{\varepsilon}\right),\widehat{P}^{k}_{\varepsilon}\left(\delta\right),0\big)

Using the matrix
\mathcal{Q}
defined in (
4.124
), we see that

\begin{pmatrix}L_{\varepsilon}^{*}\\
M_{\varepsilon}^{*}\\
\overline{N}_{\varepsilon}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}-2\frac{\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\mathcal{Q}&0&0\\
-2\frac{\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\mathcal{Q}&\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}I_{k}&0\\
0&0&I_{k}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\hat{\gamma}\\
\theta\\
\overline{N}_{\varepsilon}\end{pmatrix}.

(4.128)

Since
\mathcal{Q}
has positive determinant, if we call
\mathcal{A}
the
3k\times 3k
matrix on the right-hand side of (
4.128
) we have
\sign\left(\det\mathcal{A}\right)=(-1)^{k}
, and noticing that
\overline{N}_{\varepsilon}
only depends on
\tau
, we obtain

\mathcal{A}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}L_{\varepsilon}^{*}\\
M_{\varepsilon}^{*}\\
\overline{N}_{\varepsilon}\end{pmatrix}=\mathrm{Id}\times\mathrm{Id}\times\overline{N}_{\varepsilon}:\widehat{\Gamma}^{k}_{\varepsilon}\times\Theta^{k}_{\varepsilon}\times T^{k}_{\varepsilon}\left(\delta\right)\to\mathbb{R}^{k}\times\mathbb{R}^{k}\times\mathbb{R}^{k},

and using the product formula for the degree, we finally obtain

\displaystyle\deg\big(\left(L_{\varepsilon},M_{\varepsilon},N_{\varepsilon}\right),\widehat{P}^{k}_{\varepsilon}\left(\delta\right),0\big)=\deg\big(\left(L^{*}_{\varepsilon,},M^{*}_{\varepsilon},\overline{N}_{\varepsilon}\right),\widehat{P}^{k}_{\varepsilon}\left(\delta\right),0\big)

\displaystyle\qquad=(-1)^{k}\deg\big(\text{Id},\widehat{\Gamma}^{k}_{\varepsilon},0\big)\deg\left(\text{Id},\Theta^{k}_{\varepsilon},0\right)\deg\left(\overline{N}_{\varepsilon},T^{k}_{\varepsilon}\left(\delta\right),0\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=(-1)^{k}\deg\left(\overline{N}_{\varepsilon},T^{k}_{\varepsilon}\left(\delta\right),0\right).

(4.129)

In order to compute the degree of
\overline{N}_{\varepsilon}
, observe that
\overline{N}_{\varepsilon}^{i}\left(\tau\right)=N^{i}\left(\hat{\tau}_{\varepsilon}\right)
, where
\hat{\tau}_{\varepsilon}=\tau/d_{\varepsilon}
and
N=\left(N^{1},\dotsc,N^{k}\right)
is as in (
4.130
).
Moreover, since
\delta\in(0,1)
was chosen such that
y^{*}\in\widehat{T}^{k}(\delta)
, with
y^{*}
as in Lemma
4.7
, it follows that

\deg\big(\overline{N}_{\varepsilon},T^{k}_{\varepsilon}\left(\delta\right),0\big)=\deg\big(N,\widehat{T}^{k}\left(\delta\right),0\big)=1.

We then conclude with (
4
) that there exists
\left(\hat{\gamma}_{\varepsilon},\theta_{\varepsilon},\tau_{\varepsilon}\right)\in\widehat{P}^{k}_{\varepsilon}\left(\delta\right)
such that
\left(\gamma_{\varepsilon},\theta_{\varepsilon},\tau_{\varepsilon}\right)=\left(\hat{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}+\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\left(\tau\right),\theta_{\varepsilon},\tau_{\varepsilon}\right)\in P^{k}_{\varepsilon}\left(\delta\right)
solves (
4.117
).
∎

Lemma 4.7
.

The function

N:\widehat{T}^{k}(0):=\left\{y=\left(y_{1},\dotsc,y_{k}\right)\in\mathbb{R}^{k}:\,y_{1}<y_{2}<\dots<y_{k}\right\}\to\mathbb{R}^{k}

given by

N^{i}\left(y_{1},\dots,y_{k}\right):=a_{0}ly_{i}^{l-1}-2\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{1}{y_{i}-y_{j}},\quad\text{for }i=1,\dots,k,

(4.130)

has exactly one zero, which we call
y^{*}
. Moreover
\deg(H,\widehat{T}^{k}(0),0)=1
.

Proof.

We have that
N=\nabla J
, with

J\left(y\right)=a_{0}\sum_{i=1}^{k}y_{i}^{l}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i\neq j}\ln\frac{1}{\left(y_{i}-y_{j}\right)^{2}},\quad\forall y=\left(y_{1},\dots,y_{k}\right)\in\widehat{T}^{k}\left(0\right).

The Hessian
\nabla^{2}J
is positive definite on
\widehat{T}^{k}\left(0\right)
, since

\displaystyle\partial_{y_{i}}^{2}J

\displaystyle=\partial_{y_{i}}N^{i}=a_{0}l(l-1)y_{i}^{l-2}+\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{2}{\left(y_{i}-y_{j}\right)^{2}},

\displaystyle\partial_{y_{i}}\partial_{y_{j}}J

\displaystyle=\partial_{y_{i}}N^{j}=-\frac{2}{\left(y_{i}-y_{j}\right)^{2}},\quad\text{for }i\neq j,

so that for every
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{k}\setminus\{0\}
, using that
\xi_{i}^{2}+\xi_{j}^{2}\geq 2\xi_{i}\xi_{j}
, we get

\displaystyle\xi^{T}\nabla^{2}J\xi

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\xi_{i}^{2}\left(a_{0}l\left(l-1\right)y_{i}^{l-2}+\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{2}{\left(y_{i}-y_{j}\right)^{2}}\right)-\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{2\xi_{j}\xi_{i}}{\left(y_{i}-y_{j}\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i=1}^{k}\xi_{i}^{2}a_{0}l\left(l-1\right)y_{i}^{l-2},

and, using that
y\in\widehat{T}^{k}\left(0\right)
and
l\in 2\mathbb{N}^{*}
, the right-hand side is positive, unless
\xi=\left(0,\dots,\xi_{i_{0}},\dots 0\right)
and
y_{i_{0}}=0
for some
i_{0}\in\left\{1,\dotsc,k\right\}
, in which case

\xi^{T}\nabla^{2}J\xi=\sum_{j\neq i_{0}}\frac{2\xi_{i_{0}}^{2}}{(y_{i_{0}}-y_{j})^{2}}>0.

Then
J
is strictly convex in
\widehat{T}^{k}(0)
and since
|J(y)|\to\infty
as
y\to\partial\widehat{T}^{k}(0)
or
|y|\to\infty
,
J
has a minimum
y^{*}
, which is its only critical point and the only zero of
N
. Moreover
\det(\nabla N(y^{*}))=\det(\nabla^{2}J(y^{*}))>0
, hence
\deg(N,\widehat{T}^{k}(0),0)=1
.
∎

Finally, we can now conclude the proof of Theorems
1.2
and
1.3
.

End of proof of Theorems
1.2
and
1.3
.

It follows from Proposition
4.4
that for small
\varepsilon>0
,
u_{\varepsilon}:=U_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{\varepsilon},\tau_{\varepsilon},\theta_{\varepsilon}}+\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{\varepsilon},\tau_{\varepsilon},\theta_{\varepsilon}}\in E_{h_{\varepsilon},\beta_{\varepsilon}}
, where
\beta_{\varepsilon}:=\big\|\nabla u_{\varepsilon}\big\|_{L^{2}}^{2}
. We denote
\gamma_{\varepsilon}=\left(\gamma_{1,\varepsilon},\dotsc,\gamma_{k,\varepsilon}\right)
,
\tau_{\varepsilon}=\left(\tau_{1,\varepsilon},\dotsc,\tau_{k,\varepsilon}\right)
,
\theta_{\varepsilon}=\left(\theta_{1,\varepsilon},\dotsc,\theta_{k,\varepsilon}\right)
.
By using (
3.14
) and (
4.16
), we obtain that
\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{\varepsilon},\tau_{\varepsilon}},\Phi_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{\varepsilon},\tau_{\varepsilon},\theta_{\varepsilon}}\to 0
in
H^{1}_{0}\left(\Omega\right)
as
\varepsilon\to 0
. Since moreover
w_{\varepsilon}\to w_{0}
in
C^{1}\left(\overline{\Omega}\right)
,
H\in C^{1}\left(\overline{\Omega}\times\overline{\Omega}\right)
,
\theta_{i,\varepsilon}\to 0
and
A_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i,\varepsilon},\tau_{i,\varepsilon}}\to 0
for all
i\in\left\{1,\dotsc,k\right\}
, we obtain

\big\|\nabla{u}_{\varepsilon}\big\|_{L^{2}}=\Big\|\nabla w_{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\left(1+\theta_{i,\varepsilon}\right)\big(\nabla\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i,\varepsilon},\overline{\tau_{i,\varepsilon}}}\mathbf{1}_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i,\varepsilon}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\\
+A_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i,\varepsilon},\tau_{i,\varepsilon}}\nabla G\left(\cdot,\overline{\tau_{i,\varepsilon}}\right)\mathbf{1}_{\Omega\backslash B\left(\overline{\tau_{i,\varepsilon}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\big)\Big\|_{L^{2}}+\smallo\left(1\right)

(4.131)

as
\varepsilon\to 0
. By integrating by parts, we obtain

\left\|\nabla G\left(\cdot,\overline{\tau_{i,\varepsilon}}\right)\mathbf{1}_{\Omega\backslash B\left(\overline{\tau_{i,\varepsilon}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=-\int_{\partial B\left(\overline{\tau_{i,\varepsilon}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}G\left(\cdot,\overline{\tau_{i,\varepsilon}}\right)\partial_{\nu}G\left(\cdot,\overline{\tau_{i,\varepsilon}}\right)d\sigma\sim\frac{1}{2\pi}\ln\frac{1}{r_{\varepsilon}}\sim\frac{\delta_{0}\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}{4\pi},

(4.132)

\displaystyle\left<\nabla G\left(\cdot,\overline{\tau_{i,\varepsilon}}\right)\mathbf{1}_{\Omega\backslash B\left(\overline{\tau_{i,\varepsilon}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)},\nabla w_{0}\right>_{L^{2}}=\int_{\partial B\left(\overline{\tau_{i,\varepsilon}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}w_{0}\partial_{\nu}G\left(\cdot,\overline{\tau_{i,\varepsilon}}\right)d\sigma

\displaystyle=\bigO\big(\left\|w_{0}\right\|_{C^{0}\left(\partial B\left(\overline{\tau_{i,\varepsilon}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)\right)}\big)

\displaystyle=\smallo\left(1\right)

(4.133)

and

\Big<\nabla G\left(\cdot,\overline{\tau_{i,\varepsilon}}\right)\mathbf{1}_{\Omega\backslash B\left(\overline{\tau_{i,\varepsilon}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)},\nabla G\left(\cdot,\overline{\tau_{j,\varepsilon}}\right)\mathbf{1}_{\Omega\backslash B\left(\overline{\tau_{j,\varepsilon}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\Big>_{L^{2}}\\
=\int_{\partial B\left(\overline{\tau_{i,\varepsilon}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)\cup\partial B\left(\overline{\tau_{j,\varepsilon}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}G\left(\cdot,\overline{\tau_{i,\varepsilon}}\right)\partial_{\nu}G\left(\cdot,\overline{\tau_{j,\varepsilon}}\right)d\sigma=\bigO\left(\ln\frac{1}{d_{\varepsilon}}\right)=\bigO\left(\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\right)

(4.134)

as
\varepsilon\to 0
for all
i,j\in\left\{1,\dotsc,k\right\}
,
i\neq j
, where
\nu
and
d\sigma
are the outward unit normal vector and volume element of
\partial B\left(\overline{\tau_{i,\varepsilon}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)\cup\partial B\left(\overline{\tau_{j,\varepsilon}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)
, respectively. On the other hand, since
\gamma_{i,\varepsilon}\sim\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}
, by using (
6.2
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i,\varepsilon},\overline{\tau_{i,\varepsilon}}}\mathbf{1}_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i,\varepsilon}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=2\pi\int_{0}^{\sqrt{\lambda_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{\tau_{i,\varepsilon}}\right)}r_{\varepsilon}}\big(\overline{B}_{\gamma_{i,\varepsilon}}^{\prime}\left(r\right)\big)^{2}r\,dr

\displaystyle\sim\frac{8\pi}{\gamma_{i,\varepsilon}^{2}}\ln\left(\frac{\sqrt{\lambda_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{\tau_{i,\varepsilon}}\right)}r_{\varepsilon}}{\mu_{i,\varepsilon}}\right)\sim 4\pi\left(1-\delta_{0}\right)

(4.135)

for all
i\in\left\{1,\dotsc,k\right\}
, where
\mu_{i,\varepsilon}
is defined by
\mu_{i,\varepsilon}^{2}:=4\gamma_{i,\varepsilon}^{-2}\exp\left(-\gamma_{i,\varepsilon}^{2}\right)
. For every
j\neq i
, by remarking that
B\left(\overline{\tau_{i,\varepsilon}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)\cap B\left(\overline{\tau_{j,\varepsilon}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)=\emptyset
for small
\varepsilon>0
and

\left\|A_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j,\varepsilon},\tau_{j,\varepsilon}}\nabla G\left(\cdot,\overline{\tau_{j,\varepsilon}}\right)\mathbf{1}_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i,\varepsilon}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\right\|_{C^{0}}=\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}d_{\varepsilon}}\right)=\smallo\left(1\right),

we obtain

\Big<\nabla\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i,\varepsilon},\overline{\tau_{i,\varepsilon}}}\mathbf{1}_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i,\varepsilon}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)},\nabla w_{0}+\sum_{j\neq i}\left(1+\theta_{i,\varepsilon}\right)\big(\nabla\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i,\varepsilon},\overline{\tau_{i,\varepsilon}}}\mathbf{1}_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i,\varepsilon}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}+A_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i,\varepsilon},\tau_{i,\varepsilon}}\nabla G\left(\cdot,\overline{\tau_{i,\varepsilon}}\right)\\
\times\mathbf{1}_{\Omega\backslash B\left(\overline{\tau_{i,\varepsilon}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\big)\Big>_{L^{2}}=\bigO\left(\left\|\nabla\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i,\varepsilon},\overline{\tau_{i,\varepsilon}}}\mathbf{1}_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i,\varepsilon}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\right\|_{L^{1}}\right)=\bigO\left(\frac{r_{\varepsilon}}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}\right)=\smallo\left(1\right)

(4.136)

as
\varepsilon\to 0
. Since moreover
\theta_{\varepsilon}\to 0
, it follows from (
3.10
) and (
4.131
)–(
4.136
) that

\big\|\nabla{u}_{\varepsilon}\big\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\longrightarrow\left\|\nabla w_{0}\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+4k\pi=\beta_{0}+4k\pi

as
\varepsilon\to 0
. Standard elliptic theory gives that
\big\|{u}_{\varepsilon}\big\|_{L^{\infty}}\to\infty
as
\varepsilon\to 0
. Since
h_{\varepsilon}\to h_{0}
in
C^{2}\left(\overline{\Omega}\right)
, we then obtain that
\beta_{0}+4k\pi
is an unstable energy level of
I_{h_{0}}
. This ends the proof of Theorem
1.2
.

The above construction also proves Theorem
1.3
if
w_{0}
is non-degenerate. Otherwise we apply a diagonal procedure. More precisely, thanks to Proposition
2.3
, for
\kappa\in(0,\kappa_{0})
and
\varepsilon=\varepsilon(\kappa)
sufficiently small we construct
w_{\kappa,\varepsilon(\kappa)}\in E_{\beta_{\kappa,\varepsilon(\kappa)},h_{\kappa,\varepsilon(\kappa)}}
, with
w_{\kappa,\varepsilon(\kappa)}\to w_{0}
,
h_{\kappa,\varepsilon(\kappa)}\to h
in
C^{2}(\overline{\Omega})
and
\beta^{\prime}_{\kappa}:=\beta_{\kappa,\varepsilon(\kappa)}\to\beta_{0}
as
\kappa\to 0
; we further construct

u_{\kappa}=w_{\kappa}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}(1+\theta_{\kappa,i})B_{\kappa,\gamma_{\kappa,i},\tau_{\kappa,i}}+\Psi_{\kappa,\gamma_{\kappa},\tau_{\kappa}}+\Phi_{\kappa,\gamma_{\kappa},\theta_{\kappa},\tau_{\kappa}}\in E_{h_{\kappa},\beta_{\kappa}},

where each subscript
\kappa
on the right-hand side actually means
(\kappa,\varepsilon(\kappa))
, with
\varepsilon(\kappa)>0
sufficiently small so that

\|\nabla\Psi_{\kappa,\varepsilon(\kappa),\gamma_{\kappa,\varepsilon(\kappa)},\tau_{\kappa,\varepsilon(\kappa)}}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\Phi_{\kappa,\varepsilon(\kappa),\gamma_{\kappa,\varepsilon(\kappa)},\theta_{\kappa,\varepsilon(\kappa)},\tau_{\kappa,\varepsilon(\kappa)}}\|_{L^{2}}=\smallo(1)\quad\text{as }\kappa\to 0.

Up to renaming the indices, we conclude.
∎

Remark 4.8

(Stable vs. positively stable energy levels)
.

As in Definition
1.1
, let
(u_{\varepsilon})
be a family of functions such that
u_{\varepsilon}\in E_{h_{\varepsilon},\beta_{\varepsilon}}
with
h_{\varepsilon}\to h>0
in
C^{2}(\bar{\Omega})
and
\beta_{\varepsilon}\to\beta>0
. In particular,
u_{\varepsilon}
solves
(\mathcal{E}_{h_{\varepsilon},\beta_{\varepsilon}})
with
\lambda=\lambda_{\varepsilon}>0
obtained from
h_{\varepsilon}
,
\beta_{\varepsilon}
and
u_{\varepsilon}
thanks to (
1.1
). As a simple claim, testing
(\mathcal{E}_{h_{\varepsilon},\beta_{\varepsilon}})
against
v>0
, first eigenfuntion of
\Delta
with zero Dirichlet condition on
\partial\Omega
, the bound
\lambda_{\varepsilon}=O(1)
is automatic when defining a
positively
stable energy level in Definition
1.1
. In the sign changing case, however, let us consider the following unstable situation:
u_{\varepsilon}
goes uniformly to
0\not\in E_{h,\beta}
, while looking like a (
h
-weighted) Dirichlet eigenfunction associated to some large eigenvalue
\bar{\lambda}_{\varepsilon}\sim\lambda_{\varepsilon}\to+\infty
, but still having the given energy
\beta_{\varepsilon}\sim\beta>0
as
\varepsilon\to 0
. Then, in order not to have an empty notion of
stable energy level
, we further assume the bound
\lambda_{\varepsilon}=O(1)
in Definition
1.1
.

5.
Proof of Proposition
3.2

We fix
\varepsilon\in\left(0,1\right)
and
\delta^{\prime}\in\left(0,1-\sqrt{2\delta_{0}}\right)
. For every
p>1
, we define
\mathcal{W}^{2,p}_{0}\left(\Omega\right):=W^{2,p}\left(\Omega\right)\cap H^{1}_{0}\left(\Omega\right)
. Note that we have a compact embedding of
W^{2,p}\left(\Omega\right)
into
H^{1}\left(\Omega\right)
and
C^{0}\left(\overline{\Omega}\right)
when
p>1
and into
C^{1}\left(\overline{\Omega}\right)
when
p>2
. For every
\varepsilon\in\left(0,\varepsilon_{0}\right)
and
\tau\in T^{k}_{\varepsilon}\left(\delta\right)
, we let
L_{\varepsilon,\tau}:\mathcal{W}^{2,p}_{0}\left(\Omega\right)\to\mathcal{W}^{2,p}_{0}\left(\Omega\right)
be the operator defined as

L_{\varepsilon,\tau}\left(\Psi\right)=\Psi-\Delta^{-1}\left[\lambda_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon}\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}f^{\prime}\left(w_{\varepsilon}\right)\Psi\right]\qquad\forall\Psi\in\mathcal{W}^{2,p}_{0}\left(\Omega\right).

(5.1)

As a first step, we prove that there exists a constant
C=C\left(p,\delta\right)>0
such that

\left\|\Psi\right\|_{W^{2,p}}\leq C\left\|L_{\varepsilon,\tau}\left(\Psi\right)\right\|_{W^{2,p}}\qquad\forall\Psi\in\mathcal{W}^{2,p}_{0}\left(\Omega\right)

(5.2)

so that in particular
L_{\varepsilon,\tau}
is an isomorphism. We assume by contradiction that there exist sequences
\left(\varepsilon_{n},\tau_{n},\Psi_{n}\right)_{n}
such that
\varepsilon_{n}\to 0
,
\tau_{n}\in T_{\varepsilon_{n}}^{k}\left(\delta\right)
,
\Psi_{n}\in\mathcal{W}^{2,p}_{0}\left(\Omega\right)
and

\left\|\Psi_{n}\right\|_{W^{2,p}}=1\quad\text{and}\quad\left\|L_{\varepsilon_{n},\tau_{n}}\left(\Psi_{n}\right)\right\|_{W^{2,p}}\to 0

(5.3)

as
n\to\infty
. In particular, we obtain that
\left(\Psi_{n}\right)_{n}
converges, up to a subsequence, weakly in
W^{2,p}\left(\Omega\right)
and strongly in
H^{1}_{0}\left(\Omega\right)
and
C^{0}\left(\overline{\Omega}\right)
to a function
\Psi_{0}
. By using the second part of (
5.3
), we obtain

\int_{\Omega}\left<\nabla\Psi_{n},\nabla\phi\right>dx-\lambda_{\varepsilon_{n}}\int_{\Omega}h_{\varepsilon_{n}}\chi_{\varepsilon_{n},\tau_{n}}f^{\prime}\left(u_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right)\Psi_{n}\phi\,dx=\smallo\left(1\right)

(5.4)

for all
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}\left(\Omega\right)
. Since
\lambda_{\varepsilon_{n}}h_{\varepsilon_{n}}\chi_{\varepsilon_{n},\tau_{n}}f^{\prime}\left(u_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right)
is uniformly bounded and converges pointwise to
\lambda_{0}h_{0}f^{\prime}\left(u_{0}\right)
in
\Omega
and
\Psi_{n}\to\Psi_{0}
in
H^{1}_{0}\left(\Omega\right)
and
C^{0}\left(\overline{\Omega}\right)
, by passing to the limit into (
5.4
), we obtain that
\Psi_{0}
is a solution of the problem

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\Delta\Psi_{0}=\lambda_{0}h_{0}f^{\prime}\left(u_{0}\right)\Psi_{0}&&\text{in }\Omega\\
&\Psi_{0}=0&&\text{on }\partial\Omega.\end{aligned}\right.

Since
u_{0}
is non-degenerate, it follows that
\Psi_{0}\equiv 0
. By using (
5.3
) together with standard
L^{p}
-estimates for the Dirichlet problem (see Lemma 9.17 of Gilbarg–Trudinger
[
GilTru
]
), we then obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\Psi_{n}\right\|_{W^{2,p}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|L_{\varepsilon_{n},\tau_{n}}\left(\Psi_{n}\right)\right\|_{W^{2,p}}+\left\|\Delta^{-1}\left[\lambda_{\varepsilon_{n}}h_{\varepsilon_{n}}\chi_{\varepsilon_{n},\tau_{n}}f^{\prime}\left(u_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right)\Psi_{n}\right]\right\|_{W^{2,p}}

\displaystyle=\smallo\left(1\right)+\bigO\left(\left\|\lambda_{\varepsilon_{n}}h_{\varepsilon_{n}}\chi_{\varepsilon_{n},\tau_{n}}f^{\prime}\left(u_{\varepsilon_{n}}\right)\Psi_{n}\right\|_{L^{p}}\right)=\smallo\left(1\right)

as
n\to\infty
, which is in contradiction with (
5.3
). This ends the proof of (
5.2
).

Now, for every
\varepsilon\in\left(0,\varepsilon_{0}\right)
and
\left(\gamma,\tau\right)\in T^{k}_{\varepsilon}\left(\delta\right)\times\Gamma_{\varepsilon}^{k}\left(\delta^{\prime}\right)
, we let
N_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau},T_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}:\mathcal{W}^{2,p}_{0}\left(\Omega\right)\to\mathcal{W}^{2,p}_{0}\left(\Omega\right)
be the operators defined as

\displaystyle N_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\left(\Psi\right)

\displaystyle:=\Delta^{-1}\big[\lambda_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon}\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}\big(f\big(\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}+\Psi\big)-f\left(w_{\varepsilon}\right)-f^{\prime}\left(w_{\varepsilon}\right)\Psi\big)\big],

\displaystyle T_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\left(\Psi\right)

\displaystyle:=L^{-1}_{\varepsilon,\tau}\left(N_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\left(\Psi\right)-R_{\varepsilon,\tau}\right)

for all
\Psi\in\mathcal{W}^{2,p}_{0}\left(\Omega\right)
, where

R_{\varepsilon,\tau}:=w_{\varepsilon}-\Delta^{-1}\left[\lambda_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon}\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}f\left(w_{\varepsilon}\right)\right]=\Delta^{-1}\left[\lambda_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon}(1-\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau})f\left(w_{\varepsilon}\right)\right].

Note that the problem (
3.13
) can be rewritten as the fixed point equation
T_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\left(\Psi\right)=\Psi
. For every
C>0
and
\varepsilon\in\left(0,\varepsilon_{0}\right)
, we define

V_{\varepsilon}\left(C\right):=\left\{\Psi\in\mathcal{W}^{2,p}_{0}\left(\Omega\right):\,\left\|\Psi\right\|_{W^{2,p}}\leq C/\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\right\}.

We will prove that if
C
is chosen large enough, then
T_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}
has a fixed point in
V_{\varepsilon}\left(C\right)
for small
\varepsilon>0
. By using a standard
L^{p}
-estimate and since
\lambda_{\varepsilon}\to\lambda_{0}
,
h_{\varepsilon}\to h_{0}
and
w_{\varepsilon}\to w_{0}
in
C^{0}\left(\overline{\Omega}\right)
, we obtain

\left\|R_{\varepsilon,\tau}\right\|_{W^{2,p}}=\bigO\left(\left\|\lambda_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon}\left(1-\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}\right)f\left(w_{\varepsilon}\right)\right\|_{L^{p}}\right)=\bigO\left(\left\|1-\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}\right\|_{L^{p}}\right)=\smallo\left(1/\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\right)

(5.5)

as
\varepsilon\to 0
, uniformly in
\tau\in T_{\varepsilon}^{k}\left(\delta\right)
. Similarly, for every
\Psi,\Psi_{1},\Psi_{2}\in V_{\varepsilon}\left(C\right)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|N_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\left(\Psi\right)\right\|_{W^{2,p}}=\bigO\big(\big\|\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}\big(f\big(\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}+\Psi\big)-f\left(w_{\varepsilon}\right)-f^{\prime}\left(w_{\varepsilon}\right)\Psi\big)\big\|_{L^{p}}\big),

(5.6)

\displaystyle\left\|N_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\left(\Psi_{1}\right)-N_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\left(\Psi_{2}\right)\right\|_{W^{2,p}}=\bigO\big(\big\|\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}\big(f\big(\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}+\Psi_{1}\big)-f\big(\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}+\Psi_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 250.0pt-f^{\prime}\left(w_{\varepsilon}\right)\left(\Psi_{1}-\Psi_{2}\right)\big)\big\|_{L^{p}}\big).

(5.7)

By applying the mean value theorem together with Hölder’s inequality, it follows from (
5.6
) and (
5.7
) that

\displaystyle\left\|N_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\left(\Psi\right)\right\|_{W^{2,p}}=\bigO\bigg(\bigg\|\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}f^{\prime}\Big(w_{\varepsilon}+t_{1}\sum_{i=1}^{k}B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}+\Psi\Big)\sum_{i=1}^{k}B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}\bigg\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\hskip 214.0pt+\left\|\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}f^{\prime\prime}\left(w_{\varepsilon}+s_{1}\Psi\right)\right\|_{L^{p}}\left\|\Psi\right\|_{C^{0}}^{2}\bigg),

(5.8)

\displaystyle\left\|N_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\left(\Psi_{1}\right)-N_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\left(\Psi_{2}\right)\right\|_{W^{2,p}}

\displaystyle\quad=\bigO\big(\big\|\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}\big(f^{\prime}\big(\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}+\left(1-s_{2}\right)\Psi_{1}+s_{2}\Psi_{2}\big)-f^{\prime}\left(w_{\varepsilon}\right)\big)\big\|_{L^{p}}\left\|\Psi_{1}-\Psi_{2}\right\|_{C^{0}}\big)

\displaystyle\quad=\bigO\bigg(\bigg\|\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}f^{\prime\prime}\Big(w_{\varepsilon}+t_{2}\sum_{i=1}^{k}B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}+t_{2}\left(1-s_{2}\right)\Psi_{1}+t_{2}s_{2}\Psi_{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{k}B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}+\left(1-s_{2}\right)\Psi_{1}+s_{2}\Psi_{2}\Big)\bigg\|_{L^{p}}\left\|\Psi_{1}-\Psi_{2}\right\|_{C^{0}}\bigg)

(5.9)

for some functions
