s_{1},s_{2},t_{1},t_{2}:\Omega\to\left[0,1\right]
. Since
0\leq\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}\leq 1
in
\Omega
,
w_{\varepsilon}\to w_{0}
in
C^{0}\left(\overline{\Omega}\right)
and
\Psi\in V_{\varepsilon}\left(C\right)
, we obtain

\left\|\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}f^{\prime\prime}\left(w_{\varepsilon}+s_{1}\Psi\right)\right\|_{L^{p}}=\bigO\left(1\right).

(5.10)

For every
j\in\left\{1,\dotsc,k\right\}
, by using (
3.10
), we obtain

B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{j},\tau_{j}}\left(x\right)=\frac{2}{\gamma_{j}}\left(\ln\frac{1}{\left|x-\overline{\tau_{j}}\right|}+\bigO\left(1\right)\right)

(5.11)

uniformly in
x\in\Omega\backslash B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)
. We let
R_{\varepsilon}:=\exp\left(-\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\right)\gg r_{\varepsilon}
. Since
\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}\equiv 0
in
B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)
,
0\leq\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}\leq 1
in
\Omega
,
w_{\varepsilon}\to w_{0}
in
C^{0}\left(\overline{\Omega}\right)
,
u_{0}\left(0\right)=0
,
\Psi,\Psi_{1},\Psi_{2}\in V_{\varepsilon}\left(C\right)
and
0\leq s_{1},s_{2},t_{1},t_{2}\leq 1
, it follows from (
5.11
) that

\displaystyle\bigg\|\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}f^{\prime}\Big(w_{\varepsilon}+t_{1}\sum_{i=1}^{k}B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}+\Psi\Big)\sum_{i=1}^{k}B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}\mathbf{1}_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},R_{\varepsilon}\right)}\bigg\|_{L^{p}}^{p}

\displaystyle\qquad=\bigO\left(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{p}\int_{r_{\varepsilon}}^{R_{\varepsilon}}f^{\prime}\left(\frac{2t_{1}}{\gamma_{j}}\ln\frac{1}{r}+\smallo\left(1\right)\right)^{p}rdr\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=\bigO\left(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{p+2}\int_{2\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}/\gamma_{j}^{2}}^{\delta_{0}\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}/\gamma_{j}^{2}}f^{\prime}\left(t_{1}\gamma_{j}s+\smallo\left(1\right)\right)^{p}\exp\left(-\gamma_{j}^{2}s\right)ds\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=\bigO\left(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{3p+2}\int_{2\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}/\gamma_{j}^{2}}^{\delta_{0}\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}/\gamma_{j}^{2}}\exp\left(\left(pt_{1}^{2}s-1\right)s\gamma_{j}^{2}+\smallo\left(\gamma_{j}\right)\right)ds\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=\bigO\left(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{3p+2}\int_{\delta_{0}/[\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\left(1+\delta^{\prime}\right)^{2}]}^{\delta_{0}/\left(1-\delta^{\prime}\right)^{2}}\exp\left(\left(pt_{1}^{2}s-1\right)s\gamma_{j}^{2}+\smallo\left(\gamma_{j}\right)\right)ds\right)=\smallo\left(1\right),

(5.12)

\displaystyle\bigg\|\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}f^{\prime\prime}\Big(w_{\varepsilon}+t_{2}\sum_{i=1}^{k}B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}+t_{2}\left(1-s_{2}\right)\Psi_{1}+t_{2}s_{2}\Psi_{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\times\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{k}B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}+\left(1-s_{2}\right)\Psi_{1}+s_{2}\Psi_{2}\Big)\mathbf{1}_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{j}},R_{\varepsilon}\right)}\bigg\|_{L^{p}}^{p}

\displaystyle\qquad=\bigO\left(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{p}\int_{r_{\varepsilon}}^{R_{\varepsilon}}f^{\prime\prime}\left(\frac{2t_{2}}{\gamma_{j}}\ln\frac{1}{r}+\smallo\left(1\right)\right)^{p}rdr\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=\bigO\left(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{p+2}\int_{2\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}/\gamma_{j}^{2}}^{\delta_{0}\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}/\gamma_{j}^{2}}f^{\prime\prime}\left(t_{2}\gamma_{j}s+\smallo\left(1\right)\right)^{p}\exp\left(-\gamma_{j}^{2}s\right)ds\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=\bigO\left(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{4p+2}\int_{2\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}/\gamma_{j}^{2}}^{\delta_{0}\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}/\gamma_{j}^{2}}\exp\left(\left(pt_{2}^{2}s-1\right)s\gamma_{j}^{2}+\smallo\left(\gamma_{j}\right)\right)ds\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=\bigO\left(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{4p+2}\int_{\delta_{0}/[\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\left(1+\delta^{\prime}\right)^{2}]}^{\delta_{0}/\left(1-\delta^{\prime}\right)^{2}}\exp\left(\left(pt_{2}^{2}s-1\right)s\gamma_{j}^{2}+\smallo\left(\gamma_{j}\right)\right)ds\right)=\smallo\left(1\right)

(5.13)

as
\varepsilon\to 0
, uniformly in
(\gamma,\tau)\in\Gamma_{\varepsilon}^{k}\left(\delta^{\prime}\right)\times T_{\varepsilon}^{k}\left(\delta\right)
and
\Psi,\Psi_{1},\Psi_{2}\in V_{\varepsilon}(C)
, provided we choose
p
such that
p\delta_{0}/\left(1-\delta^{\prime}\right)^{2}-1<0
, i.e.
p<\left(1-\delta^{\prime}\right)^{2}/\delta_{0}
. By using (
5.11
), we obtain

\bigg\|\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}f^{\prime}\Big(w_{\varepsilon}+t_{1}\sum_{i=1}^{k}B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}+\Psi\Big)\sum_{i=1}^{k}B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{R_{\varepsilon},\tau}}\bigg\|_{L^{p}}^{p}\\
=\bigO\bigg(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{p}}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\int_{\Omega_{R_{\varepsilon},\tau}}\left|\ln\left|x-\overline{\tau_{i}}\right|+\bigO\left(1\right)\right|^{p}dx\bigg)=\smallo\left(1\right)

(5.14)

and, similarly,

\bigg\|\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}f^{\prime\prime}\Big(w_{\varepsilon}+t_{2}\sum_{i=1}^{k}B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}+t_{2}\left(1-s_{2}\right)\Psi_{1}+t_{2}s_{2}\Psi_{2}\Big)\\
\times\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{k}B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}+\left(1-s_{2}\right)\Psi_{1}+s_{2}\Psi_{2}\Big)\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{R_{\varepsilon},\tau}}\bigg\|_{L^{p}}=\smallo\left(1\right)

(5.15)

as
\varepsilon\to 0
, uniformly in
(\gamma,\tau)\in\Gamma_{\varepsilon}^{k}\left(\delta^{\prime}\right)\times T_{\varepsilon}^{k}\left(\delta\right)
and
\Psi,\Psi_{1},\Psi_{2}\in V_{\varepsilon}(C)
.

Note that similar estimates as in (
5.12
)–(
5.14
) yield (
3.16
).

By putting together (
5.9
)–(
5.15
) and using the continuity of the embedding
W^{2,p}\left(\Omega\right)\hookrightarrow C^{0}\left(\overline{\Omega}\right)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|N_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\left(\Psi\right)\right\|_{W^{2,p}}

\displaystyle=\smallo\big(\left\|\Psi\right\|^{2}_{W^{2,p}}\big),

(5.16)

\displaystyle\left\|N_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\left(\Psi_{1}\right)-N_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\left(\Psi_{2}\right)\right\|_{W^{2,p}}

\displaystyle=\smallo\left(\left\|\Psi_{1}-\Psi_{2}\right\|_{W^{2,p}}\right)

(5.17)

as
\varepsilon\to 0
, uniformly in
(\gamma,\tau)\in\Gamma_{\varepsilon}^{k}\left(\delta^{\prime}\right)\times T_{\varepsilon}^{k}\left(\delta\right)
and
\Psi,\Psi_{1},\Psi_{2}\in V_{\varepsilon}(C)
. It follows from (
5.2
), (
5.5
), (
5.16
) and (
5.17
) that there exist
\varepsilon_{1}\left(p,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)\in\left(0,\varepsilon_{0}\right)
and
C=C\left(p,\delta,\delta^{\prime}\right)>0
(here we do not specify the dependence in
\delta_{0}
as this number is considered to be fixed) such that for every
\varepsilon\in\left(0,\varepsilon_{1}\left(\delta\right)\right)
and
(\gamma,\tau)\in\Gamma_{\varepsilon}^{k}\left(\delta^{\prime}\right)\times T_{\varepsilon}^{k}\left(\delta\right)
,
T_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}
is a contraction mapping on
V_{\varepsilon}\left(C\right)
. By the fixed point theorem, we then obtain that there exists a unique solution
\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\in V_{\varepsilon}\left(C\right)
to the problem (
3.13
). By fixing a number
p
such that
2<p<\left(1-\delta_{0}\right)^{2}/\delta_{0}
, the first inequality in (
3.14
) then follows from the continuity of the embedding
W^{2,p}\left(\Omega\right)\hookrightarrow C^{1}\left(\overline{\Omega}\right)
. By using the Moser–Trudinger inequality together with standard elliptic regularity theory, we obtain that
\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\in C^{l,\alpha}\left(\Omega\right)\cap C^{2}\left(\overline{\Omega}\right)
. Furthermore, by symmetry of
\Omega
,
w_{\varepsilon}
,
h_{\varepsilon}
,
\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}
and
\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}
, we obtain that
\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}
is even in
x_{2}
and by using the continuous differentiability of
\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}
and
\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}
in
\left(\gamma,\tau\right)
, we obtain that
\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}
is continuously differentiable in
\left(\gamma,\tau\right)
.

Now, we prove the second inequality in (
3.14
). For
i\in\left\{1,\dotsc,k\right\}
, by differentiating (
3.13
) in
\gamma_{i}
, we obtain

\Delta\left[L_{\varepsilon,\tau}\left(\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\left[\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right]\right)\right]=\lambda_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon}\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}f^{\prime}\big(\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}+\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big)\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\big[\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big]\\
+\lambda_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon}\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}\big(f^{\prime}\big(\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}+\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big)-f^{\prime}\left(w_{\varepsilon}\right)\big)\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\left[\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right],

(5.18)

where
L_{\varepsilon,\tau}
is as in (
5.1
). By using (
5.2
) and (
5.18
) together with a standard
L^{p}
-estimate and since
\lambda_{\varepsilon}\to\lambda_{0}
and
h_{\varepsilon}\to h_{0}
in
C^{0}\left(\overline{\Omega}\right)
, we then obtain

\left\|\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\left[\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right]\right\|_{W^{2,p}}=\bigO\bigg(\left\|\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}f^{\prime}\big(\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}+\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big)\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\big[\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big]\right\|_{L^{p}}\\
+\left\|\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}\big(f^{\prime}\big(\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}+\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big)-f^{\prime}\left(w_{\varepsilon}\right)\big)\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\left[\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right]\right\|_{L^{p}}\bigg).

(5.19)

By using (
3.11
), we obtain

\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\big[\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big]=\frac{2}{\gamma_{i}^{2}}\left(\ln\left|x-\overline{\tau_{i}}\right|+\bigO\left(1\right)\right)

(5.20)

uniformly in
x\in\Omega\backslash B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)
. By using (
5.11
) and (
5.20
) and proceeding as in (
5.12
)–(
5.15
), we obtain

\bigg\|\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}f^{\prime}\big(\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}+\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big)\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\big[\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big]\bigg\|_{L^{p}}^{p}=\bigO\bigg(\int_{r_{\varepsilon}}^{R_{\varepsilon}}f^{\prime}\left(\frac{2}{\gamma_{i}}\ln\frac{1}{r}+\smallo\left(1\right)\right)^{p}rdr\\
+\frac{1}{\gamma_{i}^{2p}}\int_{\Omega_{R_{\varepsilon},\tau}}\left|\ln\left|x-\overline{\tau_{i}}\right|+\bigO\left(1\right)\right|^{p}dx\bigg)=\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2p}}\right)

(5.21)

uniformly in
(\gamma,\tau)\in\Gamma_{\varepsilon}^{k}\left(\delta^{\prime}\right)\times T_{\varepsilon}^{k}\left(\delta\right)
, provided
p
is chosen so that
p<\left(1-\delta^{\prime}\right)^{2}/\delta_{0}
. On the other hand, by applying the mean value theorem together with Hölder’s inequality, we obtain

\left\|\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}\big(f^{\prime}\big(\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}+\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big)-f^{\prime}\left(w_{\varepsilon}\right)\big)\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\left[\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right]\right\|_{L^{p}}\leq\bigg\|\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\left[\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right]\bigg\|_{C^{0}}\\
\times\bigg\|\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}f^{\prime\prime}\Big(w_{\varepsilon}+t\sum_{i=1}^{k}B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}+t\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\Big)\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{k}B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}+\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\Big)\bigg\|_{L^{p}}

(5.22)

for some function
t:\Omega\to\left[0,1\right]
. By using (
5.11
) and proceeding as in (
5.12
)–(
5.15
), we obtain

\bigg\|\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}f^{\prime\prime}\Big(w_{\varepsilon}+t\sum_{i=1}^{k}B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}+t\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\Big)\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{k}B_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}+\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\Big)\bigg\|_{L^{p}}^{p}=\smallo\left(1\right)

(5.23)

as
\varepsilon\to 0
, uniformly in
(\gamma,\tau)\in\Gamma_{\varepsilon}^{k}\left(\delta^{\prime}\right)\times T_{\varepsilon}^{k}\left(\delta\right)
, provided we choose
p
such that
p<\left(1-\delta^{\prime}\right)^{2}/\delta_{0}
. By putting together (
5.19
) and (
5.21
)–(
5.23
), we obtain

\left\|\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\left[\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right]\right\|_{W^{2,p}}=\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right)+\smallo\left(\left\|\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\left[\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right]\right\|_{C^{0}}\right)

(5.24)

as
\varepsilon\to 0
, uniformly in
(\gamma,\tau)\in\Gamma_{\varepsilon}^{k}\left(\delta^{\prime}\right)\times T_{\varepsilon}^{k}\left(\delta\right)
. By choosing
p
such that

2<p<\frac{\left(1-\delta_{0}\right)^{2}}{\delta_{0}}

and using the continuity of the embedding
W^{2,p}\left(\Omega\right)\hookrightarrow C^{1}\left(\overline{\Omega}\right)
, the second inequality in (
3.14
) then follows from (
5.24
).

Now, we prove (
3.15
). For every
i\in\left\{1,\dotsc,k\right\}
, by differentiating (
3.13
) in
\tau_{i}
, we obtain

\Delta\left[L_{\varepsilon,\tau}\left(\partial_{\tau_{i}}\left[\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right]\right)\right]=\lambda_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon}f\big(\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}+\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big)\partial_{\tau_{i}}\big[\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}\big]+\lambda_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon}\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}f^{\prime}\big(\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}+\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big)\\
\times\partial_{\tau_{i}}\big[\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big]+\lambda_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon}\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}\big(f^{\prime}\big(\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}+\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big)-f^{\prime}\left(w_{\varepsilon}\right)\big)\partial_{\tau_{i}}\left[\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right],

(5.25)

where
L_{\varepsilon,\tau}
is as in (
5.1
). By using (
5.2
) and (
5.25
) together with a standard
L^{p}
–estimate and since
\lambda_{\varepsilon}\to\lambda_{0}
and
h_{\varepsilon}\to h_{0}
in
C^{0}\left(\overline{\Omega}\right)
, we obtain

\left\|\partial_{\tau_{i}}\left[\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right]\right\|_{W^{2,p}}=\bigO\bigg(\left\|f\big(\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}+\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big)\partial_{\tau_{i}}\big[\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}\big]\right\|_{L^{p}}+\Big\|\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}f^{\prime}\big(\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}+\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big)\\
\times\partial_{\tau_{i}}\big[\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big]\Big\|_{L^{p}}+\left\|\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}\big(f^{\prime}\big(\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}+\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big)-f^{\prime}\left(w_{\varepsilon}\right)\big)\partial_{\tau_{i}}\left[\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right]\right\|_{L^{p}}\bigg).

(5.26)

It is easy to see that

\partial_{\tau_{i}}\big[\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}\big]=\bigO\left(\frac{1}{r_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{A\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon},r_{\varepsilon}+r_{\varepsilon}^{2}\right)}\right)

(5.27)

uniformly in
\Omega
. By using (
3.10
) and (
3.12
) and since
\delta^{\prime}<1-\sqrt{2\delta_{0}}
, we obtain

\partial_{\tau_{i}}\big[\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big]=\frac{2}{\gamma_{i}}\left(1+\smallo\left(1\right)\right)\frac{x_{1}-\tau_{i}}{\left|x-\overline{\tau_{i}}\right|^{2}}+\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}\right)

(5.28)

uniformly in
x=\left(x_{1},x_{2}\right)\in\Omega\backslash B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)
. By using (
5.11
), (
5.27
), (
5.28
) and proceeding as in (
5.12
)–(
5.15
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left\|f\big(\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}+\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big)\partial_{\tau_{i}}\big[\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}\big]\right\|^{p}_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\qquad=\bigO\bigg(\frac{1}{r_{\varepsilon}^{2p}}\int_{r_{\varepsilon}}^{r_{\varepsilon}+r_{\varepsilon}^{2}}f\left(\frac{2}{\gamma_{i}}\ln\frac{1}{r}\left(1+\smallo\left(1\right)\right)+\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}\right)\right)^{p}rdr\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad=\bigO\left(\frac{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{p+2}}{r_{\varepsilon}^{2p}}\int^{\delta_{0}\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}/\gamma_{i}^{2}}_{\delta_{0}\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}/\gamma_{i}^{2}-\left(2/\gamma_{i}^{2}\right)\ln\left(1+r_{\varepsilon}\right)}\exp\left(\left(ps-1\right)s\gamma_{i}^{2}\left(1+\smallo\left(1\right)\right)\right)ds\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=\bigO\left(\frac{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{p}}{r_{\varepsilon}^{2p}}\ln\left(1+r_{\varepsilon}\right)\exp\left(\left(p\delta_{0}\frac{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}{\gamma_{i}^{2}}-1\right)\delta_{0}\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}+\smallo\left(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=\bigO\left(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{p}\exp\left(p\delta_{0}^{2}\frac{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{4}}{\gamma_{i}^{2}}+\left(p-\frac{3}{2}\right)\delta_{0}\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}+\smallo\left(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=\bigO\left(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{p}\exp\left(\left(\frac{p\delta_{0}}{\left(1-\delta^{\prime}\right)^{2}}+p-\frac{3}{2}\right)\delta_{0}\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}+\smallo\left(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}\right)\right)\right)=\smallo\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{p}}\right),

(5.29)

\displaystyle\left\|\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}f^{\prime}\big(\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}+\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big)\partial_{\tau_{i}}\big[\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big]\right\|_{L^{p}}^{p}=\bigO\Bigg(\frac{1}{\gamma_{i}^{p}}\int_{\Omega_{R_{\varepsilon},\tau}}\left(\frac{1}{\left|x-\overline{\tau_{i}}\right|}+1\right)^{p}dx

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\frac{1}{\gamma_{i}^{p}}\int_{r_{\varepsilon}}^{R_{\varepsilon}}f^{\prime}\left(\frac{2}{\gamma_{i}}\ln\frac{1}{r}\left(1+\smallo\left(1\right)\right)+\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}\right)\right)^{p}r^{1-p}dr\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{p}}+\gamma_{i}^{p+2}\int_{\delta_{0}/[\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\left(1+\delta^{\prime}\right)^{2}]}^{\delta_{0}/\left(1-\delta^{\prime}\right)^{2}}\exp\left(\left(ps+\frac{p}{2}-1\right)s\gamma_{i}^{2}+\smallo\left(\gamma_{i}^{2}\right)\right)ds\right)=\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{p}}\right)

(5.30)

as
\varepsilon\to 0
, uniformly in
(\gamma,\tau)\in\Gamma_{\varepsilon}^{k}\left(\delta^{\prime}\right)\times T_{\varepsilon}^{k}\left(\delta\right)
, provided we choose
p
such that

\frac{p\delta_{0}}{\left(1-\delta^{\prime}\right)^{2}}+p-\frac{3}{2}<0\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{p\delta_{0}}{\left(1-\delta^{\prime}\right)^{2}}+\frac{p}{2}-1<0,\\
\text{i.e. }\max\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\delta_{0}}{\left(1-\delta^{\prime}\right)^{2}},\frac{2}{3}\left(1+\frac{\delta_{0}}{\left(1-\delta^{\prime}\right)^{2}}\right)\right)<\frac{1}{p}<1,

which is possible since
\delta^{\prime}<1-\sqrt{2\delta_{0}}
. Note that in this case, we cannot choose
p>2
and so
W^{2,p}\left(\Omega\right)
does not embed into
C^{1}\left(\overline{\Omega}\right)
. Furthermore, by proceeding as in (
5.22
)–(
5.23
), we obtain

\left\|\chi_{\varepsilon,\tau}\big(f^{\prime}\big(\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}+\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big)-f^{\prime}\left(w_{\varepsilon}\right)\big)\partial_{\tau_{i}}\big[\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big]\right\|_{L^{p}}=\smallo\left(\left\|\partial_{\tau_{i}}\big[\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big]\right\|_{C^{0}}\right)

(5.31)

as
\varepsilon\to 0
, uniformly in
(\gamma,\tau)\in\Gamma_{\varepsilon}^{k}\left(\delta^{\prime}\right)\times T_{\varepsilon}^{k}\left(\delta\right)
. By putting together (
5.26
), (
5.29
), (
5.30
) and (
5.31
), we obtain

\left\|\partial_{\tau_{i}}\big[\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big]\right\|_{W^{2,p}}=\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}\right)+\smallo\left(\left\|\partial_{\tau_{i}}\big[\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\big]\right\|_{C^{0}}\right)

(5.32)

as
\varepsilon\to 0
, uniformly in
(\gamma,\tau)\in\Gamma_{\varepsilon}^{k}\left(\delta^{\prime}\right)\times T_{\varepsilon}^{k}\left(\delta\right)
. By using the continuity of the embeddings of
W^{2,p}\left(\Omega\right)
into
C^{0}\left(\overline{\Omega}\right)
and
H^{1}\left(\Omega\right)
, (
3.15
) then follows from (
5.32
).

Note that (
5.29
) corresponds to the first identity in (
3.17
), while the second one follows from (
5.30
) together with the already proven (
3.15
) and (
3.16
), which yield

\displaystyle\left\|f^{\prime}\left(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right)D_{\tau_{i}}\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{r_{\varepsilon},\tau}}\right\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}\left\|\exp\big(U_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}^{2}\big)\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{r_{\varepsilon},\tau}}\right\|_{L^{p}}\left\|D_{\tau_{i}}\Psi_{\varepsilon,\gamma,\tau}\right\|_{C^{0}}\right)=\bigO\left(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\right).

This ends the proof of Proposition
3.2
.

6.
Expansions of the bubble and its derivatives

In this section we give a precise asymptotic analysis of spherical solutions, and prove some useful consequences.

Proposition 6.1
.

For every
\gamma>0
, let
\overline{B}_{\gamma}
be the unique radial solution to the problem

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\Delta\overline{B}_{\gamma}=f\left(\overline{B}_{\gamma}\right)&&\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2}\\
&\overline{B}_{\gamma}\left(0\right)=\gamma,&&\end{aligned}\right.

where
f\left(s\right):=s\exp\left(s^{2}\right)
for all
s\in\mathbb{R}
. Set

\mu_{\gamma}^{2}:=4\gamma^{-2}\exp\left(-\gamma^{2}\right)\quad\text{and}\quad t\left(r\right):=\ln\left(1+r^{2}\right)\quad\forall r\geq 0

(6.1)

and let
\varphi
be the unique radial solution to the problem

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\Delta\varphi=4e^{-2t}\left(t^{2}-t+2\varphi\right)&&\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2}\\
&\varphi\left(0\right)=0.&&\end{aligned}\right.

Then

\overline{B}_{\gamma}\left(r\right)=\gamma-\frac{t\left(r/\mu_{\gamma}\right)}{\gamma}+\frac{\varphi\left(r/\mu_{\gamma}\right)}{\gamma^{3}}+D_{\gamma}\left(r/\mu_{\gamma}\right),

where

D_{\gamma}\left(r\right)=\bigO\left(\frac{t\left(r\right)}{\gamma^{5}}\right)\quad\text{and}\quad D^{\prime}_{\gamma}\left(r\right)=\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\gamma^{5}r}\right)

(6.2)

as
\gamma\to\infty
, uniformly in
r\in\left(0,\mu_{\gamma}^{\delta-1}\right)
,
\delta\in\left(0,1\right)
fixed. Furthermore,
\varphi\left(r\right)\sim-t\left(r\right)
and
\varphi^{\prime}\left(r\right)\sim-t^{\prime}\left(r\right)
as
r\to\infty
.

Proof.

This was originally proven in
[
DruThi
]
, see Claim 5.1 and estimates (5.8) and (5.9) in particular (note that the function
B_{\gamma}
in
[
DruThi
]
corresponds to the function
\overline{B}_{\gamma}
via the relation
B_{\gamma}\left(r\right)=\overline{B}_{\gamma}(r/2)
). The estimates (5.8)–(5.9) in
[
DruThi
]
are valid as long as
0\leq t(r/\mu_{\gamma})\leq\gamma^{2}-T_{\gamma}
, where
T_{\gamma}
is chosen so that
\gamma^{k}e^{-T_{\gamma}}=\smallo\left(1\right)
as
\gamma\to\infty
for every
k\geq 0
. It is not difficult to see that this condition is satisfied uniformly for
0\leq r\leq\mu_{\gamma}^{\delta}
, for any fixed
\delta>0
.
∎

With regard to the derivative of
\overline{B}_{\gamma}
with respect to
\gamma
, we obtain the following:

Proposition 6.2
.

Let
\overline{B}_{\gamma}
,
\mu_{\gamma}
,
t
and
\varphi
be as in Proposition
6.1
. Set
\overline{Z}_{0}\left(r\right):=\frac{1-r^{2}}{1+r^{2}}
and let
\psi
be the unique radial solution to the problem

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\Delta\psi=4e^{-2t}\left(\overline{Z}_{0}\left(1-4t+2t^{2}+4\varphi\right)+2\psi\right)&&\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{2}\\
&\psi\left(0\right)=0.&&\end{aligned}\right.

Then

Z_{0,\gamma}\left(r\right):=\partial_{\gamma}\left[\overline{B}_{\gamma}\left(r\right)\right]=\overline{Z}_{0}(r/\mu_{\gamma})+\frac{\psi\left(r/\mu_{\gamma}\right)}{\gamma^{2}}+E_{\gamma}(r/\mu_{\gamma}),

where

E_{\gamma}\left(r\right)=\bigO\left(\frac{1+t\left(r\right)}{\gamma^{4}}\right)\quad\text{and}\quad E_{\gamma}^{\prime}\left(r\right)=\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\gamma^{4}r}\right)

(6.3)

as
\gamma\to\infty
, uniformly in
r\in\left(0,\mu_{\gamma}^{\delta-1}\right)
,
\delta\in\left(0,1\right)
fixed.
Furthermore,
\psi\left(r\right)\sim t\left(r\right)
and
\psi^{\prime}\left(r\right)\sim t^{\prime}\left(r\right)
as
r\to\infty
.

Proof.

We easily see that

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\Delta Z_{0,\gamma}=f^{\prime}\big(\overline{B}_{\gamma}\big)Z_{0,\gamma}&&\text{in }B(0,\mu_{\gamma}^{\delta})\\
&Z_{0,\gamma}\left(0\right)=1,&&\end{aligned}\right.

with
f\left(s\right)=se^{s^{2}}
. Set

E_{\gamma}\left(r\right):=Z_{0,\gamma}\left(\mu_{\gamma}r\right)-\overline{Z}_{0}\left(r\right)-\frac{\psi\left(r\right)}{\gamma^{2}}

and observe that

\Delta\overline{Z}_{0}=8e^{-2t}\overline{Z}_{0},

so that

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\Delta E_{\gamma}=\mu_{\gamma}^{2}f^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{\gamma}\left(\mu_{\gamma}\cdot\right)\right)Z_{0,\gamma}\left(\mu_{\gamma}\cdot\right)-8e^{-2t}\overline{Z}_{0}-\frac{\Delta\psi}{\gamma^{2}}&&\text{in }B\left(0,\mu_{\gamma}^{\delta-1}\right)\\
&E_{\gamma}\left(0\right)=0.&&\end{aligned}\right.

(6.4)

In order to expand the right-hand side of (
6.4
) we use (
6.2
),
\varphi=\bigO\left(1+t\right)
and recalling that
\mu_{\gamma}^{2}\gamma^{2}e^{\gamma^{2}}=4
, we find

\displaystyle f^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{\gamma}\left(\mu_{\gamma}\cdot\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\big(1+2\overline{B}_{\gamma}^{2}\left(\mu_{\gamma}\cdot\right)\big)\exp\big(\overline{B}_{\gamma}^{2}\left(\mu_{\gamma}\cdot\right)\big)

\displaystyle=\left[1+2\left(\gamma-\frac{t}{\gamma}+\frac{\varphi}{\gamma^{3}}+\bigO\left(\frac{t}{\gamma^{5}}\right)\right)^{2}\right]e^{\left(\gamma-\frac{t}{\gamma}+\frac{\varphi}{\gamma^{3}}+\bigO\left(\frac{t}{\gamma^{5}}\right)\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{4e^{-2t}}{\mu_{\gamma}^{2}}\left[\frac{1}{\gamma^{2}}+2-\frac{4t}{\gamma^{2}}+\bigO\left(\frac{1+t^{2}}{\gamma^{4}}\right)\right]e^{\frac{t^{2}}{\gamma^{2}}}e^{\frac{2\varphi}{\gamma^{2}}+\bigO\left(\frac{1+t^{2}}{\gamma^{4}}\right)}.

(6.5)

Using that
e^{s}=1+s+\bigO(s^{2})e^{s}
for
s>0
, we write

e^{\frac{t^{2}}{\gamma^{2}}}=1+\frac{t^{2}}{\gamma^{2}}+\bigO\left(\frac{t^{4}}{\gamma^{4}}\right)e^{\frac{t^{2}}{\gamma^{2}}},

and using that
t=\bigO\left(\gamma^{2}\right)
uniformly on
\left(0,\mu_{\gamma}^{\delta-1}\right)
,

e^{\frac{2\varphi}{\gamma^{2}}+\bigO\left(\frac{1+t^{2}}{\gamma^{4}}\right)}=1+\frac{2\varphi}{\gamma^{2}}+\bigO\left(\frac{1+t^{2}}{\gamma^{4}}\right).

We now multiply and reorder, using that
\exp\left(t^{2}/\gamma^{2}\right)\geq 1
, to obtain

\displaystyle f^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{\gamma}\left(\mu_{\gamma}\cdot\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{4e^{-2t}}{\mu_{\gamma}^{2}}\left(2+\frac{1}{\gamma^{2}}\left(1-4t+4\varphi\right)+\bigO\left(\frac{1+t^{4}}{\gamma^{4}}\right)\right)e^{\frac{t^{2}}{\gamma^{2}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{4e^{-2t}}{\mu_{\gamma}^{2}}\left(2+\frac{1}{\gamma^{2}}\left(1-4t+2t^{2}+4\varphi\right)\right)+\frac{e^{-2t+\frac{t^{2}}{\gamma^{2}}}}{\mu_{\gamma}^{2}}\bigO\left(\frac{1+t^{4}}{\gamma^{4}}\right).

Together with (
6.4
) and using that
\psi=\bigO\left(1+t\right)
(as we shall prove later), we now estimate

\displaystyle\Delta E_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=\mu_{\gamma}^{2}\left(f^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{\gamma}\left(\mu_{\gamma}\cdot\right)\right)\overline{Z}_{0}+\frac{f^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{\gamma}\left(\mu_{\gamma}\cdot\right)\right)\psi}{\gamma^{2}}+f^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{\gamma}\left(\mu_{\gamma}\cdot\right)\right)E_{\gamma}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-8e^{-2t}\overline{Z}_{0}-4e^{-2t}\left(\frac{\overline{Z}_{0}}{\gamma^{2}}\left(1-4t+2t^{2}+4\varphi\right)+\frac{2\psi}{\gamma^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mu_{\gamma}^{2}f^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{\gamma}\left(\mu_{\gamma}\cdot\right)\right)E_{\gamma}+e^{-2t+\frac{t^{2}}{\gamma^{2}}}\bigO\left(\frac{1+t^{4}}{\gamma^{4}}\right).

We now go back to (
6.5
) and, still using that
t=\bigO\left(\gamma^{2}\right)
on
B\left(0,\mu_{\gamma}^{\delta-1}\right)
, we bound

f^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{\gamma}\left(\mu_{\gamma}\cdot\right)\right)=\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\mu_{\gamma}^{2}}e^{-2t+\frac{t^{2}}{\gamma^{2}}}\right),

so that

\Delta E_{\gamma}=e^{-2t+\frac{t^{2}}{\gamma^{2}}}\left(\bigO\left(\left|E_{\gamma}\right|\right)+\bigO\left(\frac{1+t^{4}}{\gamma^{4}}\right)\right).

(6.6)

Multiplying by
\gamma^{4}
and using ODE theory, we see that

\gamma^{4}E_{\gamma}\longrightarrow\widetilde{E}_{\infty}\quad\text{in }C^{1}_{\loc}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}\right).\

In particular, for any fixed
T>0
and for
\gamma
large (
\gamma\geq\gamma_{0}\left(T\right)
), we have

\left|E_{\gamma}\right|\leq\frac{C\left(T\right)}{\gamma^{4}}\quad\text{and}\quad\left|E^{\prime}_{\gamma}\right|\leq\frac{C^{\prime}\left(T\right)}{\gamma^{4}}\quad\text{on }\left[0,T\right].

(6.7)

From now on, it is understood that
\gamma\geq\gamma_{0}\left(T\right)
, so that (
6.7
) holds.
In order to prove (
6.3
), observe that the first identity in (
6.3
) follows from the second one and (
6.7
) by integration over
\left[T,r\right]
. Then, for
T,M>0
to be chosen later, set

R_{\gamma}:=\sup\left\{r\in\left(T,\mu_{\gamma}^{\delta-1}\right]:\,\left|E_{\gamma}^{\prime}\left(\rho\right)\right|\leq\frac{M}{\gamma^{4}\rho},\,\forall\rho\in\left[T,r\right]\right\}.

We shall prove that for
T
and
M
suitable, we have
R_{\gamma}=\mu_{\gamma}^{\delta-1}
for every
\gamma
sufficiently large.

Arguing by contradiction, assume that
R_{\gamma}<\mu_{\gamma}^{\delta-1}
,
so that in particular

\left|E^{\prime}_{\gamma}(R_{\gamma})\right|=\frac{M}{R_{\gamma}\gamma^{4}}.

(6.8)

By definition of
R_{\gamma}
, using (
6.7
) and integrating, we get

\left|E_{\gamma}\left(r\right)\right|\leq\left|E_{\gamma}\left(T\right)\right|+\int_{T}^{r}\frac{M}{\gamma^{4}\rho}d\rho\leq\frac{C\left(T\right)}{\gamma^{4}}+\frac{Mt\left(r\right)}{2\gamma^{4}}\quad\text{on }\left[T,R_{\gamma}\right].

(6.9)

With the divergence theorem, (
6.6
) and (
6.9
), we now bound for
t\in[T,R_{\gamma}]
,

\displaystyle\left|2\pi rE^{\prime}_{\gamma}\left(r\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|2\pi TE^{\prime}_{\gamma}\left(T\right)\right|+\int_{B\left(0,r\right)\setminus B\left(0,T\right)}|\Delta E_{\gamma}\left(x\right)dx|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\pi TC^{\prime}\left(T\right)}{\gamma^{4}}+\int_{B\left(0,r\right)\setminus B\left(0,T\right)}e^{-2t+\frac{t^{2}}{\gamma^{2}}}\left(\widetilde{C}|E_{\gamma}|+\widetilde{C}\left(\frac{1+t^{4}}{\gamma^{4}}\right)\right)dx

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\pi TC^{\prime}\left(T\right)}{\gamma^{4}}+\frac{\widetilde{C}M}{2\gamma^{4}}\int_{B\left(0,r\right)\setminus B\left(0,T\right)}e^{-2t+\frac{t^{2}}{\gamma^{2}}}tdx

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{\gamma^{4}}\int_{B\left(0,r\right)\setminus B\left(0,T\right)}e^{-2t+\frac{t^{2}}{\gamma^{2}}}\widetilde{C}\left(C\left(T\right)+1+t^{4}\right)dx

\displaystyle=:\frac{2\pi TC^{\prime}\left(T\right)}{\gamma^{4}}+\frac{(I_{\gamma})}{\gamma^{4}}+\frac{(II_{\gamma})}{\gamma^{4}}.

(6.10)

Observing that

-2t+\frac{t^{2}}{\gamma^{2}}\leq-\left(1+\delta\right)t\quad\text{and}\quad e^{-\frac{\delta}{2}t}t^{k}=\bigO\left(1\right)\quad\text{on }B\left(0,\mu_{\gamma}^{\delta-1}\right),\quad\forall k\geq 0,

we bound

\displaystyle\int_{B\left(0,\mu_{\gamma}^{\delta-1}\right)\setminus B\left(0,T\right)}e^{-2t+\frac{t^{2}}{\gamma^{2}}}t^{k}dx

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\int_{B\left(0,T\right)^{c}}e^{-\left(1+\frac{\delta}{2}\right)t}dx\right)=\smallo_{T}\left(1\right),

with
\smallo_{T}\left(1\right)\to 0
as
T\to\infty
. We can therefore choose
T
sufficiently large (independent of
M
) so that

(I_{\gamma})\leq\frac{\pi M}{2}.

Then, choosing
M
sufficiently large (depending on
T
), so that

2\pi TC^{\prime}\left(T\right)+(II_{\gamma})\leq\frac{\pi M}{2}

and dividing by
2\pi
in (
6.10
), we finally obtain

r\left|E^{\prime}\left(r\right)\right|\leq\frac{M}{2\gamma^{4}},\quad\forall r\in\left[T,R_{\gamma}\right],

which for
r=R_{\gamma}
is a contradiction to (
6.8
). Therefore
R_{\gamma}=\mu_{\gamma}^{\delta-1}
.

To prove that
\psi\left(r\right)\sim~t\left(r\right)
and
\psi^{\prime}\left(r\right)\sim~t^{\prime}\left(r\right)
as
r\to\infty
, we recall from
[
ManMar
]
*Lemmas 15 and 16 (see also
[
DruThi
]
*Lemma 5.1) that if
\psi
is radially symmetric and solves

\Delta\psi=4e^{-2t}\left(g+2\psi\right),

with
g\left(r\right)=\bigO\big(\left(\ln r\right)^{k}\big)
as
r\to\infty
for some
k\geq 1
, then

\psi\left(r\right)=\beta\ln r+\bigO\left(r\right),\quad\psi^{\prime}\left(r\right)=\frac{\beta}{r}+\bigO\left(\frac{\left(\ln r\right)^{k}}{r^{3}}\right),\quad\beta:=\frac{2}{\pi}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\overline{Z}_{0}e^{-2t}gdx,

as
r\to\infty
.
With
g=\overline{Z}_{0}\left(1-4t+2t^{2}+4\varphi\right)
we compute

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\overline{Z}_{0}^{2}e^{-2t}dx=\frac{\pi}{3},\quad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\overline{Z}_{0}^{2}e^{-2t}4tdx=\frac{16\pi}{9},

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\overline{Z}_{0}^{2}e^{-2t}2t^{2}dx=\frac{70\pi}{27}\quad\text{and}\quad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\overline{Z}_{0}^{2}e^{-2t}4\varphi dx=-\frac{4\pi}{27},

so that
\beta=2
.
∎

Let us see a few consequences of the above estimates.

Proposition 6.3
.

Let
\overline{B}_{\gamma}
,
\mu_{\gamma}
,
t
,
\overline{Z}_{0}
and
Z_{0,\gamma}
be as in Propositions
6.1
and
6.2
. Given
\delta\in\left(0,1\right)
,
a,b\geq 0
and
t_{\gamma}\left(r\right)=t(r/\mu_{\gamma})
, we have

\int_{B\left(0,r\right)}\exp\big(\overline{B}_{\gamma}^{2}\big)\overline{B}_{\gamma}^{b}\left(1+\bigO\left(\frac{t_{\gamma}}{\gamma^{2}}\right)\right)^{a}dx=4\pi\gamma^{b-2}+\bigO\left(\gamma^{b-4}\right),

(6.11)

as
\gamma\to\infty
, uniformly for
\gamma\mu_{\gamma}\leq r\leq\mu_{\gamma}^{\delta}
.
Moreover,

\int_{B\left(0,r\right)}f^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{\gamma}\right)Z_{0,\gamma}dx=\frac{-4\pi+\smallo\left(1\right)}{\gamma^{2}}

(6.12)

as
\gamma\to\infty
, uniformly for
\gamma\mu_{\gamma}=\smallo\left(r\right)
and
r\leq\mu_{\gamma}^{\delta}
, and

\int_{B\left(0,r\right)}f^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{\gamma}\left(x\right)\right)\frac{2x_{1}^{2}}{\mu_{\gamma}^{2}+\left|x\right|^{2}}dx=4\pi+\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\gamma^{2}}\right)

(6.13)

as
\gamma\to\infty
, uniformly for
\gamma\mu_{\gamma}\leq r\leq\mu_{\gamma}^{\delta}
.

Proof.

Using Proposition
6.1
and noticing that
\varphi=\bigO\left(1+t\right)
,
t_{\gamma}=\bigO\left(\gamma^{2}\right)
in
B\left(0,r\right)
for
r\leq\mu_{\gamma}^{\delta}
, we write

\exp\big(\overline{B}_{\gamma}^{2}\big)=e^{\left[\gamma-\frac{t_{\gamma}}{\gamma}+\bigO\left(\frac{1+t_{\gamma}}{\gamma^{3}}\right)\right]^{2}}=e^{\gamma^{2}}e^{-2t_{\gamma}}e^{\frac{t_{\gamma}^{2}}{\gamma^{2}}+\bigO\left(\frac{1+t_{\gamma}}{\gamma^{2}}\right)}=\frac{4e^{-2t_{\gamma}}}{\mu_{\gamma}^{2}\gamma^{2}}\left(1+\bigO\left(\frac{t_{\gamma}^{2}}{\gamma^{2}}\right)e^{\frac{t_{\gamma}^{2}}{\gamma^{2}}}\right),

(6.14)

where we used the inequality
|e^{x}-1|\leq\left|x\right|e^{\left|x\right|}
to estimate

\bigg|e^{\bigO\left(\frac{t_{\gamma}^{2}}{\gamma^{2}}\right)}-1\bigg|=\bigO\left(\frac{t_{\gamma}^{2}}{\gamma^{2}}\right)e^{\frac{t_{\gamma}^{2}}{\gamma^{2}}}.

Further, we use Proposition
6.1
together with
\left(1+x\right)^{a}=1+\bigO\left(x\right)
uniformly for
x=\bigO\left(1\right)
, to bound

\displaystyle\overline{B}_{\gamma}^{b}\left(1+\bigO\left(\frac{t_{\gamma}}{\gamma^{2}}\right)\right)^{a}

\displaystyle=\left(\gamma+\bigO\left(\frac{1+t_{\gamma}}{\gamma}\right)\right)^{b}\left(1+\bigO\left(\frac{t_{\gamma}}{\gamma^{2}}\right)\right)^{a}

\displaystyle=\gamma^{b}\left(1+\bigO\left(\frac{1+t_{\gamma}}{\gamma}\right)\right)^{a+b}=\gamma^{b}\left(1+\bigO\left(\frac{1+t_{\gamma}^{2}}{\gamma^{2}}\right)\right).

(6.15)

We can then estimate the left-hand side of (
6.11
) as

\int_{B\left(0,r\right)}\frac{4e^{-2t_{\gamma}}}{\mu_{\gamma}^{2}\gamma^{2-b}}\left(1+\bigO\left(\frac{1+t_{\gamma}^{2}}{\gamma^{2}}e^{\frac{t_{\gamma}^{2}}{\gamma^{2}}}\right)\right)dx\\
=\int_{B\left(0,r/\mu_{\gamma}\right)}\frac{4e^{-2t}}{\gamma^{2-b}}\left(1+\bigO\left(\frac{1+t^{2}}{\gamma^{2}}e^{\frac{t^{2}}{\gamma^{2}}}\right)\right)dx=\frac{4\pi}{\gamma^{2-b}}\int_{0}^{r/\mu_{\gamma}}\frac{2\rho}{\left(1+\rho^{2}\right)^{2}}d\rho\\
+\frac{1}{\gamma^{4-b}}\int_{B\left(0,r/\mu_{\gamma}\right)}\bigO\left(\left(1+t^{2}\right)e^{-t\left(2-\frac{t}{\gamma^{2}}\right)}\right)dx=:\left(I\right)_{\gamma}+(II)_{\gamma}.

Using that
r\geq\gamma\mu_{\gamma}
, one computes

\left(I\right)_{\gamma}=\frac{4\pi}{\gamma^{2-b}}\left(1+\bigO\left(\gamma^{-2}\right)\right),

and using that
0\leq t/\gamma^{2}\leq\left(1-\delta+\smallo\left(1\right)\right)
in
B\left(0,r/\mu_{\gamma}\right)
as
\gamma\to\infty
, uniformly for
r\leq\mu_{\gamma}^{\delta}
, and observing that
\left(1+t^{2}\right)e^{-\left(1+\delta^{\prime}\right)t}\in L^{1}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}\right)
for every
\delta^{\prime}>0
, one has

(II)_{\gamma}=\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\gamma^{4-b}}\int_{B\left(0,r/\mu_{\gamma}\right)}\left(1+t^{2}\right)e^{-t\left(1+\delta+\smallo\left(1\right)\right)}\right)=\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\gamma^{4-b}}\right)

(6.16)

as
\gamma\to\infty
, uniformly for
r\leq\mu_{\gamma}^{\delta}
, so that (
6.11
) is proven.

In order to prove (
6.12
) we use Proposition
6.2
to expand
Z_{0,\gamma}
and compute

\displaystyle\int_{B\left(0,r\right)}f^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{\gamma}\right)Z_{0,\gamma}dx

\displaystyle=\int_{B\left(0,r\right)}\partial_{\gamma}\left[f\left(\overline{B}_{\gamma}\right)\right]dx=\int_{B\left(0,r\right)}\Delta Z_{0,\gamma}dx

\displaystyle=\int_{B\left(0,r\right)}\Delta\left(\overline{Z}_{0}\left(\frac{x}{\mu_{\gamma}}\right)+\frac{1}{\gamma^{2}}\psi\left(\frac{x}{\mu_{\gamma}}\right)+E_{\gamma}\left(\frac{x}{\mu_{\gamma}}\right)\right)dx

\displaystyle=-\frac{2\pi r}{\mu_{\gamma}}\left(\overline{Z}_{0}^{\prime}\left(\frac{r}{\mu_{\gamma}}\right)+\frac{1}{\gamma^{2}}\psi^{\prime}\left(\frac{r}{\mu_{\gamma}}\right)+E_{\gamma}^{\prime}\left(\frac{r}{\mu_{\gamma}}\right)\right).

A direct computation shows

\frac{r}{\mu_{\gamma}}\overline{Z}_{0}^{\prime}\left(\frac{r}{\mu_{\gamma}}\right)=\bigO\left(\frac{\mu_{\gamma}^{2}}{r^{2}}\right)=\smallo\left(\frac{1}{\gamma^{2}}\right)

as
\gamma\to\infty
, uniformly for
\gamma\mu_{\gamma}=\smallo\left(r\right)
. Using that

\psi^{\prime}\left(s\right)=t^{\prime}\left(s\right)\left(1+\smallo\left(1\right)\right)=\frac{2s}{1+s^{s}}=\frac{2}{s}\left(1+\smallo\left(1\right)\right)\quad\text{as }s\to\infty,

we obtain

\frac{r}{\mu_{\gamma}}\psi^{\prime}\left(\frac{r}{\mu_{\gamma}}\right)=2+\smallo\left(1\right).

Finally, from the second part of (
6.3
), we infer

\frac{r}{\mu_{\gamma}}E_{\gamma}^{\prime}\left(\frac{r}{\mu_{\gamma}}\right)=\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\gamma^{4}}\right)=\smallo\left(\frac{1}{\gamma^{2}}\right).

Summing up, (
6.12
) follows at once.

It remains to prove (
6.13
). Using (
6.14
) and (
6
), we write

\displaystyle\int_{B\left(0,r\right)}f^{\prime}\left(\overline{B}_{\gamma}\left(x\right)\right)\frac{2x_{1}^{2}}{\mu_{\gamma}^{2}+\left|x\right|^{2}}dx

\displaystyle=4\gamma^{2}e^{\gamma^{2}}\int_{B\left(0,r\right)}\left(1+\bigO\left(\frac{1+t_{\gamma}}{\gamma^{2}}\right)\right)e^{-3t_{\gamma}}\left(1+\bigO\left(\frac{t_{\gamma}^{2}}{\gamma^{2}}\right)e^{\frac{t_{\gamma}^{2}}{\gamma^{2}}}\right)\left(\frac{y_{1}}{\mu_{\gamma}}\right)^{2}dy

\displaystyle=\int_{B\left(0,r/\mu_{\gamma}\right)}16e^{-3t}y_{1}^{2}dy+\bigO\left(\int_{B\left(0,r/\mu_{\gamma}\right)}\frac{1+t^{2}}{\gamma^{2}}e^{-t\left(3-t/\gamma^{2}\right)}y_{1}^{2}dy\right)=:\left(I\right)_{\gamma}+(II)_{\gamma}

To compute
\left(I\right)_{\gamma}
, we observe that its value does not change if we replace
y_{1}
with
y_{2}
, so that

\displaystyle\left(I\right)_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{B\left(0,r/\mu_{\gamma}\right)}16e^{-3t}\left|x\right|^{2}dy=16\pi\left[-\frac{1+2\rho^{2}}{4\left(1+\rho^{2}\right)^{2}}\right]_{\rho=0}^{r/\mu_{\gamma}}=4\pi+\smallo\left(1\right)

as
\gamma\to\infty
, uniformly for
r\geq\gamma\mu_{\gamma}
. The term
(II)_{\gamma}
can be estimated as in (
6.16
) since
y_{1}^{2}\leq e^{t}
, so that

(II)_{\gamma}=\bigO\left(\int_{B\left(0,r/\mu_{\gamma}\right)}\frac{t^{2}}{\gamma^{2}}e^{-t\left(1+\delta+\smallo\left(1\right)\right)}dx\right)=\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\gamma^{2}}\right)

as
\gamma\to\infty
, uniformly for
r\leq\mu_{\gamma}^{\delta}
.
∎

Proposition 6.4
.

Let
\overline{B}_{\gamma}
and
\mu_{\gamma}
be as in Proposition
6.1
. Given
\delta_{0}\in\left(0,1/2\right)
, we have

\int_{B\left(0,r\right)}\exp\big(\overline{B}_{\gamma}\left(x\right)^{2}\big)\left|x\right|dx=\bigO\big(\mu_{\gamma}^{3\delta_{0}-2\delta_{0}^{2}+\smallo\left(1\right)}\big)

(6.17)

as
\gamma\to\infty
, uniformly for
r=\bigO\left(\mu_{\gamma}^{\delta_{0}}\right)
.

Proof.

Let
t_{\gamma}
be as in Proposition
6.2
. Using Proposition
6.1
, we write

\displaystyle\exp\big(\overline{B}_{\gamma}^{2}\big)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(\left[\gamma-\frac{t_{\gamma}}{\gamma}+\bigO\left(\frac{1+t_{\gamma}}{\gamma^{3}}\right)\right]^{2}\right)=e^{\gamma^{2}}e^{-2t_{\gamma}+\frac{t_{\gamma}^{2}}{\gamma^{2}}}e^{\bigO\left(\frac{1+t_{\gamma}}{\gamma^{2}}\right)}

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\frac{e^{-t_{\gamma}\left(2-t_{\gamma}/\gamma^{2}\right)}}{\mu_{\gamma}^{2}\gamma^{2}}\right),\quad\text{for }r=\bigO\left(\mu_{\gamma}^{\delta_{0}}\right),

(6.18)

Then, using that

t_{\gamma}\left(r\right)\leq\left(1-\delta_{0}+\smallo\left(1\right)\right)\gamma^{2},\quad\text{for }r=\bigO\left(\mu_{\gamma}^{\delta_{0}}\right),

(6.19)

together with a change of variables, we get

\displaystyle\int_{B\left(0,r\right)}\exp\big(\overline{B}_{\gamma}\left(x\right)^{2}\big)\left|x\right|dx=\bigO\left(\int_{B\left(0,r\right)}\frac{e^{-t_{\gamma}\left(2-t_{\gamma}/\gamma^{2}\right)}}{\mu_{\gamma}^{2}\gamma^{2}}\left|x\right|dx\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=\bigO\left(\mu_{\gamma}\int_{B\left(0,r/\mu_{\gamma}\right)}\frac{\left|y\right|dy}{\big(1+\left|y\right|^{2}\big)^{2-t/\gamma^{2}}}\right)=\bigO\left(\mu_{\gamma}\int_{B\left(0,r/\mu_{\gamma}\right)}\frac{\left|y\right|dy}{\big(1+\left|y\right|^{2}\big)^{1+\delta_{0}+\smallo\left(1\right)}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=\bigO\left(\mu_{\gamma}\left(\frac{r}{\mu_{\gamma}}\right)^{1-2\delta_{0}+\smallo\left(1\right)}\right)=\bigO\left(\mu_{\gamma}^{3\delta_{0}-2\delta_{0}^{2}+\smallo\left(1\right)}\right)

as
\gamma\to\infty
, uniformly for
r=\bigO\left(\mu_{\gamma}^{\delta_{0}}\right)
, which proves (
6.17
).
∎

7.
Poincaré–Sobolev inequalities

The standard Poincaré–Sobolev inequality on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
says that for every
p\in[1,\infty)
there exists
C_{p}>0
such that for every
\phi\in H^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{2})
with
\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\phi dv_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}=0
, we have

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\left|\phi\right|^{p}dv_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\leq C_{p}\left(\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\left|\nabla\phi\right|^{2}dv_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}.

(7.1)

Pulling back the spherical metric onto
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, we can also rewrite (
7.1
) as

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left|\phi\right|^{p}e^{-2t}dx\leq C_{p}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left|\nabla\phi\right|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{p}{2}},

(7.2)

for every
\phi\in D^{1,2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}\right)
such that
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\phi e^{-2t}dx=0
, where
t\left(x\right):=\ln\big(1+\left|x\right|^{2}\big)
, so that
4e^{-2t\left(x\right)}=4\big(1+\left|x\right|^{2}\big)^{-2}
is the conformal factor of the pull-back metric.

We will need a perturbed version of (
7.2
), where we replace
e^{-2t}
with suitable scaled versions of
\exp\big(\overline{B}_{\gamma}^{2}\big)
.

Lemma 7.1
.

Let
(\chi_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon>0}
be a sequence of functions in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
such that for every
q>1
, we have
\chi_{\varepsilon}\to\chi_{0}
as
\varepsilon\to 0
in
L^{q}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2},e^{-2t}dx\right)
, i.e.

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left|\chi_{\varepsilon}-\chi_{0}\right|^{q}e^{-2t}dx\longrightarrow 0

for some function
\chi_{0}
in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
and further assume that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\chi_{0}e^{-2t}dx\neq 0.

(7.3)

Then, for every
p\in\left[1,\infty\right)
, there exists a constant
C>0
(depending on
p
and
(\chi_{\varepsilon})
) such that for
\varepsilon>0
small enough, the following holds:

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left|\phi\right|^{p}e^{-2t}dx\leq C\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left|\nabla\phi\right|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}

(7.4)

for every
\phi\in D^{1,2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}\right)
such that
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\phi\chi_{\varepsilon}e^{-2t}dx=0
.

Proof.

Assume by contradiction that there exists a sequence
(\phi_{\varepsilon})_{\varepsilon}
in
D^{1,2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}\right)
such that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\phi_{\varepsilon}|^{p}e^{-2t}dx=1,\quad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\phi_{\varepsilon}\chi_{\varepsilon}e^{-2t}dx=0,\quad\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\nabla\phi_{\varepsilon}|^{2}dx=0.

(7.5)

Let
\Pi:\mathbb{S}^{2}\to\mathbb{R}^{2}
be the stereographic projection. By the first equation in (
7.5
), the average of
\phi_{\varepsilon}\circ\Pi
on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
is bounded, so by the Sobolev–Poincaré inequality and weak compactness, up to a subsequence,
\phi_{\varepsilon}\circ\Pi\to\phi_{0}\circ\Pi
strongly in
L^{q^{\prime}}(\mathbb{S}^{2})
, in
L^{p}(\mathbb{S}^{2})
, and weakly in
H^{1}(\mathbb{S}^{2})
, for some function
\phi_{0}\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{2},e^{-2t}dx)
. By lower-semicontinuity of the Dirichlet integral we get
\|\nabla(\phi_{0}\circ\Pi)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}=\|\nabla\phi_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}=0
, so that
\phi_{0}
is constant, non-zero since
\|\phi_{0}\circ\Pi\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}=1
. Then, we obtain

0=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\phi_{\varepsilon}\chi_{\varepsilon}e^{-2t}dx\to\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\phi_{0}\chi_{0}e^{-2t}dx\quad\Rightarrow\quad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\chi_{0}e^{-2t}dx=0,

contradicting our assumption.
∎

Proposition 7.2
.

Let
\overline{B}_{\gamma}
,
\mu_{\gamma}
and
t_{\gamma}
be as in Propositions
6.1
and
6.2
. Let
\phi\in D^{1,2}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2}\right)
be such that

\int_{B_{r}}f\big(\overline{B}_{\gamma}\big)\phi dx=0

(7.6)

for
r
such that
\mu_{\gamma}=\smallo\left(r\right)
and
r=\bigO\left(\mu_{\gamma}^{\delta_{0}}\right)
for some
\delta_{0}\in\left(0,1\right)
. Then for every
p\in[1,\infty)
, we have

\int_{B\left(0,r\right)}\exp\big(\overline{B}_{\gamma}^{2}\big)\left(1+t_{\gamma}\right)\left|\phi\right|^{p}dx=\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\gamma^{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left|\nabla\phi\right|^{2}dx\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}\right).

(7.7)

Proof.

With Proposition
6.1
we can rewrite condition (
7.6
) as

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\gamma\int_{B\left(0,r\right)}\left(1+\bigO\left(\frac{1+t_{\gamma}}{\gamma^{2}}\right)\right)e^{\gamma^{2}}e^{-2t_{\gamma}+\frac{t_{\gamma}^{2}}{\gamma^{2}}+\bigO\left(\frac{1+t_{\gamma}}{\gamma^{2}}\right)}\phi dx

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{\gamma}\int_{B\left(0,r/\mu_{\gamma}\right)}\left(1+\bigO\left(\frac{1+t}{\gamma^{2}}\right)\right)e^{-2t+\frac{t^{2}}{\gamma^{2}}+\bigO\left(\frac{1+t}{\gamma^{2}}\right)}\phi(\mu_{\gamma}\cdot)dx=\frac{4}{\gamma}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{-2t}\chi_{\gamma}\tilde{\phi}dy,

where
\widetilde{\Phi}\left(y\right)=\phi(\mu_{\gamma}y)
, and we claim that

\chi_{\gamma}:=\mathbf{1}_{B\left(0,r/\mu_{\gamma}\right)}\left(1+\bigO\left(\frac{1+t}{\gamma^{2}}\right)\right)e^{\frac{t^{2}}{\gamma^{2}}+\bigO\left(\frac{1+t}{\gamma^{2}}\right)}\longrightarrow\chi_{0}\equiv 1\\
\text{in }L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{2},e^{-2t}dx)\text{ for }1\leq q<\frac{1}{1-\delta_{0}}.

(7.8)

Indeed, it is clear that
\chi_{\gamma}\to\chi_{0}
pointwise, while we can uniformly bound
\chi_{\gamma}
by a function in
L^{q}\left(\mathbb{R}^{2},e^{-2t}dx\right)
as follows. By using (
6.19
), we obtain

\chi_{\gamma}=\bigO\Big(e^{\frac{t^{2}}{\gamma^{2}}}\Big)=\bigO\big(e^{t\left(1-\delta_{0}+\smallo\left(1\right)\right)}\big),\quad\text{so that }\chi_{\gamma}^{q}=\bigO\big(e^{tq\left(1-\delta_{0}+\smallo\left(1\right)\right)}\big).

On the other hand,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{tq\left(1-\delta_{0}+\smallo\left(1\right)\right)}e^{-2t}dx

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}e^{-t\left(2-q+q\delta_{0}+\smallo\left(1\right)\right)}dx=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\bigO\left(\frac{1}{1+\left|x\right|^{4-2q+2q\delta_{0}+\smallo\left(1\right)}}\right)dx

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(1\right)\quad\text{for }4-2q+2q\delta_{0}>2,\quad\text{i.e. }1\leq q<\frac{1}{1-\delta_{0}},

so that (
7.8
) follows by dominated convergence.

We can then apply Lemma
7.1
to
\widetilde{\Phi}
, so that (
7.4
) holds. On the other hand, for any
r\in[1,\infty)
,

\int_{B\left(0,r\right)}\exp\big(\overline{B}_{\gamma}^{2}\big)\left(1+t_{\gamma}\right)\left|\phi\right|^{p}dx\\
=\frac{1}{\gamma^{2}}\int_{B\left(0,r/\mu_{\gamma}\right)}e^{-2t+\frac{t^{2}}{\gamma^{2}}+\bigO\left(\frac{1+t}{\gamma^{2}}\right)}\left(1+t\right)|\tilde{\phi}|^{p}dx=\frac{1}{\gamma^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\tilde{\chi}_{\gamma}|\tilde{\phi}|^{p}e^{-2t}dx,

(7.9)

where, as in (
7.8
) we have

\tilde{\chi}_{\gamma}:=\mathbf{1}_{B\left(0,r/\mu_{\gamma}\right)}\left(1+t\right)e^{\frac{t^{2}}{\gamma^{2}}+\bigO\left(\frac{1+t}{\gamma^{2}}\right)}\longrightarrow 1+t\quad\text{in }L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{2},e^{-2t}dx)

for
q<1/\left(1-\delta_{0}\right)
, and with Hölder’s inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\tilde{\chi}_{\gamma}|\tilde{\phi}|^{p}e^{-2t}dx\leq\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\tilde{\chi}_{\gamma}^{q}e^{-2t}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\tilde{\phi}|^{pq^{\prime}}e^{-2t}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\nabla\tilde{\phi}|^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left|\nabla\phi\right|^{2}\right)^{\frac{p}{2}}\right).

Substituting into (
7.9
), we then obtain (
7.7
).
∎

8.
Proof of Claim
3.1

From (
3.2
), (
6.2
) and the divergence theorem, we get

A_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}=-2\pi r_{\varepsilon}\overline{B}^{\prime}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}\left(r_{\varepsilon}\right)=-2\pi r_{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\lambda_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{\tau}_{i}\right)}\overline{B}^{\prime}_{\gamma_{i}}\big(\sqrt{\lambda_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{\tau}_{i}\right)}r_{\varepsilon}\big)=\frac{4\pi}{\gamma_{i}}+\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{3}}\right).

(8.1)

Considering that

\ln\lambda_{\varepsilon}=\bigO\left(1\right)\quad\text{and}\quad\ln\mu_{\gamma_{i}}=-\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{i}^{2}-\ln\gamma_{i}+\bigO\left(1\right),

from (
6.2
), we infer

\displaystyle\overline{B}_{\gamma_{i}}\big(\sqrt{\lambda_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{\tau}_{i}\right)}r_{\varepsilon}\big)

\displaystyle=\gamma_{i}-\frac{2\ln(r_{\varepsilon}/\mu_{\gamma_{i}})+\ln(\lambda_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{\tau}_{i}\right))}{\gamma_{i}}+\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\gamma_{i}}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{2\ln r_{\varepsilon}}{\gamma_{i}}-\frac{2\ln\gamma_{i}}{\gamma_{i}}+\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}\right),

(8.2)

which together with (
3.3
) and (
8.1
) gives

C_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}=-\frac{2\ln\gamma_{i}}{\gamma_{i}}+\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}\right)=-\frac{2\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\gamma_{i}}+\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}\right).

This proves (
3.10
). Further, Proposition
6.2
gives

\displaystyle\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\left[A_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}\right]

\displaystyle=-2\pi r_{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\lambda_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{\tau}_{i}\right)}\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\big[\overline{B}^{\prime}_{\gamma_{i}}\big(\sqrt{\lambda_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{\tau}_{i}\right)}r_{\varepsilon}\big)\big]

\displaystyle=-\frac{4\pi}{\gamma_{i}^{2}}+\bigO\left(\frac{\mu_{\gamma_{i}}^{2}}{r_{\varepsilon}^{3}}\right)+\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{4}}\right)=-\frac{4\pi}{\gamma_{i}^{2}}+\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{4}}\right).

Similarly,

\partial_{\gamma_{i}}[\overline{B}_{\gamma_{i}}\big(\sqrt{\lambda_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{\tau}_{i}\right)}r_{\varepsilon}\big)]=\frac{2\ln r_{\varepsilon}}{\gamma_{i}^{2}}+\frac{2\ln\gamma_{i}}{\gamma_{i}^{2}}+\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right),

so that

\displaystyle\partial_{\gamma_{i}}\left[C_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}\right]

\displaystyle=\partial_{\gamma_{i}}[\overline{B}_{\gamma_{i}}\big(\sqrt{\lambda_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{\tau}_{i}\right)}r_{\varepsilon}\big)]+\frac{1}{2\pi}\partial_{\gamma_{i}}[A_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}]\ln r_{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\ln\gamma_{i}}{\gamma_{i}^{2}}+\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right)=\frac{2\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}}{\gamma_{i}^{2}}+\bigO\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}\right),

which proves (
3.11
). To prove (
3.12
), we observe that

\displaystyle A_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}=\lambda_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{\tau_{i}}\right)\int_{B\left(\overline{\tau_{i}},r_{\varepsilon}\right)}f\left(\overline{B}_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\overline{\tau_{i}}}\right)dx

\displaystyle=2\pi\lambda_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{\tau_{i}}\right)\int_{0}^{r_{\varepsilon}}f\big(\overline{B}_{\gamma_{i}}\big(\sqrt{\lambda_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{\tau_{i}}\right)}r\big)\big)rdr

\displaystyle=2\pi\int_{0}^{\sqrt{\lambda_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{\tau_{i}}\right)}r_{\varepsilon}}f\big(\overline{B}_{\gamma_{i}}\left(r\right)\big)rdr.

(8.3)

By differentiating (
8
) in
\tau_{i}
, we obtain

\partial_{\tau_{i}}\left[A_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}\right]=\pi\lambda_{\varepsilon}\partial_{x_{1}}h_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{\tau_{i}}\right)r_{\varepsilon}^{2}f\big(\overline{B}_{\gamma_{i}}\big(\sqrt{\lambda_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{\tau_{i}}\right)}r_{\varepsilon}\big)\big).

(8.4)

By using (
8
) together with the definition of
r_{\varepsilon}
, and using that
\gamma_{i}\geq\left(1-\delta^{\prime}\right)\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}
, we obtain

\displaystyle r_{\varepsilon}^{2}f\big(\overline{B}_{\gamma_{i}}\big(\sqrt{\lambda_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{\tau_{i}}\right)}r_{\varepsilon}\big)

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(r_{\varepsilon}^{2}\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\exp\left(\frac{4}{\gamma_{i}^{2}}\left(\ln r_{\varepsilon}+\ln\gamma_{i}+\bigO\left(1\right)\right)^{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\exp\left(2\ln r_{\varepsilon}\left(1+2\frac{\ln r_{\varepsilon}}{\gamma_{i}^{2}}+4\frac{\ln\gamma_{i}}{\gamma_{i}^{2}}\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\exp\left(-\delta_{0}\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}\left(1-\delta_{0}\frac{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}}{\gamma_{i}^{2}}+\smallo\left(1\right)\right)+\ln\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\bigO\left(\exp\left(-\delta_{0}\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{2}\left(1-\frac{\delta_{0}}{\left(1-\delta^{\prime}\right)^{2}}+\smallo\left(1\right)\right)\right)\right)=\smallo\left(\frac{1}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{a}}\right)

(8.5)

uniformly in
(\gamma,\tau)\in\Gamma_{\varepsilon}^{k}\left(\delta^{\prime}\right)\times T_{\varepsilon}^{k}\left(\delta\right)
for all
a\geq 0
, provided
\delta^{\prime}<1-\sqrt{\delta_{0}}
. By using (
8.4
) and (
8
) and since
\lambda_{\varepsilon}\to\lambda_{0}
and
h_{\varepsilon}\to h_{0}
in
C^{1}\left(\overline{\Omega}\right)
, we obtain the first part of (
3.12
). By differentiating
C_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}
in
\tau_{i}
and using (
6.2
), (
8.4
) and (
8
), we then obtain

\partial_{\tau_{i}}\left[C_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}\right]=\frac{\sqrt{\lambda_{\varepsilon}}\partial_{x_{1}}h_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{\tau}_{i}\right)r_{\varepsilon}}{2\sqrt{h_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{\tau}_{i}\right)}}\overline{B}^{\prime}_{\gamma_{i}}\big(\sqrt{\lambda_{\varepsilon}h_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{\tau}_{i}\right)}r_{\varepsilon}\big)\\
-\frac{1}{2\pi}\partial_{\tau_{i}}\left[A_{\varepsilon,\gamma_{i},\tau_{i}}\right]\ln\frac{1}{r_{\varepsilon}}=-\frac{\partial_{x_{1}}h_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{\tau_{i}}\right)}{h_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{\tau_{i}}\right)\gamma_{i}}+\bigO\left(\frac{\left|\partial_{x_{1}}h_{\varepsilon}\left(\overline{\tau_{i}}\right)\right|}{\overline{\gamma}_{\varepsilon}^{3}}\right),

which gives the second part of (
3.12
). This ends the proof of Claim
3.1
.
